# Repository Structure

three.ws ships all platform surfaces from a **single npm-workspaces monorepo**.
This file maps each product surface to where it lives in this repository, so
external developers can find what they need without reading 50 top-level
directories.

When an internal surface gains an external consumer that needs an independent
release cadence, we promote it to its own published package (and optionally its
own repo via `git subtree split`). See [Promotion path](#promotion-path) at the
bottom.

## three.ws surface map

| Surface | Location | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Web renderer / viewer | [avatar-sdk/](avatar-sdk) `→ /viewer` | Published as `@three-ws/avatar` | `<agent-3d>` web component |
| Entity studio (one detail page for agents AND avatars) | [pages/avatar-page.html](pages/avatar-page.html) + [src/avatar-page.js](src/avatar-page.js) `→ /avatars/:id` and `→ /agents/:id`, view switcher [src/view-switcher.js](src/view-switcher.js), AR lane [src/ar-page.js](src/ar-page.js) `→ /avatars/:id/ar` + `/agents/:id/ar`, long-form agent profile [pages/agent-detail.html](pages/agent-detail.html) + [src/agent-detail.js](src/agent-detail.js) `→ /agents/:id/profile` | Live | The canonical page every entity link on the platform lands on: search, `/agents`, `/gallery`, the marketplace, galaxy, launches and profiles all point here. One controller renders both kinds from one shell (3D stage + wallet-reactive aura, identity, tabs for Overview / Chat / Pose / Skills / Plugins / Embed, related grid). Agent mode projects the agent record onto the avatar shape (`agentStudioRecord`): the agent's name, description, tags and custodial wallet win, its bound body supplies geometry and fork lineage, and chat posts the real `agentId` so replies come from that agent's brain and memory. Agents and avatars are separate tables with separate UUIDs, so an id that lands on the wrong route is probed against the other store and redirected to its canonical path instead of 404ing. An agent with no body yet renders a designed stage state ("create a body" / "pick one from the marketplace") rather than an empty viewer, and every body-scoped surface (AR, Pose, forks, coin launch, GLB stats) stays hidden. Docs: [docs/agents-vs-avatars.md](docs/agents-vs-avatars.md) |
| Agent shell (companion identity + agentic ⌘K + persistent navigation) | [src/agents/guest-agent.js](src/agents/guest-agent.js), [src/shared/agent-names.js](src/shared/agent-names.js), [src/walk-companion-identity.js](src/walk-companion-identity.js), [public/search.js](public/search.js), [src/view-transitions.js](src/view-transitions.js), [src/shell/page-lifecycle.js](src/shell/page-lifecycle.js) | Live | The agent IS the interface: every visitor gets a named ephemeral agent in the first five seconds (auto-summoned corner companion with an identity chip; “Claim →” prefills /create-agent and POST /api/agents makes it real); the ⌘K palette executes real commands in place (forge → free text→3D lane, digest → clustered news, price → live market data, ask → streamed /api/chat) and announces completions so the companion reacts; pages marked data-shell (`/markets`, `/coins`) navigate by swapping only the main element so the agent and shell never unload (page modules re-init via onPageReady). Docs: [docs/agent-shell.md](docs/agent-shell.md). Tests: [tests/search-commands.test.js](tests/search-commands.test.js), [tests/guest-agent.test.js](tests/guest-agent.test.js), [tests/agent-names.test.js](tests/agent-names.test.js) |
| Walk companion + playground | [walk-sdk/](walk-sdk) `→ /walk-companion.js` | Published as `@three-ws/walk` | Corner mascot + full-page stroll/platformer playground + diverse avatar picker. App entries: [src/walk-companion.js](src/walk-companion.js), [src/walk-playground.js](src/walk-playground.js) |
| The walkaround world (`/temporary`) | [pages/temporary.html](pages/temporary.html) `→ /temporary`, app [src/walk.js](src/walk.js), environments [src/walk-environments.js](src/walk-environments.js) + [public/environments/](public/environments), multiplayer [src/walk-net.js](src/walk-net.js) → `walk_world`, day/night [src/walk-day-night.js](src/walk-day-night.js), radar [src/game/hud/minimap.js](src/game/hud/minimap.js), companions [src/walk-npcs.js](src/walk-npcs.js), agent desks [src/walk-agent-desk.js](src/walk-agent-desk.js) | Live | Walk your avatar through six real staged environments (park, cyberpunk street, beach, gallery, void, office) with physics-driven locomotion on a heightfield, four camera modes, gestures, voice chat, trails, capture, and live peers. **Time of day is real and shared:** the same deterministic world clock `/play` runs on ([src/shared/world-clock.js](src/shared/world-clock.js)) drives the sun arc, sky gradient, light levels, and IBL strength, so two people in the same world always agree on whether it is night. The sky crossfades on a wider curve than the light levels, so dawn and dusk linger instead of flipping. Armed for outdoor stages only (indoor/void keep their authored manifest lighting) and paused while AR passthrough owns the rig. **The radar is `/play`'s:** `/temporary` mounts the same rotating GTA-style minimap the world HUD uses, with a compass, the walkable-disc boundary ring, and live blips for peers (their nameplate colours), NPC companions, live agent desks, the guide's named landmarks, and scenery. Click or tap it to set a walk-to waypoint (`Minimap.worldFromScreen()` inverts the rotating projection); `M` or the dock button toggles it and the choice persists. **Frame pacing is shared too:** the loop runs through [src/shared/frame-governor.js](src/shared/frame-governor.js) (60fps focused, 30 unfocused, 30 under power saver) and the controls overlay carries the cross-surface Power-saver switch. Tests: [tests/walk-day-night.test.js](tests/walk-day-night.test.js), [tests/minimap-projection.test.js](tests/minimap-projection.test.js) |
| Walk landing + one-tag embed (`/walk`) | [pages/walk-landing.html](pages/walk-landing.html) `→ /walk`, embed app [pages/walk-embed.html](pages/walk-embed.html) `→ /walk/app` and `→ /walk-embed` ([src/walk-embed.js](src/walk-embed.js)), board [pages/walk-leaderboard.html](pages/walk-leaderboard.html) `→ /walk-leaderboard`, ranking API [api/walk/leaderboard.js](api/walk/leaderboard.js), curated quotes [data/walk-social.json](data/walk-social.json) | Live | The marketing entry for Walk and the surface the `→ /walk` route actually serves (the walkaround world above is at `/temporary`). Live demo embeds across six environments, the copyable one-tag iframe snippet, the Chrome extension pitch, and a top-three teaser off the real all-time distance ranking, which reads `rows` straight from the leaderboard endpoint and falls back to designed empty and retryable error states. All nine demo iframes stay keyboard-reachable, which axe's `frame-focusable-content` rule requires of any frame whose document has focusable content, and each gets a scripted focus ring because an iframe whose document holds focus matches no focus pseudo-class. Page copy is mirrored in the `walk_landing` namespace of [public/locales/en.json](public/locales/en.json), which the i18n runtime applies over the served HTML, so the two must be edited together. |
| Live Docs (runnable code samples) | Pure decisions [public/docs-live-core.js](public/docs-live-core.js), runner [public/docs-live.js](public/docs-live.js), styles [public/docs-live.css](public/docs-live.css), mounted by the docs viewer [docs/index.html](docs/index.html) | Live | Every code sample across the ~318 documentation pages is editable, and the runnable ones execute in place: an `html` snippet containing `<agent-3d>` renders a real animated avatar in a sandboxed frame directly under the code, a single-`curl` `bash` block sends a real request and reports status, latency, size, body and response headers, and a `js` snippet runs as a module with its console streamed back. Slots such as `$API_KEY` or `<agent-id>` become input fields, with credential-shaped names held in memory and never written to storage. The safety split is the design: everything deciding what a reader may fire lives in the pure core and is unit-tested rather than trusted to a click handler. Only three.ws hosts are reachable, any path that can move funds or mint is refused outright on every verb, writes arm before they send, every fetch goes out with `credentials: omit`, and frames run `sandbox="allow-scripts"` without `allow-same-origin`. Authors opt a block out with `<!-- live:off -->`. Loaded lazily after the first page render, so a page with no runnable samples costs nothing. Docs: [docs/live-docs.md](docs/live-docs.md) `→ /docs/live-docs`. Tests: [tests/docs-live-core.test.js](tests/docs-live-core.test.js) |
| Docs World (immersive 3D docs) | [pages/docs-world.html](pages/docs-world.html) `→ /docs/world`, app [src/docs-world/](src/docs-world) (world/player/controls/overlays), styles [public/docs-world.css](public/docs-world.css), shared manifest [docs/nav.json](docs/nav.json) | Live | The documentation as a walkable place: each section of the docs sidebar is a glowing pavilion on a ring; walk up (WASD / touch joystick, four shared camera modes via [src/game/camera-modes.js](src/game/camera-modes.js)) or tap one to list its pages, and read the live markdown in an in-world DOM reader ([src/shared/markdown.js](src/shared/markdown.js)). One content source: both surfaces read `docs/nav.json` + `/docs/<slug>.md`, so classic and immersive docs cannot drift. Deep links mirror the classic hash routes (`/docs/world#forge`); the classic header's "3D world" button carries the open page across. Avatar is the platform's default rigged body (the same one `/walk` uses, since the agent mannequin ships unskinned and cannot be clip-driven) or any rigged GLB via `?avatar=`, animated by the canonical clip library. No-WebGL devices get a designed fallback routing to `/docs`. Docs: [docs/docs-world.md](docs/docs-world.md). Tests: [tests/docs-world.test.js](tests/docs-world.test.js) |
| React avatar creator | [avatar-sdk/](avatar-sdk) `→ /react` `/creator` | Published as `@three-ws/avatar` | Same package, React subpath |
| Avatar CLI (manifests in the build pipeline) | [packages/avatar-cli/](packages/avatar-cli), landing [pages/avatar-cli.html](pages/avatar-cli.html) `→ /avatar-cli`, styles [public/avatar-cli.css](public/avatar-cli.css), page runtime [src/avatar-cli-page.js](src/avatar-cli-page.js) | Published as `@three-ws/avatar-cli` | The terminal half of the avatar story, and the half that makes an avatar auditable. Four commands over one file: `init` scaffolds a schema-valid manifest from a wallet plus a mesh (reading the GLB once to compute its SHA-256 and size, so the document cannot disagree with the bytes it was built from), `validate` checks it against [packages/avatar-schema/](packages/avatar-schema) and exits non-zero so it works as a build gate, `hash` prints a bare digest that composes in a shell, and `preview` emits a resolver URL, an `<agent-3d>` snippet paired with the loader that registers it, and a zero-install iframe. Offline and deterministic throughout: no account, no API key, no network call, which is what makes it safe in a release script. `preview` warns instead of printing a dead snippet when `mesh.uri` is still a local `file://` path. The landing page proves the emitted markup by rendering it live rather than screenshotting it. Docs: [docs/avatar-cli.md](docs/avatar-cli.md) `→ /docs/avatar-cli`, tutorial [docs/tutorials/ship-an-avatar-manifest.md](docs/tutorials/ship-an-avatar-manifest.md). Tests: [packages/avatar-cli/test/](packages/avatar-cli/test) |
| Asset catalog (one search over every ready-made asset) | join + ranking [api/_lib/asset-catalog.js](api/_lib/asset-catalog.js), code generation [api/_lib/asset-snippets.js](api/_lib/asset-snippets.js), REST [api/catalog.js](api/catalog.js) `→ /api/catalog`, MCP tools [api/_mcp/tools/library.js](api/_mcp/tools/library.js), CLI [packages/assets-cli/](packages/assets-cli) | Published as `@three-ws/assets` | The CC0 prop library, the ready-made character library and the motion-clip library joined into one ranked catalog (3,492 items) that also hands back paste-ready source: the `<model-viewer>` tag the browse grids render with, `<agent-3d>` pinned to the exact published version plus its SRI hash, plain three.js, or React. Reachable three ways off one implementation: `GET /api/catalog`, the free MCP tools `search_catalog` / `get_catalog_item` / `get_item_source` (on the dispatcher's public allowlist, so no OAuth and no x402), and `npx @three-ws/assets add <id>`, which downloads the asset into the caller's project and rewrites the snippet to the local path. Every word of a query must match, with a labelled partial-match fallback so a phrase never dead-ends. Docs: [docs/mcp.md](docs/mcp.md) `→ /docs/mcp`, [docs/api-reference.md](docs/api-reference.md). Tests: [tests/api/mcp-asset-catalog.test.js](tests/api/mcp-asset-catalog.test.js), [tests/api/catalog-endpoint.test.js](tests/api/catalog-endpoint.test.js), [packages/assets-cli/test/](packages/assets-cli/test) |
| Terminal avatar (any avatar or agent body, live in a terminal) | [packages/tty-avatar/](packages/tty-avatar) | Published as `@three-ws/tty-avatar` | `npx @three-ws/tty-avatar <avatar id>` draws the avatar in colour at 24 fps in any terminal: a software rasterizer ([packages/tty-avatar/src/raster.js](packages/tty-avatar/src/raster.js), z-buffered scanline fill, key + fill + rim lighting) over a subpixel grid that three encoders turn into truecolor half-blocks, braille, or plain ASCII ([packages/tty-avatar/src/encode.js](packages/tty-avatar/src/encode.js)). Sources: a `.glb` path, a URL, an avatar id (`/api/avatars/:id`), `agent:<id>` (the agent's bound body via `/api/agents/:id`), or a three.ws page URL. Moods are procedural whole-body motion ([packages/tty-avatar/src/moods.js](packages/tty-avatar/src/moods.js)), so a rigless prop animates too, and they cross-fade. The point of it: `install-hooks --write` merges nine Claude Code hooks whose command is a bare `cat > ~/.three-ws/tty-avatar/event.json` (zero startup cost per tool call), the viewer polls that file and maps each payload to a mood and a caption ([packages/tty-avatar/src/state.js](packages/tty-avatar/src/state.js)), so the avatar thinks, works, errors, asks and celebrates in step with the agent. Every avatar and agent page's Embed tab prints the command ([src/avatar-page.js](src/avatar-page.js)). Docs: [docs/tty-avatar.md](docs/tty-avatar.md) `→ /docs/tty-avatar`. Tests: [packages/tty-avatar/test/](packages/tty-avatar/test) (builds a real GLB in memory and exercises loader, rasterizer, encoders, moods, state bridge, hook installer and CLI) |
| Model Diff (what changed between two GLBs) | [pages/diff.html](pages/diff.html) `→ /diff` + page runtime [src/model-diff.js](src/model-diff.js), 3D stage [src/model-diff-stage.js](src/model-diff-stage.js), styles [public/model-diff.css](public/model-diff.css), engine [packages/glb-diff/](packages/glb-diff), free API [api/3d/diff.js](api/3d/diff.js) `→ /api/3d/diff` (catalog entry [api/_lib/3d-catalog/diff.js](api/_lib/3d-catalog/diff.js)), MCP tool `diff_models` in [api/_mcp/tools/models.js](api/_mcp/tools/models.js) | Live | The check that catches the most expensive silent failure in an asset pipeline: a checksum flags every re-export, and cannot tell a lossless recompression from a rig that quietly lost three finger joints. Objects are paired in three passes the way git pairs renamed files (name, then content hash, then similarity), so an exporter that renumbers `Mesh.001` reads as a rename rather than a rewrite, and a node that kept its name but changed parent reads as a move. Vertex data is hashed at a 1e-4 quantum with normals, UVs and **skin weights** included, so a Draco round trip registers as nothing and a re-skin registers as a change. Every difference carries a severity (`none` to `breaking`), where breaking means something a consumer addresses by name is gone: a clip, a joint, a mesh. One engine, four surfaces, so a CI verdict can never disagree with the page: the browser page (drop two files, nothing uploaded; overlay / wipe / side-by-side in 3D, clicking a row frames that node), the free keyless API (`?a=&b=`, `format=markdown` for a pull-request comment), the `glb-diff` CLI (`--fail-on breaking`, exit 2 reserved for "could not run"), and the MCP tool. Docs: [docs/model-diff.md](docs/model-diff.md) `→ /docs/model-diff`. Tests: [packages/glb-diff/tests/glb-diff.test.js](packages/glb-diff/tests/glb-diff.test.js), [tests/model-diff-api.test.js](tests/model-diff-api.test.js) |
| Rig Doctor (will this GLB animate?) | [pages/rig-doctor.html](pages/rig-doctor.html) `→ /rig-doctor`, analysis [src/rig-report.js](src/rig-report.js), page runtime [src/rig-doctor-page.js](src/rig-doctor-page.js), styles [public/rig-doctor.css](public/rig-doctor.css) | Live | The answer to the most expensive silent failure on the platform: a rig loads, renders, and never moves, because clips address bones by name and this skeleton calls them something else. Drop a `.glb` and the page reads its glTF JSON chunk in the browser, names the authoring convention (twelve fingerprints, from Mixamo and Ready Player Me to Unreal, VRM, MMD, Biped, Rigify, and HumanIK), and scores four limb groups independently against the 52-joint canonical set, because "62% covered" is not actionable and "your legs will not move" is. The verdict mirrors the runtime gate in [src/animation-manager.js](src/animation-manager.js) exactly (a skinned mesh plus 8 mapped joints), so it predicts rather than approximates. It then does two things a report cannot: mounts the real [src/avatar-pose.js](src/avatar-pose.js) stage on a blob URL of the file so the user watches their own rig perform the actual clip library, and runs [src/glb-canonicalize.js](src/glb-canonicalize.js) in-browser to hand back a repaired GLB with canonical joint names. Nothing is uploaded: the only network calls are for the clip library and the sample rigs. Also emits a schema-v1 manifest, an `<agent-3d>` snippet, and the matching [packages/avatar-cli/](packages/avatar-cli) commands. Docs: [docs/rig-doctor.md](docs/rig-doctor.md) `→ /docs/rig-doctor`. Tests: [tests/rig-report.test.js](tests/rig-report.test.js) |
| The pump.fun pill (`/pill`) | [pages/pill.html](pages/pill.html) `→ /pill` + [src/pill.js](src/pill.js) + [src/pill.css](src/pill.css), rigger [scripts/rig-pill-mascot.py](scripts/rig-pill-mascot.py), decimator [scripts/decimate-glb.mjs](scripts/decimate-glb.mjs), assets [public/avatars/pumpfun-pill-cupsey.glb](public/avatars/pumpfun-pill-cupsey.glb) (rigged) + `pumpfun-pill-cupsey-static.glb` (the rigger's input) | Live | pump.fun verified $THREE as officially ours, and this is the thank-you: their pill mascot rigged, animated, and drivable in the browser. WASD or a touch joystick walks it, Shift runs, Space jumps, drag orbits, and the toolbar fires wave/jump/dance. The stage plays the model's own six embedded clips, so it needs no retargeting at load and no clip fetch. The model could not go through [workers/rig](workers/rig): a capsule with four stubby nubs has no neck, no separate head and no long limbs for a humanoid predictor to find, so it got its own pipeline. That pipeline is general to capsule-with-nubs mascots, not to this character: limbs discovered from the mesh's distance transform, medial curves traced with a minimum-cost path, skin weights decided by walking each vertex inward to the medial axis of the part it is skin for (straight-line and geodesic distance both weld the raised hand to the cheek it rests against), and the neutral standing pose baked into the shipped geometry so bind and rest coincide. Canonical bone names mean the whole clip library retargets onto it at 100% coverage. Docs: [docs/pill-mascot.md](docs/pill-mascot.md) `→ /docs/pill-mascot`. Tests: [tests/pill-rig.test.js](tests/pill-rig.test.js) |
| Talking page guide | [page-agent-sdk/](page-agent-sdk) | Published as `@three-ws/page-agent` | Drop-in rigged 3D agent that narrates any page + visitor avatar picker. `<page-agent>` web component. Rigged-only catalog. `preset="…"` resolves one of 5 personas (greeting/role/suggested-prompt chips/tool allowlist) — see [page-agent-sdk/README.md#persona-presets](page-agent-sdk/README.md#persona-presets). See [page-agent-sdk/PUBLISHING.md](page-agent-sdk/PUBLISHING.md) |
| Guided site tour | [tour-sdk/](tour-sdk) | Published as `@three-ws/tour` | 3D guide walks a live site, spotlights sections, narrates each. One-tag CDN build + free roam |
| Concierge (embeddable AI support chat) | [concierge-sdk/](concierge-sdk), backend [api/concierge.js](api/concierge.js) `→ /api/concierge`, landing [pages/concierge.html](pages/concierge.html) `→ /concierge`, first-party CDN copy `→ /concierge/concierge.global.js` (mirrored by [concierge-sdk/build.mjs](concierge-sdk/build.mjs)) | Published as `@three-ws/concierge` | An AI concierge for ANY website: floating launcher, chat panel with a rigged 3D avatar bust (blink/idle/viseme lipsync via the page-agent stage lineage), SSE-streamed answers grounded in the live page (ask-time DOM harvest + host `knowledge`, no crawler), speaks while streaming (sentence-level TTS handoff), push-to-talk mic, themes/accent/position, avatar picker or custom GLB. Backend is anonymous + CORS-`*` (third-party origins by design) with its own rate buckets (`limits.conciergeIp`/`conciergeGlobal`), no platform-side content filter on the visitor question, and streaming failover over the shared free-first chain (api/_lib/llm.js). Shopping mode (Shopify): when embedded on a Shopify storefront it auto-detects the store (`window.Shopify` / `shop` config) and becomes a shopping assistant, reading the store's public `/products.json` + `/collections.json` + `/policies/*` at ask-time (no key, no crawler, same-origin), retrieving the relevant products per question via keyword+intent search ([concierge-sdk/src/shopify.js](concierge-sdk/src/shopify.js)), grounding the answer in them, and rendering real product cards (image/live price/link + on-store `/cart/add.js` add-to-cart). The endpoint accepts a bounded `shopping` payload and swaps the system prompt to a store assistant. `<three-concierge>` element, `data-concierge` one-tag auto-init, or `new Concierge({...})`. Also reachable from any AI agent via the MCP server [packages/concierge-mcp/](packages/concierge-mcp) (`@three-ws/concierge-mcp`, registry `io.github.nirholas/concierge-mcp`): `concierge_ask` (ask any site's concierge, grounded in the fetched page), `concierge_embed` (generate the embed), `concierge_avatars`. Examples: [concierge-sdk/examples/](concierge-sdk/examples) (one-tag, web component, imperative, custom avatar, React, self-hosted backend). Docs: [docs/concierge.md](docs/concierge.md), tutorial [docs/tutorials/build-a-site-concierge.md](docs/tutorials/build-a-site-concierge.md). Tests: [concierge-sdk/test/](concierge-sdk/test), [tests/api-concierge.test.js](tests/api-concierge.test.js), [packages/concierge-mcp/test/](packages/concierge-mcp/test) |
| Assistant widget (3D avatar assistant for any website) | Loader [public/assistant/v1.js](public/assistant/v1.js) `-> /assistant/v1.js`, iframe app [pages/assistant-frame.html](pages/assistant-frame.html) + [src/assistant-frame.js](src/assistant-frame.js) `-> /assistant-frame`, builder [pages/assistant.html](pages/assistant.html) + [src/assistant-page.js](src/assistant-page.js) `-> /assistant`, shared validators [src/assistant-widget-core.js](src/assistant-widget-core.js). npm SDK [assistant-sdk/](assistant-sdk) (`@three-ws/assistant`) is the single source for the loader; its [build.mjs](assistant-sdk/build.mjs) emits the npm ESM + the mirrored one-tag build at `public/assistant/v1.js`. MCP server [packages/assistant-mcp/](packages/assistant-mcp) (`@three-ws/assistant-mcp`, registry `io.github.nirholas/assistant-widget`): `build_assistant_widget` (config -> paste-ready snippet + frame URL + JS-API call), `list_assistant_options` (avatars, backgrounds, modes, attributes). | Published as `@three-ws/assistant` | One script tag adds a floating launcher to any site; clicking it pops up a full-body animated avatar against the host page (transparent) or a color/gradient. Two modes: chatbot (free chain via POST /api/chat, or the visitor's own Groq/OpenRouter key sent browser-to-provider, stored in the frame's localStorage only) and speak mode (avatar repeats typed text out loud via Web Speech + head-anchored speech bubble + talking clip). All params re-validated in-frame (CSS/URL injection safe). Sibling of Concierge (chat panel) - this one is the full-body, avatar-first widget. Install three ways: one-tag CDN, `npm i @three-ws/assistant`, or the MCP server for AI agents. Examples: [assistant-sdk/examples/](assistant-sdk/examples). Docs: [docs/assistant-widget.md](docs/assistant-widget.md) `-> /docs/assistant-widget`, tutorial [docs/tutorials/add-a-3d-assistant.md](docs/tutorials/add-a-3d-assistant.md). Tests: [tests/assistant-widget.test.js](tests/assistant-widget.test.js), [assistant-sdk/test/](assistant-sdk/test), [packages/assistant-mcp/test/](packages/assistant-mcp/test) |
| Chat (conversational surface) | [chat/](chat) `→ /chat` | Live | Svelte 4 + Vite client, fork of eplus, built by `build:chat` inside `build:gcp` into `public/chat` → `dist/chat`. Free Built-in lane via `/api/chat/proxy` plus BYOK providers; inline 3D forge tools + ModelViewer3D; shares the platform wallet via [src/shared/](src/shared). Sign-in is wallet (SIWE/SIWS) or email+password against the platform's own `/api/auth/*`, on the same cookie session as the rest of the site ([chat/src/passwordAuth.js](chat/src/passwordAuth.js)). The app is the chat and only the chat: marketing lives at the site root, and the hash routes that once rendered in-app copies of it resolve to the real page and redirect ([chat/src/three-ui/site-routes.js](chat/src/three-ui/site-routes.js)). Docs: [chat/README.md](chat/README.md). Tests: [tests/chat-app-core.test.js](tests/chat-app-core.test.js) |

The top is 17 copy-paste snippets that RUN on the page, grouped into four lanes (avatars and embeds, 3D models, agents, agent economy) behind a sticky jump nav and a text/tag filter that folds away lanes as they empty (a deep link always beats an active filter): each is a complete document loading the production CDN bundle into an iframe, so "Run" executes exactly the code shown, and the model/agent/economy lanes call the live CORS-open production APIs rather than illustrating them (free text→3D at `/api/v1/ai/text-to-3d`, free text→speech at `/api/v1/ai/tts` played through the new `speakAudio()` viseme path, `/api/3d/inspect`, the 112-clip `/animations/manifest.json`, `/api/forged`, the public no-auth MCP server at `/api/mcp-studio` for both `tools/list` and a live `look_at_model` render-and-critique, the 402 quote from `/api/x402/forge`, `/api/pump/launches`). The bottom is every example that ships in the repo, generated by [scripts/build-examples-index.mjs](scripts/build-examples-index.mjs) scanning every `examples/` directory into [data/examples.json](data/examples.json), mirrored to the served [public/examples.json](public/examples.json) by [scripts/mirror-examples-index.mjs](scripts/mirror-examples-index.mjs) (`npm run build:examples` runs all three). Because it is scanned, the page cannot advertise a deleted example or miss an added one; each card carries the files it contains and the command that runs it. Five recipes, each one executed against the live API before it was published: a standard-library text-to-3D CLI, a parallel asset-pack builder that emits a manifest plus a gallery, an MCP server that hands Claude Code or Cursor a 3D tool, a CI budget gate over [api/3d/inspect.js](api/3d/inspect.js), and the function-calling-plus-vision notebook also submitted to the OpenAI Cookbook. The manifest is the single source of truth for both surfaces; recipe prose is markdown under `docs/cookbook/` fetched by the viewer, so adding a recipe is a manifest entry plus a `.md` plus the file itself. Route order is load-bearing in both [vercel.json](vercel.json) and the dev middleware: the notebook's static export must be matched before the generic `/cookbook/<slug>` rule or the viewer shadows it. Index and player read the same generated manifest, so a walkthrough that did not capture cleanly cannot be advertised. Registered in [data/pages.json](data/pages.json). |
| Cross-entity search | [pages/search.html](pages/search.html) `→ /search` + page module [src/search-page.js](src/search-page.js), backend [api/search.js](api/search.js) + query layer [api/_lib/cross-search.js](api/_lib/cross-search.js) | Live | The one search across every creation type: avatars, on-chain/Solana agents, forged 3D models, worlds, and coins. Federates (not one SQL UNION, because the stores are genuinely heterogeneous and two of five are not even in the same database) five parallel queries, normalizes into one card shape, and ranks by recency plus real signals (follower count via `user_follows`, remix count, view count); see the file header in `api/search.js` for the full unify-vs-federate writeup. Model results carry a working "Remix, $0.25" action against the existing paid rail ([api/x402/remix-asset.js](api/x402/remix-asset.js)), reused from [pages/creations.html](pages/creations.html) rather than re-derived. Coin search checks three.ws's own launch directory (`pump_agent_mints`) first, then falls back to market-wide pump.fun/Birdeye search for broader coverage (those external matches carry no verified timestamp or creator profile, and are ranked/labelled accordingly). The page reads `?q=` and `?type=` from the URL before it paints the filter row, so a shared scoped link shows the filter it is actually applying; a type whose source returned nothing for the current query is dimmed from the real `counts` in the response. The backend answering `{enabled:false}` (creation stores unreachable) renders its own "search is warming up" state with a retry and live browse links, never a false "nothing matched". Enter runs the query without waiting out the debounce and Escape clears it. Linked from the Discover nav menu. |
| Cross-surface rankings, streaks & badges | [pages/rankings.html](pages/rankings.html) `→ /rankings`, backend [api/leaderboard/unified.js](api/leaderboard/unified.js), streak/badge engine [api/_lib/streaks.js](api/_lib/streaks.js) (migration [api/_lib/migrations/20260712020000_leaderboard_streaks_badges.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260712020000_leaderboard_streaks_badges.sql)), daily Top-10 badge sweep [api/cron/leaderboard-rollup.js](api/cron/leaderboard-rollup.js) | Live | Five real, queried leaderboard metrics — creations (forge models + worlds), remixes received, coin launches, followers, and walk distance (signed-in walkers, all-time) — ranked live with the viewer's own rank pinned even off-page. Every signed-in qualifying action (login, a finished forge model, a saved world, a `/walk` session) rolls a per-user daily streak in `user_streaks` via `recordDailyActivity()`, called from [api/_lib/auth.js](api/_lib/auth.js), [api/_lib/forge-store.js](api/_lib/forge-store.js), [api/_lib/diorama-store.js](api/_lib/diorama-store.js), and [api/walk/metrics.js](api/walk/metrics.js). Four real earned badges persist in `user_badges`: First Creation, Remixed (awarded in [api/x402/remix-asset.js](api/x402/remix-asset.js) when a remix lands on your creation), 7-Day Streak, and a daily-refreshed `top10_<metric>` per leaderboard. Streak + badges surface on the profile ([pages/profile.html](pages/profile.html)) and the Rankings page itself. Rejected metrics (documented in `api/leaderboard/unified.js`'s header): royalties earned in USD (the settlement record is a single overwritten JSONB column, not a cumulative ledger) and `/play` activity (no per-human-user session table exists). |
| create-agent (one command, one 3D character) | [packages/create-agent/](packages/create-agent) (client [src/forge.js](packages/create-agent/src/forge.js), scaffold [src/scaffold.js](packages/create-agent/src/scaffold.js), CLI [bin/create-agent.js](packages/create-agent/bin/create-agent.js)), server tools [api/_mcp-studio/tools.js](api/_mcp-studio/tools.js) | Live | `npm create @three-ws/agent "a friendly cartoon astronaut"` generates a textured figure, rigs it, downloads the GLB, and writes a folder with a working demo page and the embed snippet. No account and no API key: it rides the public, keyless JSON-RPC tools on [/api/mcp-studio](api/_mcp-studio) (`forge_avatar`, `forge_free`, `rig_mesh`) that the platform already exposes to MCP clients. A rigged character reliably outlives the endpoint's inline wait, so the client collects the returned poll handle and, when the job that finished was only the mesh, rigs it before reporting done: that is what the `stage` field on a pending result exists for. Progress is real elapsed time, never an invented bar. Docs [docs/create-agent.md](docs/create-agent.md). Tests: [tests/create-agent.test.js](tests/create-agent.test.js) |
| Glance (agent cards for home screens, READMEs and chat) | Page [public/glance/index.html](public/glance/index.html) `-> /glance` (+ [glance-page.js](public/glance/glance-page.js), [glance.css](public/glance/glance.css)), hosted element [public/glance/element.js](public/glance/element.js), endpoints [api/glance/card.js](api/glance/card.js) / [api/glance/mine.js](api/glance/mine.js) / [api/glance/token.js](api/glance/token.js) / [api/glance/template.js](api/glance/template.js), model [api/_lib/glance-card.js](api/_lib/glance-card.js), SVG [api/_lib/glance-svg.js](api/_lib/glance-svg.js), PNG [api/_lib/glance-png.js](api/_lib/glance-png.js), widget tokens [api/_lib/glance-tokens.js](api/_lib/glance-tokens.js) (`glance_widget_tokens`), Adaptive Card [api/_lib/glance-adaptive.js](api/_lib/glance-adaptive.js), Windows widget worker [public/glance-sw.js](public/glance-sw.js) (imported by the VitePWA service worker, manifest `widgets` member in [vite.config.js](vite.config.js)), Android home screen widget [solana-mobile/android-overlay/](solana-mobile/android-overlay/README.md) (applied by [solana-mobile/scripts/apply-overlay.mjs](solana-mobile/scripts/apply-overlay.mjs) inside `build-apk.sh`), macOS and iOS WidgetKit widget [apple/](apple/README.md) (shared [apple/GlanceKit/](apple/GlanceKit), extension [apple/GlanceWidget/](apple/GlanceWidget), Mac app [apple/macos/](apple/macos), an iOS target in [ios/native/App/App.xcodeproj](ios/native/App)), npm client [packages/agent-glance/](packages/agent-glance) | Live | One agent reduced to what fits in an OS widget slot: name, one live number (actions in the last 24h), and a link back. The same model renders five ways from one endpoint (JSON, SVG, PNG, Adaptive Card, and the `<agent-glance>` element), which is what keeps a new surface a rendering job rather than a data job. Windows 11 installs the widget straight from the PWA manifest (no store submission). Android ships it in the app (1.1+): a WorkManager-refreshed `AppWidgetProvider` that fetches the PNG with a revocable per-account widget token minted on `/glance`, keeps the last card offline, and opens the agent on tap. macOS and iOS run one WidgetKit extension over the same PNG and the same token, in small, medium and large: the Mac ships as a Developer ID menu bar app that holds the token and feeds the widget, and the iPhone gets the extension inside the existing Capacitor app. Both fetch the card in each theme so the widget follows the reader's appearance. `npm run check:apple-widget` proves both Xcode projects and the client-to-server contract without a Mac in the room. Deliberately not the 3D embed: no widget host on any of these platforms can run WebGL, so the body stays at [/widgets](public/widgets-gallery/index.html) and the status lives here. Private avatars never leak (the thumbnail is attached only for a public or unlisted avatar; everything else draws a generated monogram). Spec [specs/GLANCE_CARD.md](specs/GLANCE_CARD.md), docs [docs/glance.md](docs/glance.md), [docs/native-widgets.md](docs/native-widgets.md). Tests: [tests/glance-card.test.js](tests/glance-card.test.js), [tests/glance-png.test.js](tests/glance-png.test.js), [tests/glance-tokens.test.js](tests/glance-tokens.test.js), [tests/glance-sw.test.js](tests/glance-sw.test.js), [tests/agent-glance-sdk.test.js](tests/agent-glance-sdk.test.js), guard [scripts/check-apple-widget.mjs](scripts/check-apple-widget.mjs) |
| Daily Match (agent output arena) | [pages/daily-match.html](pages/daily-match.html) `→ /daily-match`, backend [api/leaderboard/daily-match.js](api/leaderboard/daily-match.js) | Live | Live daily standings ranking public agents by real output shipped since 00:00 UTC: agent_actions events, sniper + pump trades, confirmed skill sales, and coin launches, with realized sniper P&L shown for context (never scored). Computed live per request (no rollup cron), resets naturally at UTC midnight; includes yesterday's champion and a live-output feed. Reputation-first: no payouts hang off the score. Format adopted with credit from Bowyer's Arena (bowyer.app/arena), who build their agent pages on three.ws avatars. |

An announcement is always delivered to the caller's own sessions (no recipient field, so no key can interrupt anyone else); the queue is Redis-backed, drained not broadcast, and expires in five minutes. Speed comes from `navigator.geolocation`'s real ground speed, so moving above walking pace locks text entry back and a sustained stop is what unlocks it. Control phrases ("repeat that", "stop talking", "louder", "night mode") are answered locally with no round trip, matched against the whole utterance so "stop at the next charger" still reaches the agent. Hands free ends a turn from the real captured mic level on the Riva lane. **The car screen is never ours to draw on, on either platform:** Apple's voice-based conversational category (iOS 26.4) grants templates and an audio session and no drawing surface, and Android grants a canvas only to navigation apps and has no conversational category at all, so both car screens render the same four rows and this page runs beside them, joined by the versioned `threeWsDrive` channel (a WebKit message handler on iOS, a JavaScript interface on Android). Android Auto declares `androidx.car.app.category.IOT` because that is accurate: the home tools in `api/chat.js` reach a real house, so "turn the porch light on" from the car is the product. Its web view has no window and therefore no animation frames, so `?surface=androidauto` runs the loop as audio with the stage off, and refuses tap-to-approve for guarded actions because a person who cannot see what they are approving must not be asked. A guarded action while moving is refused on every surface and offered for when the car stops. Wake-word matching runs entirely in the tab on committed openWakeWord v0.5.1 weights served from three.ws, so nothing is uploaded until after the wake word; nothing about detection is even downloaded until the user opts in. Barge-in cuts playback ~110 ms after the user starts talking, and a playback-aware guard means the agent saying its own wake word (score 0.99) wakes it zero times. A pause is not an ending: a closed segment stays open 450 ms so "Hey Jarvis… turn the kitchen light off" is one command. Guarded actions are spoken in full, shown on screen by name and entity id, and satisfied only by the token `confirm` redeemed at `POST /api/home/:id/confirm`; a general "yeah" is refused by name and a screenless surface is refused outright. Twelve designed states, mute that ends the track rather than hiding a dot, and every leg timed live. Docs: [docs/home-voice.md](docs/home-voice.md). | Knock: a priced door to a person (`/knock`) | Hub [pages/knock.html](pages/knock.html) `→ /knock` + [src/knock/page.js](src/knock/page.js), public door [pages/knock-door.html](pages/knock-door.html) `→ /knock/<handle>` + [src/knock/door.js](src/knock/door.js), API [api/knock/](api/knock) (door, directory, send, reply, settings, inbox, escrowed, escrow-sync), paid lane [api/x402/knock.js](api/x402/knock.js), escrowed lane [api/_lib/knock/escrow.js](api/_lib/knock/escrow.js) + browser client [src/knock/escrow-program.js](src/knock/escrow-program.js) / [src/knock/escrow-checkout.js](src/knock/escrow-checkout.js), engine [api/_lib/knock/](api/_lib/knock) (pure policy, store, delivery, receipt tokens), migration [api/_lib/migrations/20260828210000_knock_doors.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260828210000_knock_doors.sql), SDK + CLI [packages/knock-sdk/](packages/knock-sdk) (`@three-ws/knock`), MCP [packages/knock-mcp/](packages/knock-mcp) (`@three-ws/knock-mcp`) | Live | Every account can publish a door at `/knock/<username>` and set what one message from a stranger costs. Paying it buys exactly one message through, and the delivery is **in person**: the knock becomes a `companion_events` row whose importance is derived from the amount paid, so the recipient's corner avatar walks on wherever they are and says who is at the door and what they paid. **The USDC settles straight to the recipient** (`payTo` on the 402 challenge is their own address, and a priced door cannot be opened without one), so there is no platform wallet in the path and no cut. Solana leads; Base is advertised only when the owner sets an address for it. **Every refusal happens before the 402** (shut door, daily cap, block list, length limits) and again before settlement, so a knock that was never going to land is never a knock somebody paid for. Only the subject line is spoken; an untrusted body never uses the recipient's avatar as a speaker. The sender gets a derived HMAC receipt URL and reads the reply through it with no account, which is why answering someone never hands out an address. Free doors take a plain POST on a tight per-IP bucket. A door can also take **escrowed** knocks (row below), which the door page offers beside paying up front and the owner runs from their own wallet in the settings panel; nothing on this server can move an escrowed payment. Spec: [specs/KNOCK_PROTOCOL.md](specs/KNOCK_PROTOCOL.md). Docs: [docs/knock.md](docs/knock.md). Tests: [tests/knock-policy.test.js](tests/knock-policy.test.js), [packages/knock-sdk/test/](packages/knock-sdk/test), [packages/knock-mcp/test/](packages/knock-mcp/test) |

The companion offers proactively at the one moment worth asking (right after the page actually threw), once per route per session, never in a background tab. **Feedback is data, never an instruction:** the model that reads untrusted visitor text writes six scalars to that report's own row and has no path to the repo, a deploy, or any other write; the admin-only `/feedback` queue groups reports into one row per problem and a person decides. **A report arrives as a runnable failing test:** the recorded session compiles into a Playwright spec asserting the failure is GONE (red until fixed, green after), reachable from the queue's Copy test button, `GET /api/feedback/repro`, `npm run feedback:repro`, and the `get_feedback_repro` MCP tool. Docs: [docs/feedback.md](docs/feedback.md), [docs/witness.md](docs/witness.md). Tests: [tests/feedback-triage.test.js](tests/feedback-triage.test.js) |
| Session-to-test recorder | [packages/witness/](packages/witness) `→ @three-ws/witness`: recorder [recorder.js](packages/witness/src/recorder.js), selector synthesis [selector.js](packages/witness/src/selector.js), capture-time privacy [redact.js](packages/witness/src/redact.js), spec compiler [compile.js](packages/witness/src/compile.js); compiled repros land in [tests/repros/](tests/repros) with [their own Playwright config](tests/repros/playwright.config.mjs) | Published as `@three-ws/witness` (Apache-2.0, zero deps) | Turns a real user session into a runnable failing test. Records INTENT (about 2 KB) rather than DOM mutations (megabytes), so it can be always-on, which is the point: the trace already exists when somebody decides to complain. Every element gets the strongest handle that is unique in the live document (test id > id > aria-label > role+name > text > name > placeholder > class > structural path), framework-generated ids and utility classes refused, and each trace carries a replay confidence equal to its WEAKEST link. The compiled spec asserts the failure is gone, never that it happened, so it locks in the fix rather than the bug; a session with no machine-visible failure emits `test.fail()` and says which assertion a human must add rather than faking green. Privacy is enforced at capture: typed values are never held (only a length and a shape), password/hidden/email/tel/date inputs and `data-witness="off"` subtrees record nothing, URLs lose their credentials before storage. Docs: [docs/witness.md](docs/witness.md). Tests: [packages/witness/test/](packages/witness/test) (36, `npm run test:witness`) |

The price is derived from the agent's own ledger rather than a rule of thumb: the public `?action=pricing` endpoint finds every buyer who already bought 2+ of the selected skills, suggests the median of what they really paid (`basis: median_basket`), and backtests any candidate price against those same baskets; with no such buyer yet it falls back to 20% off the sum of the parts and labels itself `discount_off_list` so a default is never read as a finding. Every amount is an atomic integer carried as a BigInt end to end, because a 9-decimal mint puts real sums past `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`. Checkout settles on-chain and writes one `skill_purchases` row per skill (`kind = 'bundle'`, amount 0) so one bundle sale is never counted as N skill sales. Free trials now render as their own stat and as a four-stage funnel (granted → used → exhausted → converted), which also names the two shapes that mean a wiring fault rather than weak demand: trials never spent, or trials exhausted with nothing converting. | OKX.AI marketplace chat bot host | [workers/okx-chat-bot/](workers/okx-chat-bot) (Cloud Run service `okx-chat-bot`), local revive [scripts/okx-bot-revive.mjs](scripts/okx-bot-revive.mjs) `→ npm run okx:bot`, session seeding [scripts/okx-bot-seed-state.mjs](scripts/okx-bot-seed-state.mjs) `→ npm run okx:bot:seed-state` | Built · deploy owner-gated | Always-on host for the XMTP chat behind agent #2632. Supervises the `okx-a2a` daemon plus the `onchainos` wallet session, persists the wallet/XMTP identity to GCS across revisions, and rebuilds the AI subsession's briefing from [api/_lib/okx-chat-briefing.js](api/_lib/okx-chat-briefing.js) on every boot so quoted prices can never drift from the catalog. It exists because the stack used to live on a codespace that sleeps, and its outage mode is silent: an expired wallet session leaves the process healthy while no chat is delivered, which flagged the listing offline once. Health reaches the platform as the `okx_chat_bot` subsystem on `/api/healthz` via a `bot_heartbeat` row; a logged-out session pages with the exact login commands. The AI credential is probed against the provider rather than assumed present ([workers/okx-chat-bot/provider.js](workers/okx-chat-bot/provider.js)), because a key the provider refuses is indistinguishable from a working one until a buyer's message goes unanswered. Vertex AI is the deploy's transport, so the runtime service account is the credential and there is no secret to mint. Deploy: [workers/okx-chat-bot/cloudbuild.yaml](workers/okx-chat-bot/cloudbuild.yaml) (`--max-instances=1` is load-bearing, one GCS state writer). Tests: [tests/okx-chat-bot.test.js](tests/okx-chat-bot.test.js) |

| 3D Vision (let an agent see a model) | [api/_lib/3d-vision.js](api/_lib/3d-vision.js), route [api/3d/look.js](api/3d/look.js) `→ /api/3d/look`, MCP tool `look_at_model` in [api/_mcp-studio/tools.js](api/_mcp-studio/tools.js) and on every OKX forge endpoint ([api/_okx3d/forge.js](api/_okx3d/forge.js)), SDK [packages/see/](packages/see) (`@three-ws/see`) | Live | Every text-to-3D API answers with a URL to a binary an AI agent cannot open, so the agent that generated a model has no way to check its own work; that blindness is why agentic 3D stalls at one shot. This renders any public GLB from up to six angles (three-quarter, front, side, back, top, bottom; the default four are the smallest set that answers "is this good") and hands the frames back as **MCP image content blocks**, which a multimodal client renders into the conversation, so the model literally looks at its own output and can regenerate naming the fault it saw. Reuses the platform's headless renderer ([api/_lib/avatar-render.js](api/_lib/avatar-render.js)) with the `full-body` preset so props and creatures are not cropped like humanoids, renders frames sequentially because the browser is shared, and reports a partial turntable rather than failing. Geometry comes from the existing free inspector and is best-effort: a hiccup costs the numbers, never the frames. Free and keyless on REST and MCP, and free for OKX buyers on the paid forge endpoints, so an agent can confirm it got what it paid for. Docs: [docs/3d-vision.md](docs/3d-vision.md). | x402 Preflight (signed payability attestations) | Format core [api/_lib/x402/preflight.js](api/_lib/x402/preflight.js), server [api/x402/preflight.js](api/x402/preflight.js) `→ /.well-known/x402-preflight`, page [pages/preflight.html](pages/preflight.html) `→ /preflight` + [src/preflight-page.js](src/preflight-page.js), client SDK + CLI [packages/x402-preflight](packages/x402-preflight), MCP tool [api/_mcp3d/tools/preflight.js](api/_mcp3d/tools/preflight.js), spec [specs/x402-preflight.md](specs/x402-preflight.md), docs [docs/x402-preflight.md](docs/x402-preflight.md) | Live | The reliability layer x402 is missing. x402 says what a resource costs; nothing says whether the seller can still SETTLE it, so a buyer discovers an empty seller fee wallet only after signing. Born from the 2026-08-28 incident where three.ws was that seller: sponsor at 0.000899107 SOL against a 0.02 floor, 95 payment attempts, 0 settled, 3 hours. A seller publishes a signed, time-bounded ed25519 attestation of per-network payability, built from the same `gatherX402SettleHealth()` sensor healthz uses plus the live sponsor floor guard, so every field is measured rather than declared. `payable` is three-valued (a seller that cannot measure must say `unknown`, never `true`), every rate ships with its window and sample, `alternates` names the rails that DO work so a client re-routes in the same round trip, and `retry_after` stops a fleet synchronising into a retry storm. Verification enforces expiry, so a healthy attestation cannot be replayed through an outage. The open format is unencumbered: any x402 seller can serve one, any client can verify one, nothing routes through three.ws. Tests: [tests/x402-preflight.test.js](tests/x402-preflight.test.js) (38, including a cross-implementation digest check that the browser SDK and the node server sign byte-identical bytes). |

A settled tip is what puts a dancer on the pole, so the payment IS the interaction. | Living Stages (an AI host performs live, tipped in $THREE) | Page [pages/stage.html](pages/stage.html) `→ /stage` (the marquee) and `→ /stage?id=<stage>` (the venue), runtime [src/stage.js](src/stage.js), realtime client [src/stage-net.js](src/stage-net.js) → `stage_world` in [multiplayer/src/rooms/StageRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/StageRoom.js) with the show logic in [multiplayer/src/stage-show.js](multiplayer/src/stage-show.js); API [api/stage/index.js](api/stage/index.js) `→ /api/stage`, tips [api/stage/tip.js](api/stage/tip.js) `→ /api/stage/tip`, host brain [api/stage/host.js](api/stage/host.js) `→ /api/stage/host` (HMAC, multiplayer server only) | Live | An embodied agent hosts a show in a 3D venue. Each beat is a timed `utterance` broadcast every client renders identically: TTS through a `PositionalAudio` whose analyser drives lip-sync, plus live captions, so a late joiner or a no-WebGL browser still reads the show. The crowd is co-present on a server-assigned ring, questions queue for the host, and a $THREE tip that settles on-chain to the host wallet is verified by the API and pushed into the room so the host reacts within a beat. The page resolves the realtime server through the same chain as `/play` ([src/shared/game-server-url.js](src/shared/game-server-url.js)) from the `game-server` meta it carries; localhost talks to `:2567`. The client subscribes to the host frame with `listen` (the join resolves before the first state patch, so `onChange` on the not-yet-decoded child throws) and registers message handlers before any state work, because the room opens the show the instant the first person arrives. With no feed the pill reads offline and reconnects on tap. Docs: [docs/stage.md](docs/stage.md). Tests: [tests/multiplayer-server-boot.test.js](tests/multiplayer-server-boot.test.js) (a real `stage_world` join wired the way the page does it), [tests/stage-show.test.js](tests/stage-show.test.js) |

Rarest premium fits ranked by how few wallets own them (owner count first, rarity as the tie-break, so a legendary never sorts under an equally-scarce rare), top collectors by rarity-weighted flex score, top creators by **actually settled** USDC, and a live sales drip; each fit deep-links to its coin world at `/play?coin=<mint>`. Plus a creator-earnings lookup any wallet can run without an account (lifetime + 30-day, paid vs pending, per-cosmetic and per-coin-world breakdowns), reachable in one click from each creator row rather than by copy-pasting an address. Coin mints are runtime ledger values, never hardcoded. Headline numbers are derived from the same rows rendered beneath them, so they cannot disagree; the volume tile is labelled as the returned sales window, not as an all-time total. Sub-cent sales keep their precision instead of collapsing to $0.00. Tests: [tests/fits-lib.test.js](tests/fits-lib.test.js) |
| Receipt Vault: buyer-side proof of purchase | [pages/receipts.html](pages/receipts.html) `→ /receipts`, page module [src/receipts.js](src/receipts.js) + pure helpers [src/receipts-lib.js](src/receipts-lib.js), backend [api/x402/my-receipts.js](api/x402/my-receipts.js), storage [api/_lib/x402/receipt-storage.js](api/_lib/x402/receipt-storage.js), issuer [api/_lib/x402/offer-receipt-issuer.js](api/_lib/x402/offer-receipt-issuer.js), key publication [api/x402/did.js](api/x402/did.js) `→ /.well-known/did.json` | Live | What a given wallet **spent** on the platform's paid endpoints. Every settled x402 call issues a signed Offer & Receipt artifact (USE-17) and durably logs it; a buyer connects a Solana or EVM wallet, signs one read-only message (5-minute window, no transaction, no fee) and gets every receipt ever issued to that wallet: endpoint, settled amount, network, explorer-linked tx, the full signed artifact as JSON, plus search and CSV export. The signing message is a fixed four-line string that must stay byte-identical between `buildReceiptsMessage()` (client) and `buildExpectedMessage()` (server); [tests/receipts-lib.test.js](tests/receipts-lib.test.js) pins that contract against a verbatim copy of the server's function. The signed artifact carries no amount and omits the tx hash under the spec §5.2 privacy default, so the row records both separately as our own audit trail (migration [api/_lib/migrations/2026-07-28-x402-receipt-settlement.sql](api/_lib/migrations/2026-07-28-x402-receipt-settlement.sql)); receipts predating that capture report as "N of M priced" rather than under-reporting a total. Issuance requires `OFFER_RECEIPT_JWK` (jws) or `OFFER_RECEIPT_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` (eip712); unset means no receipts are issued at all. Docs: [docs/x402-endpoints.md](docs/x402-endpoints.md) |

A pure-CPU software rasterizer (z-buffer, per-vertex linear blend skinning off the glTF animation samplers, meshopt decode on the way in) draws into a framebuffer the size of the caller's terminal and encodes it as `▀` half-blocks, so one cell carries two independently coloured pixels and pixels come out square. The server streams paced frames as `text/plain`, so the HTTP response IS the animation. Sibling of [packages/tty-avatar/](packages/tty-avatar), which adds moods, avatar-id resolution and Claude Code hooks on top of a terminal renderer but deliberately does not do skeletal animation; this package owns the 3D. |

Every AI restyle / saved checkpoint mints a real, `gltf-validator`-checked, durably-stored GLB recorded in an immutable parent→child lineage — non-destructive in the durable sense, not just an in-browser Reset. Emotion/rig/lip-sync cores under [src/embodiment/](src/embodiment) + [src/voice/](src/voice). | Live | A generated avatar becomes a persistent, named body that renders inline in ChatGPT/Claude: it lip-syncs each reply, blends the matching expression + gesture, idles between turns, and reloads by `persona_id` in a fresh session (the id is the capability, no sign-in). Rides the universal canonicalize/retarget clip library; a rig without face morphs animates the jaw, a non-humanoid GLB falls back to a gentle idle (`decideRigMode`/`supportsCanonicalClips`), never a bind-pose T-pose. Zero crypto/token surface. | Claude.ai artifact embed + builder | Endpoint [api/artifact.js](api/artifact.js) `→ /api/artifact`, prebuilt viewer [public/artifact-viewer.bundle.js](public/artifact-viewer.bundle.js) (source [scripts/artifact-viewer/src.js](scripts/artifact-viewer/src.js), `npm run build:artifact-viewer`), builder page [public/artifact/index.html](public/artifact/index.html) `→ /artifact` + [README](public/artifact/README.md), contract [specs/CLAUDE_ARTIFACT.md](specs/CLAUDE_ARTIFACT.md), vendored sandbox CSP [public/claude-artifact-csp.txt](public/claude-artifact-csp.txt) (refresh: [scripts/refresh-claude-csp.mjs](scripts/refresh-claude-csp.mjs)). | Live | `GET /api/artifact?agent=<uuid>` returns ONE self-contained HTML document with three.js, GLTFLoader, the viewer and the agent GLB all inlined, because Claude's artifact sandbox CSP blocks every external script and fetch. Paste the URL into a Claude conversation and the agent renders inline. The builder page is the pre-flight: it lists real public agents that carry an embeddable avatar (`/api/agents/public?avatar=1`), renders the response in a sandboxed iframe under the same CSP Claude applies, and reports payload size, fetch time, frame render time and sandbox compliance before you paste. Size is graded against the ceiling the endpoint can actually emit (a 6 MB model base64-inlines to 8 MB, plus the 565 KB viewer), not against the raw model cap, so a large-but-accepted artifact is not flagged as over budget. `?model=<glb url>` is the viewer-only lane (allowlisted https origins, SSRF-guarded, 6 MB cap). Tests: [tests/api/artifact.test.js](tests/api/artifact.test.js) pins the generated HTML against the vendored Claude CSP. |

Ships no third-party CDN (three.js is bundled, because an embed host never agreed to a cross-origin script) and suspends its render loop on a hidden tab. An avatar with no baked clips is driven by the shared retargeted `idle` from the universal clip library rather than left in a bind-pose T-pose. Every failure lane (no such agent, private avatar, no avatar yet, blocked host, dead model, no WebGL) has its own sentence and its own next move; the Copy embed snippet carries the artifact parameters so the iframe shows what you were looking at. |

| AR-ready exports ("View in your space") | Device-routing core [api/_lib/ar-launch.js](api/_lib/ar-launch.js), UA-branched launch endpoint [api/ar.js](api/ar.js) `→ /api/ar`, the actual AR page it hands off to [pages/ar-view.html](pages/ar-view.html) + [src/ar-view.js](src/ar-view.js) `→ /ar/view`, MCP tool [api/_mcp3d/tools/ar.js](api/_mcp3d/tools/ar.js) (`export_ar`, free), in-viewer AR button [public/viewer.html](public/viewer.html), docs [docs/ar.md](docs/ar.md). | Live · both stores | `GET /api/ar?src=<glb>` branches on the request User-Agent server-side: Android gets a clean 302 into the Google Scene Viewer ARCore intent (browser fallback to the WebGL viewer); iOS, desktop, and any live avatar get a 200 interstitial carrying this model's real `og:image`/title (so a pasted link still unfurls, which a bare 302 cannot do) that immediately hands off to `/ar/view`. `/ar/view` is a real Vite-bundled page, not HTML this endpoint writes inline, because Quick Look needs a genuine USDZ and `<model-viewer>` does not generate one on its own: the page fetches the GLB and runs it through the same client-side `usdz-pipeline.js` (three.js `USDZExporter`) that `/avatars/:id/ar` ([src/ar-page.js](src/ar-page.js)) already used correctly, sets the result as `ios-src`, then offers Quick Look. An earlier version of this endpoint inlined a `<model-viewer>` with no `ios-src` and a comment claiming model-viewer converted GLB to USDZ on its own; it does not, so iOS visitors silently fell back to the plain 3D viewer for months. Bad input rejected at the boundary with a designed error page. `export_ar` returns the AR launch link + a conformant Spatial-MCP artifact; response is OpenAI-clean (no ids/coin surface). `kind=avatar` is the living-agent lane: the interstitial's redirect target leads with a "Bring it to life" handoff into `/irl?avatar=<glb>` (avatar-producing tools return `irlUrl` automatically), and Android goes to `/ar/view` too instead of the blind Scene Viewer redirect so the living path stays visible. Deep link `/ar?src=<glb>&title=` reopens any shared model; recent forges persist in localStorage. Designed idle/working/failed/result states; https-only GLB guard. |

Every UTC day resolves to one curated creative theme (the same for everyone, hashed from the date — no backend, no randomness) with a running "Day N" counter; forging today's theme on the free lane lights a localStorage streak (consecutive-day state machine: extend / reset-on-gap / never-double-count, `best` preserved, at-risk vs lapsed display), and milestones (3/7/14/30/50/100/365) fire a celebration. The generation lands in the shared recents so it's one tap from `/ar` and `/ar/studio`; a "Fresh from the community" strip reads `/api/forge-gallery?scope=community`. `changefreq: daily` in the sitemap. | AR Studio (multi-model live camera scene) | [pages/ar-studio.html](pages/ar-studio.html) `→ /ar/studio` + [src/ar-studio.js](src/ar-studio.js), multi-anchor WebXR session [src/ar/multi-place.js](src/ar/multi-place.js), pure scene math [src/ar/studio-scene.js](src/ar/studio-scene.js) | Live | The standalone multi-model AR surface: place ANY number of models/avatars into one live camera view (every earlier AR lane was single-model). Model sources: in-view forge (`POST /api/forge {backend:"nvidia"}` runs behind the live camera, result drops into the room), the forge gallery (`/api/forge-gallery`, yours + community tabs), recent forges (localStorage, shared with `/ar`), the CC0 Object Library (`/api/objects/library`, an Objects tray tab with client-side name/category search), a pasted GLB link, or repeatable `?src=` deep links (https/relative only). Rendering ladder: WebXR immersive-ar with one XRAnchor per placed model + always-armed reticle (Android), getUserMedia passthrough + gyro world-lock (iOS/everywhere), grid preview + drag-look + QR handoff (desktop). Gestures: tap select, drag move, pinch resize, two-finger twist rotate; per-model duplicate/rotate/remove, clear-with-undo. **Real device AR, not just the camera composite:** the AR control resolves what the device can actually do and routes there rather than defaulting every non-WebXR device to the composite: the immersive WebXR session where it exists, Apple Quick Look on iOS and Google Scene Viewer on Android otherwise (both selectable per-model from the selection bar too), and an honest "open this on a phone" QR prompt everywhere else. The button relabels itself to match ("Immersive" only where an immersive session is real). Quick Look reads USDZ, so the selected model's GLB is converted on the device through the same `usdz-pipeline.js` bake `/avatars/:id/ar` uses, not the camera-passthrough composite, which has no real-world plane detection or scale and previously shipped as the only iOS experience. Humanoids with no baked clips idle via the universal retarget ([src/irl/pin-idle.js](src/irl/pin-idle.js)); scene persists in localStorage and the whole ARRANGEMENT (models + transforms) round-trips through a base64url `#s=` hash on the share/QR URL, so a composed scene reopens exactly on any device. **Presence realism:** WebXR placements are lit by the real room via lighting-estimation ([src/ar/estimated-lighting.js](src/ar/estimated-lighting.js), three's `XREstimatedLight`, real primary-light direction/colour + a live environment cube map so metal/glass reflect the actual room, reverting to baked lights when the device isn't estimating) and occluded by real geometry via depth-sensing ([src/ar/depth-occlusion.js](src/ar/depth-occlusion.js)); the passthrough path matches room brightness AND colour by sampling the camera feed. Desktop keyboard controls (arrows nudge / R rotate / D duplicate / Delete). Photo capture composites camera + WebGL ([src/irl/share-frame.js](src/irl/share-frame.js)). Reachable from `/ar`, the forge AR panel, and the `/viewer` action sheet. **Shared rooms (build together, live):** "Share live" opens a room with a 6-char code; anyone who opens `/ar/studio?room=CODE` joins the same scene and every add / move / scale / rotate / remove delta-syncs in real time, with a live "N here" presence pill. New Colyseus room [multiplayer/src/rooms/StudioRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/StudioRoom.js) + schema [multiplayer/src/studio-schemas.js](multiplayer/src/studio-schemas.js) (owner-gated edits, per-room/per-owner caps, per-client rate limit, https-only src validation, the same discipline as WalkRoom's object channel), client wrapper [src/ar/studio-net.js](src/ar/studio-net.js), and a shared logical coordinate frame [src/ar/studio-coords.js](src/ar/studio-coords.js) (relEast/relNorth/yawDeg) so every device agrees on placement; QR-marker colocalization ([src/irl/marker-anchor.js](src/irl/marker-anchor.js)) is the co-located "same physical table" upgrade. Runs on the same Colyseus host as /walk and /irl (a new room, not a new process). Tests: [tests/ar-studio-scene.test.js](tests/ar-studio-scene.test.js), [tests/ar-studio-coords.test.js](tests/ar-studio-coords.test.js), [tests/ar-estimated-lighting.test.js](tests/ar-estimated-lighting.test.js), [tests/studio-room.test.js](tests/studio-room.test.js). **Shipped as open source:** the same surface is extracted, generalized and published as [3d-ar-studio](https://www.npmjs.com/package/3d-ar-studio) (repo [nirholas/3D-AR-Studio](https://github.com/nirholas/3D-AR-Studio), demo https://nirholas.github.io/3D-AR-Studio/, MCP server `io.github.nirholas/3d-ar-studio`), so any site can embed a multi-model AR studio with one script tag. It defaults to OUR catalogue and generation lane (`/cdn/objects/library/manifest.json`, `/api/mcp-studio`, `/animations/manifest.json`, `/hdri/*`, `/api/ar`), which is why those surfaces answer open CORS. |

The layout is a pure, seeded function so the browser, the GLB exporter, the MCP tools and the npm package all build the identical city from one link. Crawler manners are part of the product: a declared user agent, `robots.txt` honoured per RFC 9309, one fetch per page cached fleet-wide for an hour, and an IP-pinned SSRF guard with size and time ceilings. `format=glb` returns the world as glTF with named nodes. Free public `verify_provenance` / `GET /api/provenance?src=…` recompute the hash and return `verified | tampered | unknown` (a single changed byte → tampered); the verify path and response are coin-clean (both stores). `anchor_provenance` signs + writes the SPL Memo anchor + stores the credential in R2. Tests: [tests/provenance-3d.test.js](tests/provenance-3d.test.js). Evidence: [prompts/store-submissions/_generated/provenance/](prompts/store-submissions/_generated/provenance/). Store-submission prompt 19. |
| Simulation readiness (a physics grade for 3D assets) | Spec [specs/SIM_READINESS.md](specs/SIM_READINESS.md), pure core [api/_lib/sim-readiness.js](api/_lib/sim-readiness.js), storage [api/_lib/sim-readiness-store.js](api/_lib/sim-readiness-store.js) + migration [20260813180000_sim_readiness_grades.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260813180000_sim_readiness_grades.sql), free MCP tool [api/_mcp3d/tools/sim-readiness.js](api/_mcp3d/tools/sim-readiness.js) (`grade_sim_readiness`), free REST [api/sim-readiness.js](api/sim-readiness.js) `→ /api/sim-readiness`, shared badge + details panel [public/sim-readiness-panel.js](public/sim-readiness-panel.js) (`<sim-readiness>`, used by [public/viewer.html](public/viewer.html) and [src/model-page.js](src/model-page.js)), write path in [api/_lib/forge-store.js](api/_lib/forge-store.js) `materializeCreation`, docs [docs/sim-readiness.md](docs/sim-readiness.md). | Live | The question a renderer never asks and a rigid-body solver always does: can this mesh be simulated as-is? Four verdicts (`simulation_ready`, `needs_scale`, `needs_repair`, `unusable`, plus `unreadable` for non-glTF bytes) over measurements derived entirely from the geometry: watertightness and winding via welded-position topology, real-world extents read as meters per the glTF unit convention, exact volume/centroid/inertia from the divergence theorem and tetrahedron covariance, and a convex-hull proxy with its convexity ratio. Nothing is guessed: where a value cannot be known from the mesh (true scale, density) the report says so. Grades are keyed by GLB content hash, not URL, so one row serves the forge creation, the Object Library entry, a remix and an arbitrary third-party URL; every finished generation is graded on the write path, so a model page renders its verdict with no extra request. Signed into the content credential as `simReadiness` (provenance v2), which is what makes the physics claim as forge-proof as the authorship claim. Free, no account, no coin surface, both stores. Tests: [tests/sim-readiness.test.js](tests/sim-readiness.test.js), [tests/api/sim-readiness-endpoint.test.js](tests/api/sim-readiness-endpoint.test.js), [tests/provenance-3d.test.js](tests/provenance-3d.test.js). |

| Live Agents wall (mission control) | [pages/agents-live.html](pages/agents-live.html) + [src/agents-live.js](src/agents-live.js) `→ /agents-live` | In-repo · roster [api/agents/public.js](api/agents/public.js) (`sort=live`), batched activity [api/agents/activity.js](api/agents/activity.js), per-agent SSE [api/agent-screen-stream.js](api/agent-screen-stream.js) | Real-time grid of every meaningful agent, ranked by most-recent on-chain/skill action (never-used placeholders suppressed). Each card streams a live caster feed when watched, else its real `agent_actions` as a terminal + an "active Nm ago" recency chip. Two feed layers, because they answer different questions: every mounted card gets its activity from ONE batched `/api/agents/activity` call (whole page, one round-trip, shared row mapper [api/_lib/agent-activity.js](api/_lib/agent-activity.js)), while SSE listeners are pooled ([src/shared/stream-pool.js](src/shared/stream-pool.js), sized from the origin's real transport) and spent only where something is genuinely pushed: a casting agent, the spotlight, a warming caster, or the card a viewer reaches for. Layers: showrunner spotlight ([src/showrunner.js](src/showrunner.js)), reputation arena ([src/agents-live-arena.js](src/agents-live-arena.js)), platform ticker ([src/theater-feed.js](src/theater-feed.js)) |
| On-demand caster pool (the live browser behind a watched agent) | Worker [workers/agent-screen-pool/](workers/agent-screen-pool/README.md) (deploy [workers/agent-screen-pool/cloudbuild.yaml](workers/agent-screen-pool/cloudbuild.yaml)); API watch set [api/agent/watch-intent.js](api/agent/watch-intent.js) + [api/agent/watch-wanted.js](api/agent/watch-wanted.js) + [api/agent/watch-status.js](api/agent/watch-status.js), frame sink [api/agent-screen-push.js](api/agent-screen-push.js) | In-repo · built + deploy-ready, not yet deployed as a Cloud Run service | Casts a real Chromium page for exactly the agents viewers are watching right now (bounded by `MAX_BROWSERS`), running a real multi-step web task from [tasks/index.js](workers/agent-screen-pool/tasks/index.js) and pushing JPEG frames the wall renders; tears each browser down when the last viewer leaves, so cost scales with concurrent viewers instead of agent count. Auth is the shared `SCREEN_WORKER_SECRET` (Secret Manager `screen-worker-secret`); each authenticated poll refreshes the pool-liveness key so a card with no pool running stays on the honest zero-cost activity view. Tests: [tests/agent-screen-task-sequencer.test.js](tests/agent-screen-task-sequencer.test.js) (sequencer + task library), [scripts/agent-screen-pool-proof.mjs](scripts/agent-screen-pool-proof.mjs) (real worker, real Chromium, real wire contract) |
| Take the wheel (owner drives the agent's computer) | Client [src/agent-screen-control.js](src/agent-screen-control.js) mounted in [src/agent-screen.js](src/agent-screen.js) `→ /agent-screen`; API [api/agent-screen-control.js](api/agent-screen-control.js) (lease + input) + [api/agent-screen-control-drain.js](api/agent-screen-control-drain.js) (caster read side); caster dispatch [workers/agent-screen-pool/control.js](workers/agent-screen-pool/control.js); shared sanitizer [src/shared/agent-screen-control.js](src/shared/agent-screen-control.js) | In-repo · Live · doc [docs/agent-screen-control.md](docs/agent-screen-control.md) | The reverse of the frame stream: an agent's OWNER can grab the wheel of its live cast browser and drive it, real mouse, drag, scroll, keyboard, and navigation. Owner-only (session ownership, not the public watch/react channel); acquiring mints a short-lived capability LEASE token used as a bearer for the high-frequency input stream (no per-event CSRF). Events travel normalized (0..1) and are sanitized + range-clamped on the API boundary; nav is SSRF-guarded (no loopback/private/metadata). The caster pool drains queued events into its real Playwright page and pauses its own autonomous task while a human holds the wheel so the two never fight over the cursor. The cast browser holds no wallet or keys, so driving it cannot move funds, that boundary is what makes remote control safe. Tests: [tests/agent-screen-control.test.js](tests/agent-screen-control.test.js) (21 unit), [scripts/agent-screen-control-proof.mjs](scripts/agent-screen-control-proof.mjs) (real Chromium dispatch) |

Play mode also has an opt-in **"Record on-chain (BNB testnet)"** toggle (OFF by default) that gaslessly commits your moves to the `WorldMoves` contract every ~block via MegaFuel (self-pay fallback) and renders other on-chain players as lightweight ghost markers read back from real `Moved` events: [src/agora/onchain-presence.js](src/agora/onchain-presence.js), sender [src/bnb/move-sender.js](src/bnb/move-sender.js), event reader [src/bnb/world-presence-reader.js](src/bnb/world-presence-reader.js), pure ghost interpolation [src/bnb/onchain-ghosts.js](src/bnb/onchain-ghosts.js), server relay lib [api/_lib/bnb/world-moves.js](api/_lib/bnb/world-moves.js), config endpoint [api/bnb/world-config.js](api/bnb/world-config.js). WorldMoves contract: [contracts/src/WorldMoves.sol](contracts/src/WorldMoves.sol) (code-complete + anvil-fork-proven; public BSC testnet deploy pending a funded deployer key — see `contracts/DEPLOYMENTS.md`). **Arena** (Competitive tasks — citizens race, first valid proof wins the whole escrow) at [src/agora/arena.js](src/agora/arena.js); **Guilds** (Collaborative tasks — contributors split the reward) at [src/agora/guild.js](src/agora/guild.js), both live-driven by [api/agora/task](api/agora/[action].js). Spec: [docs/agora.md](docs/agora.md) |
| `/play` in-game economy (cash, bank/ATM, vendors, $THREE boutique) | Client: [src/game/coincommunities.js](src/game/coincommunities.js) `→ /play`, general-store/bank NPCs [src/game/npc/economy-npcs.js](src/game/npc/economy-npcs.js), store/bank UI [src/game/economy-ui.js](src/game/economy-ui.js), on-chain purchase flow [src/game/boutique-purchase.js](src/game/boutique-purchase.js), wardrobe [src/game/play-systems.js](src/game/play-systems.js). Server: [multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js) (store/bank/boutique handlers), price tables [multiplayer/src/shop.js](multiplayer/src/shop.js), purse/bank model [multiplayer/src/economy.js](multiplayer/src/economy.js), on-chain settlement [multiplayer/src/game-token.js](multiplayer/src/game-token.js) | In-repo | The GTA-style open world's real economy (W04 of the World Online program, program brief retired to git history). Walk up to a General Store clerk (E) to buy tools/consumables or sell gathered goods for cash, server-priced and validated every time; walk up to the Bank Teller to deposit cash into a protected balance (survives a death drop) or withdraw it. Premium wardrobe cosmetics unlock with a real on-chain `$THREE` payment from the player's connected Solana wallet; the server only grants the item after re-verifying the confirmed transaction on RPC. | `/play` character & avatar customization (avatar creator, selfie→3D, wardrobe boutique) | Lobby creator flow [src/game/coincommunities-ui.js](src/game/coincommunities-ui.js) `_openCreate()`, selfie SDK wrapper [src/avatar-creator.js](src/avatar-creator.js), shop [src/game/cosmetics-shop.js](src/game/cosmetics-shop.js), wardrobe [src/game/cosmetics-wardrobe.js](src/game/cosmetics-wardrobe.js), boutique NPCs [src/game/npc/npc-catalog.js](src/game/npc/npc-catalog.js) (`npc-tailor`, `npc-fitting-room`), boutique spawn points [src/game/world-zones.js](src/game/world-zones.js) (`type: 'boutique'`), in-world avatar switcher [src/game/avatar-switcher.js](src/game/avatar-switcher.js) (HUD Avatar button / `V`, swap applied in [src/game/coincommunities.js](src/game/coincommunities.js) `_applyAvatarSwap()`) | In-repo | W03 of the World Online program (program brief retired to git history).
