| `/play` forge-in-world (prompt or photo to a live prop) | Lane client [src/game/forge-prop.js](src/game/forge-prop.js), palette form + `/forge` chat command [src/game/coincommunities-ui.js](src/game/coincommunities-ui.js) (`_buildForgeRow()`, `_submitForge()`), placement handler [src/game/coincommunities.js](src/game/coincommunities.js) (`_forgeProp()`), styles [src/game/coincommunities.css](src/game/coincommunities.css), rendering [src/game/world-objects.js](src/game/world-objects.js) (`registerUploadedProp`), server gate [multiplayer/src/build-limits.js](multiplayer/src/build-limits.js) (`normalizePropAssetUrl`) | In-repo | Describe an item in the build palette's Forge form (or type `/forge a glowing campfire` in world chat, or attach a reference photo) and the free draft forge lane generates a real textured GLB in about half a minute. The finished model is registered as a placeable prop and armed, so the next click drops it into the shared world through the SAME `obj:spawn` channel player uploads use: the URL travels on the object, every connected player renders it, and the room persists it like any other build. No new server surface was needed because the durable forge URL (`S3_PUBLIC_DOMAIN`, an `*.r2.dev` host) already satisfies the world asset allow-list; an off-list host is refused with `obj:reject`/`asset_url`. Generation is one-at-a-time per player, and every failure (rate limit, refused prompt, dead upload, timeout) reports a recovery line in the palette's status strip. Docs: [docs/play-forge-in-world.md](docs/play-forge-in-world.md). Tests: [tests/forge-prop.test.js](tests/forge-prop.test.js) |
| `/play` photo mode (the screenshot people post) | Card compositor + preview sheet [src/game/photo-mode.js](src/game/photo-mode.js) + [src/game/photo-mode.css](src/game/photo-mode.css), shared offscreen capture [src/game/scene-capture.js](src/game/scene-capture.js), HUD camera button [src/game/coincommunities-ui.js](src/game/coincommunities-ui.js) (`photoBtn`, `setPhotoActive()`), `P` keybinding + lazy host [src/game/coincommunities.js](src/game/coincommunities.js) (`_openPhotoMode()`), event agenda action [src/game/meetup-event.js](src/game/meetup-event.js) (`_capturePhoto()`) | In-repo | Press `P` or the HUD camera button and the world lands on a monochrome share card: the shot, the three.ws mark, the coin's name and symbol, the date, and during a live event that event's own name, read from the same `public/event.json` the countdown and agenda run on, so an event photo stamps itself with nothing to keep in sync. Download and Copy-image sit under a preview that the world keeps running behind; Escape or the X closes it, and `P` retakes over it. **The shot is an offscreen render, not a canvas grab.** `captureSceneCanvas()` renders one extra frame into a `WebGLRenderTarget` and reads that back, which is why the HUD, chat, name tags and the preview card itself are never in the frame, why the classic black-screenshot bug (reading a drawing buffer the compositor already took) cannot happen, and why nobody pays the permanent per-frame cost of `preserveDrawingBuffer`. Cost when no one is taking a photo is zero twice over: no extra render, and the whole module plus its CSS is imported on the FIRST press. Deliberately alive in zen mode, building and driving, since a clean world is exactly when the shot is wanted. Clipboard support is feature-detected: where `ClipboardItem` is missing the Copy button stays visible and says so rather than breaking. Same capture path backs the build-share thumbnail (`_captureBuildShot()`). Tests: [tests/photo-mode.test.js](tests/photo-mode.test.js) (compositor geometry, preview sheet, clipboard fallback, event stamp), [tests/e2e/play-photo-mode.spec.js](tests/e2e/play-photo-mode.spec.js) (real Chromium + WebKit, pixels of the saved PNG), walkthrough [scripts/play-photo-mode-check.mjs](scripts/play-photo-mode-check.mjs) |
| `/play` combat & weapons (server-authoritative, danger zones, wanted, tombstones) | Client: [src/game/combat-system.js](src/game/combat-system.js) `→ /play` (vitals HUD wiring, danger-zone ground, tombstone loot, hit feedback, the `window.twsCombat` bridge into W08's `MobSystem`), HUD [src/game/hud/world-hud.js](src/game/hud/world-hud.js). Server: [multiplayer/src/combat-handlers.js](multiplayer/src/combat-handlers.js) (mob AI, attack/loot intents, death + respawn), pure math [multiplayer/src/combat.js](multiplayer/src/combat.js), weapon/mob/loot tables [multiplayer/src/items.js](multiplayer/src/items.js), zone geometry [multiplayer/src/world-features.js](multiplayer/src/world-features.js), handlers wired in [multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js) | In-repo | W07 of the World Online program (program brief retired to git history). Equip a sword/bat/pistol/bow from the hotbar and press `X` (or tap the touch Attack button) to fight; but only inside one of three named danger zones (Southern Wilds, Northern Wilds, Eastern Marches); town stays lawful by construction. PvE mobs chase and swing back through the same server-side damage path a player's attack uses; a kill (mob or PvP) spills a lootable tombstone, PvP raises the attacker's wanted/heat stars (visible on `WorldHud` and a downed peer's nameplate), and death drops carried cash/pack before an automatic respawn in town. |
| `/play` NPCs & world life (traffic, vendors, quest-givers, mobs) | Manager [src/game/npc/world-life.js](src/game/npc/world-life.js), nav graph [src/game/npc/nav-graph.js](src/game/npc/nav-graph.js), ambient crowd/traffic [src/game/npc/ambient-life.js](src/game/npc/ambient-life.js), quest-giver NPCs [src/game/npc/quest-npcs.js](src/game/npc/quest-npcs.js), quest-zone waypoints [src/game/npc/quest-markers.js](src/game/npc/quest-markers.js), Jobs Board UI [src/game/quests-ui.js](src/game/quests-ui.js), W07-gated mobs [src/game/npc/mobs.js](src/game/npc/mobs.js). Server: quest engine [multiplayer/src/quests.js](multiplayer/src/quests.js), quest zones [multiplayer/src/quest-zones.js](multiplayer/src/quest-zones.js), handlers in [multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js) | In-repo | W08 of the World Online program (program brief retired to git history). A deterministic nav graph drives client-side ambient pedestrians + ring-road traffic that read identically for every player without a single packet; real gameplay stays server-authoritative. Five quest-giver NPCs (Dockmaster Reyes, Warden Okoro, Cook Mara, Foreman Dell, The Fixer) front the jobs board the quest engine (W05) already ran server-side but no client ever reached; pressing E opens the real board, accepting a job drops a waypoint at its objective (goto zones auto-complete by walking there, interact zones get their own "press E"), and finishing pays real cash server-side. Hostile mobs are live: W07's `CombatSystem` ([src/game/combat-system.js](src/game/combat-system.js)) installs the `window.twsCombat` contract this module's mobs were built against, so `MobSystem` renders real server-authoritative PvE hostiles with zero changes here. Two jobs; `cross-town-delivery` (North↔South) and `east-west-express` (East↔West); are real driving missions: each `goto` leg is flagged `vehicle: true` and only completes off the authoritative vehicle-driver map ([multiplayer/src/vehicles.js](multiplayer/src/vehicles.js)), so walking through a depot never counts, only driving through it does. Depots sit on the avenue vehicle spawns, so a car is always parked at the start. Tests: [tests/quests-vehicle-delivery.test.js](tests/quests-vehicle-delivery.test.js). |
| `/play` vehicles (the drivable fleet & the Trench Car) | Handling spec + fleet registry [multiplayer/src/vehicles.js](multiplayer/src/vehicles.js), client manager [src/game/vehicles.js](src/game/vehicles.js), meshes [src/game/vehicle-mesh.js](src/game/vehicle-mesh.js), shared GLB loader [src/game/vehicle-model.js](src/game/vehicle-model.js), staged model [public/vehicles/](public/vehicles) (built by [scripts/build-trench-car.mjs](scripts/build-trench-car.mjs), `npm run build:trench-car`), Rapier chassis [src/physics/physics-world.js](src/physics/physics-world.js), server gate + anti-cheat [multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js) | In-repo | W02 of the World Online program. Real cars you drive with WASD (touch gets pedals and a wheel), simulated locally on the SAME Rapier world the character controller uses, so a car collides with the town exactly as a walking avatar does, and relayed through a server that validates per-type speed and bounds and owns who may drive what. The **Trench Car**, a community model from the avatar gallery (`trench-car`), is the only car in the world: ambient traffic drives it in every town ([src/game/npc/ambient-life.js](src/game/npc/ambient-life.js)) and it is parked in every vehicle bay, so the car you watch go past is the car you can get into (the four procedural box types it used to share the road with were retired 2026-08-17). A car builds a procedural stand-in instantly and swaps the GLB body and wheels into the same group and pivots when the download lands, so a car that upgrades mid-drive keeps its physics, camera and driver, and a failed download still leaves a drivable car. One shared template per model; only the brake-light material is per car. Drivers sit in a seated pose that peers see too (server-authored `drive` motion). Docs: [docs/play-vehicles.md](docs/play-vehicles.md). Tests: [tests/vehicles-trench-car.test.js](tests/vehicles-trench-car.test.js), [tests/e2e/play-vehicles.spec.js](tests/e2e/play-vehicles.spec.js), [tests/quests-vehicle-delivery.test.js](tests/quests-vehicle-delivery.test.js); real-Rapier repro `node scripts/verify-w02-physics-core.mjs trench` |

Guards: `cssBgImage()` + `clampParam()` + `isPlausibleMint()` in [src/game/coincommunities-ui.js](src/game/coincommunities-ui.js) / [src/game/coincommunities.js](src/game/coincommunities.js), avatar template cache + `releaseAvatar()` in [src/game/avatar-rig.js](src/game/avatar-rig.js), reconnect pruning (`_markRemotesStale`/`_pruneStaleRemotes`) and `_disposeObject3D()` in [src/game/coincommunities.js](src/game/coincommunities.js). Harnesses: [scripts/play-desktop-audit.mjs](scripts/play-desktop-audit.mjs), [scripts/play-mobile-repro.mjs](scripts/play-mobile-repro.mjs) | Live | The rules that keep the platform's largest interactive surface safe, honest and phone-survivable, each written against a failure that actually shipped: untrusted display names and coin metadata never become markup or CSS (a tombstone owner's name once ran as HTML for everyone who walked past it; a crafted coin `image_uri` could paint a full-screen overlay across the lobby), a malformed `?coin=` is refused instead of building a convincing world nobody else can join, a *valid* `?coin=` that lost the rest of its query (a hand-typed link, a truncated share, an unfurl that kept only the mint) is named from the coin feed by `_fetchCoinIdentity()`/`mergeCoinIdentity()` instead of building a real district under a totem reading "COMMUNITY" with a blank market cap, a reconnect retires the peers who left rather than accumulating frozen ghosts and a tripled headcount, and everything the world loads (crowd/NPC GLBs, the sun's 2048² shadow map, totem, jumbotron, dance floor, district, environment) is genuinely freed on the way out instead of held until the tab is killed. Tests: [tests/play-deeplink-safety.test.js](tests/play-deeplink-safety.test.js), [tests/play-deeplink-identity.test.js](tests/play-deeplink-identity.test.js) |

| `/play` Wheel of Fortune (Fortune's Folly; free/paid $THREE spins) | Client: [src/game/wheel-station.js](src/game/wheel-station.js) (the plaza landmark, proximity prompt, `E` to spin; lazy-imports the actual UI on first interact), spin UI [src/game/spin-wheel-ui.js](src/game/spin-wheel-ui.js) (20-segment wheel, free/paid flow, wallet signing). Server: [multiplayer/src/spin-wheel.js](multiplayer/src/spin-wheel.js) (paytable, RNG, level/cooldown/pack gates, handlers), split-payment settlement [multiplayer/src/game-token.js](multiplayer/src/game-token.js) (`buildSpinPayment`/`verifySpinPayment`; the same primitives the $THREE boutique uses), wheel location [multiplayer/src/world-features.js](multiplayer/src/world-features.js) `WHEEL`, persisted cooldown field in [multiplayer/src/economy.js](multiplayer/src/economy.js), handlers wired in [multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js) | In-repo | W09 of the World Online program (program brief retired to git history). Walk up to the wheel in the plaza and press `E`: one free spin every 12h (a real persisted cooldown; survives a disconnect, unlike the ephemeral per-action cooldowns everything else on this row's neighbors use), or pay $3 in `$THREE`; split 50% to the holder-rewards pool / 50% to the treasury, verified on-chain before any prize is granted, mirroring the boutique's proven settlement pattern exactly. Gated behind an average skill level of 3 (a light anti-farm floor, not a grind) and never lets a won prize get lost to a full pack; room for at least one possible item prize is checked before a spin is even offered, paid or free. All 20 wedges are equal-odds by design, matching the client's fixed-angle wheel rendering exactly (see the header note in `spin-wheel.js` on why that must never drift). Tests: [tests/spin-wheel.test.js](tests/spin-wheel.test.js). |
| `/play` live events (countdown chip, agenda drawer, synchronized fireworks) | Config `public/event.json` (one file, shared with the lobby countdown and the server-side souvenir grant). Client: in-world layer [src/game/meetup-event.js](src/game/meetup-event.js) + [src/game/meetup-event.css](src/game/meetup-event.css), pure schedule math [src/game/meetup-schedule.js](src/game/meetup-schedule.js), particle show [src/game/fireworks.js](src/game/fireworks.js), lobby banner + wayfinding pill [src/game/event-countdown.js](src/game/event-countdown.js). Mounted from [pages/play.html](pages/play.html) | In-repo | Turns the `$THREE` home town into a place something is *happening* in for the length of a scheduled event, driven entirely by `public/event.json`: reset it to its no-event state (`npm run event:schedule -- --clear --apply`) and the world is normal again, with no code change. A countdown chip docks under the King of the Totem card (and stands the generic pill down, so there is only ever one countdown on screen) and opens a right-docked agenda drawer that marks the running segment, dims finished ones, counts the rest down in local time, and carries a live `$THREE` pulse row (`/api/three-signal`) plus one-tap Buy and commemorative-photo actions. Six phases derive from the wall clock (`far → upcoming → preshow → live → afterglow → ended`), so every player agrees on what is on and what is next without a round-trip. **Fireworks are deterministic, not networked:** four-second buckets hashed with the event id give every client the identical sky (`fireworkPlan()`), the same trick [src/shared/world-clock.js](src/shared/world-clock.js) uses for the shared sun; density ramps into a closing barrage, power-saver halves it, at most 14 shells live at once, and a backgrounded tab rejoins the show live instead of replaying the backlog `requestAnimationFrame` paused through. Preview any schedule with `?meetup=now` or `?meetup=<ISO>`. Doc: [docs/play-live-events.md](docs/play-live-events.md). Browser audit: `npm run audit:meetup` ([scripts/meetup-audit.mjs](scripts/meetup-audit.mjs)). Tests: [tests/meetup-schedule.test.js](tests/meetup-schedule.test.js), [tests/meetup-event-ui.test.js](tests/meetup-event-ui.test.js) |
| `/play` event souvenirs (a free wearable you keep for having been there) | Config `public/event.json` (`souvenir.cosmeticId`, the same file the countdown reads). Server: window/world/item resolution [multiplayer/src/event-drop.js](multiplayer/src/event-drop.js), join-time grant `_grantEventSouvenir` in [multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/WalkRoom.js), `event` tier + catalog entry [multiplayer/src/cosmetics-catalog.js](multiplayer/src/cosmetics-catalog.js), unlock + persistence [multiplayer/src/economy.js](multiplayer/src/economy.js). Client: drop card [src/game/event-souvenir.js](src/game/event-souvenir.js), wardrobe treatment [src/game/cosmetics-wardrobe.js](src/game/cosmetics-wardrobe.js). Asset: [public/accessories/laurel-meetup.glb](public/accessories/laurel-meetup.glb) generated by [scripts/generate-accessory-glbs.mjs](scripts/generate-accessory-glbs.mjs), poster by [scripts/render-accessory-thumbs.mjs](scripts/render-accessory-thumbs.mjs) | In-repo | Everyone who joins the event world between an event's `startsAt` and `endsAt` is granted a free commemorative wearable (the Meetup Laurel), once, forever, wearable in every coin world. After `endsAt` it is never granted again and there is no purchase path at any point: a third catalog tier, `event`, carries `price: 0`, so the boutique's `tier === 'premium' && price > 0` filter excludes it by construction and the x402 purchase ledger is filtered to premium ids so it cannot mint one. Server-authoritative and idempotent: the window and the world both come from the shared event config (never a client option), the grant is a no-op on an account that already owns it, and the one-shot `souvenir` message only fires on the not-owned to owned transition, so a reconnect produces neither a duplicate nor a second announcement. Fails open on every axis (no config, unreachable config, closed window, wrong world all mean grant nothing and never delay a join). Doc: [docs/event-souvenirs.md](docs/event-souvenirs.md). Tests: [tests/event-souvenir.test.js](tests/event-souvenir.test.js); live conformance `node scripts/play-souvenir-conformance.mjs` (real server, no browser) and `node scripts/play-souvenir-e2e.mjs` (real browser) |

API: [api/wars.js](api/wars.js) `→ /api/wars` over [api/_lib/wars-store.js](api/_lib/wars-store.js) (ledger + queue + live registry) and [api/_lib/war-ticket.js](api/_lib/war-ticket.js) (signs a pairing). Server: [multiplayer/src/rooms/ClashRoom.js](multiplayer/src/rooms/ClashRoom.js) `clash_arena`, pairing math [multiplayer/src/war-matchmaking.js](multiplayer/src/war-matchmaking.js), ticket verify [multiplayer/src/war-ticket.js](multiplayer/src/war-ticket.js), spectator snapshots [multiplayer/src/war-live.js](multiplayer/src/war-live.js), league math [multiplayer/src/war-standings.js](multiplayer/src/war-standings.js), portal location [multiplayer/src/world-features.js](multiplayer/src/world-features.js) `WAR_PORTAL` | In-repo | F18. Coin Wars ran full community-vs-community battles with an Elo league behind it and no door into it from any world. The portal is that door: a plaza landmark whose board shows this coin's real league standing, its last battles, and any war running right now (read from `/api/wars`, folded through the arena's own standings module, never a second implementation). Press `E` to run the coin's holder gate and queue the community; the matchmaker pairs it with another waiting community, seals both sides into an HMAC war ticket, and hands the player to `/play/war` with a return link carrying the full coin identity, so walking back lands in the same world with the result already echoing on the board. While a war is live the board becomes a spectator screen (score, round clock, kill feed) off a cheap poll, and a win takes the board over for a few seconds. Costs nothing when nobody is near it: no poll fires outside `WAR_PORTAL_BOARD_REACH`. Docs: [docs/coin-wars.md](docs/coin-wars.md). Tests: [tests/war-matchmaking.test.js](tests/war-matchmaking.test.js), [tests/clash-match.test.js](tests/clash-match.test.js) |
| `/play` event countdown (lobby banner + in-world pill) | Self-attaching module [src/game/event-countdown.js](src/game/event-countdown.js) loaded by [pages/play.html](pages/play.html); config `public/event.json` (single source of the event window; the file is the edit surface for scheduling an event), read through the shared parser + state machine in [src/shared/event-config.js](src/shared/event-config.js). Verified end to end in a real browser by `npm run verify:event` ([scripts/verify-event-countdown.mjs](scripts/verify-event-countdown.mjs)). Campaign orders: the `event-` files in [prompts/finish/](prompts/finish/) | In-repo | While `public/event.json` describes an upcoming or live event, the lobby shows a banner (name, tagline, local-time start, ticking countdown, CTA into the event world) and the in-world view gets a dismissible top-center pill (dismissal persisted per event in localStorage). Three explicit states: upcoming, live (pulsing marker, CTA suppressed when already standing in the event world), and over (everything unmounts, the interval stops). Zero edits to `coincommunities*.js` by design (the ambient-crowd pattern): it waits for the lobby DOM and mounts nothing when the config is missing, malformed, or expired. |
| The event landing page (`/event`) | [pages/event.html](pages/event.html) `→ /event`, page module [src/event-page.js](src/event-page.js); config `public/event.json` (the SAME file the `/play` countdown reads, never a second copy of the times); live population [api/play/population.js](api/play/population.js) `→ /api/play/population`, backed by the multiplayer server's public `/population` aggregate in [multiplayer/src/index.js](multiplayer/src/index.js) | Live | The one link the community shares before and during an event, written for someone with zero context. Hero countdown ticking off `public/event.json`, a single large CTA into the event world, a plain-language "what to expect" built only from what `/play` actually does, and the run of show rendered in the visitor's own timezone with an "Add to calendar" button that builds a real RFC 5545 `.ics` in the browser (no third-party calendar service, so the guest list never leaves the device). Four designed states: upcoming (days/hours/minutes/seconds), live (LIVE marker plus the real number of people standing in the world), ended (says it happened, relabels the agenda to "What happened", and still routes people into `/play`), and no-config (an honest "no event scheduled" with the same door). The live number is real or absent: when the multiplayer server is unreachable the panel keeps the live state and drops the count instead of inventing one. Doc: [docs/api-reference.md](docs/api-reference.md#play-population-api). |

Press `J` or the HUD `Friends` button to open a right-docked drawer showing your account's friend graph; live online/offline presence per coin world, requests, and DM threads; the same graph and threads `/walk` and the standalone `/friends` page already share. `community-net.js` carries a signed presence ticket into every `walk_world` join and forwards the server's `social` push onto its own event bus, which is what makes a `/play` player visible to friends and able to receive a live DM. W10 makes the platform identity first-class in-world: the ticket also carries the account's username + display name (signed, so nobody can wear a handle they don't own), WalkRoom publishes the verified `username` on the Player schema, nameplates show the @handle, the signed-in lobby defaults to the account's display name with an @handle chip, and clicking any verified player (or `I`) opens their real three.ws profile card in the shared avatar inspector: bio, follower counts, follow/unfollow (`/api/users/:username/follow`), the right friends verb (Message → the in-world DM drawer when already friends, Add friend / Accept otherwise), their latest creations, and a link to `/u/<username>`. Works identically in `/walk` (same room, same inspector). | Crew HQ (crews, tags, 3D presence room) | Page [pages/crews.html](pages/crews.html) + [src/crews-page.js](src/crews-page.js) + [src/crews.css](src/crews.css) `→ /crews` and `/crews/<TAG>`, data layer [api/_lib/crews-store.js](api/_lib/crews-store.js), endpoints [api/crews/](api/crews) (`index.js`, `[tag].js`, `search.js`, `directory.js`), presence [api/_lib/presence-store.js](api/_lib/presence-store.js), schema `api/_lib/migrations/2026-06-05-crews.sql` | Live | The group half of the social system: a crew is a set of accounts flying one 2-6 character tag, with one owner, one crew per account, and a public headquarters. The HQ renders the roster as a room rather than a list, each member standing as their own agent's 3D avatar (resolved by `standeesFor()`, private models never emitted) and lit by the same Redis presence the friends drawer reads. Crew colour is an FNV-1a hash of the tag, so a crew looks like itself everywhere without stored art. Live `<agent-3d>` figures are capped and lazily mounted so a large crew cannot exhaust the browser's WebGL contexts. The backend shipped with W09 and had no UI at all until this page; the in-world friends drawer now links to it. Docs: [docs/crews.md](docs/crews.md). Tests: [tests/crews-shared.test.js](tests/crews-shared.test.js), [tests/crews-wiring.test.js](tests/crews-wiring.test.js) |
| Agent Spotlight (community showcase) | Index [pages/spotlight.html](pages/spotlight.html) + [src/spotlight.js](src/spotlight.js) `→ /spotlight`, entry page [pages/spotlight-entry.html](pages/spotlight-entry.html) + [src/spotlight-entry.js](src/spotlight-entry.js) `→ /spotlight/:id`, shared render + form modules [src/spotlight-shared.js](src/spotlight-shared.js) / [src/spotlight-form.js](src/spotlight-form.js), styles [src/spotlight.css](src/spotlight.css), crawler share cards [api/spotlight-og.js](api/spotlight-og.js), HTTP boundary [api/spotlight/[action].js](api/spotlight), queries + ranking [api/_lib/spotlight-store.js](api/_lib/spotlight-store.js), schema `api/_lib/migrations/20260901160000_agent_showcase.sql`, curated seed [scripts/seed-spotlight.mjs](scripts/seed-spotlight.mjs) | Live | The curated half of `/agents`: the index lists every agent ever registered, this lists the ones a person wrote up and the community voted on. An entry stores the pitch only (headline, one-liner, write-up, category, tags, demo link); the agent's name, avatar, skills, on-chain identity, conversation count and builder are read live off `agent_identities` on every request, so a card can never drift from the agent it points at. Ranked by `(votes + 1) / (age_days + 1) ^ 1.2`, computed identically in JS and in the SQL `ORDER BY`; the `+ 1` keeps a brand-new entry on the first page, and the day scale (rather than the hour scale every feed copies from Hacker News) is what stops a low-volume board's `trending` from collapsing into `newest`. One entry per agent (re-submitting edits it), one vote per account, votes counted on read rather than denormalised. Entries badged `Curated` were written by three.ws about someone else's public agent, say so in words on the entry page, and are replaced the moment that builder submits their own. The API lives at `/api/spotlight/*` because `/api/showcase` is the unrelated ERC-8004 directory ([api/showcase.js](api/showcase.js)); the tables keep the `agent_showcase` name they were applied under. Docs: [docs/spotlight.md](docs/spotlight.md). Tests: [tests/spotlight-ranking.test.js](tests/spotlight-ranking.test.js) |

| Sealed wallet gift drops (send/claim) | Client: [src/pages/sealed-drops.js](src/pages/sealed-drops.js) — create + "My drops" sections mounted into [public/vanity-wallet.html](public/vanity-wallet.html) (`#drop-create`/`#drop-mine`, below the vanity grinder — vanity pattern fields are shared), claim flow on its own page [pages/drop.html](pages/drop.html) `→ /drop/:id` (24-hex-char id, routed in [vite.config.js](vite.config.js) dev middleware + [vercel.json](vercel.json)). Crypto: [src/solana/vanity/sealed-envelope.js](src/solana/vanity/sealed-envelope.js) (ECIES seal/open, X25519 keygen — all client-side, three.ws never sees a plaintext key), [src/solana/vanity/drop-protocol.js](src/solana/vanity/drop-protocol.js) (claim-token derivation). Server: [api/vanity/drops.js](api/vanity/drops.js) (create/claim/reveal/reclaim, on-chain funding), OG card [api/og/sealed-drop.js](api/og/sealed-drop.js) | In-repo | Fund a fresh wallet — optionally vanity-ground with the same prefix/suffix grinder above — wrap it in a sealed link, and send it. Two seal modes: a **bearer link** (the claim key rides in the URL fragment, anyone with the full link claims once) or **sealed to a recipient's X25519 key** (only they can ever open it, even with the link). The create fee settles via x402; the gift amount funds on-chain into a fresh wallet server-side. Claiming decrypts entirely in the browser and offers the recovered seed phrase/secret key for import — the server only ever sees the sealed ciphertext. "My drops" (browser-local, by a random sender tag) lists what you've sent so expired-unclaimed gifts can be reclaimed. |
| Premium vanity inventory (sell pre-ground addresses) | [public/vanity/premium/](public/vanity/premium/index.html) `→ /vanity/premium`, batch grinder [workers/vanity-grinder/](workers/vanity-grinder), store [api/_lib/vanity-inventory-store.js](api/_lib/vanity-inventory-store.js), sell [api/x402/vanity-premium.js](api/x402/vanity-premium.js), replenish [api/cron/vanity-inventory-replenish.js](api/cron/vanity-inventory-replenish.js) | In-repo · GCP spot CPU | Long (4–5+ char) brandable Solana addresses ground ahead of time on cheap spot CPU, sealed at rest (AES-256-GCM or GCP-KMS envelope, [api/_lib/vanity-vault.js](api/_lib/vanity-vault.js)), and sold from stock via x402 — priced by rarity ($1–$50) on `vanity-premium`, or by fixed 4–5 char tiers ($2.50/$10) directly through [`/api/x402/vanity`](docs/vanity.md#tier-1--live-grinder) (instant delivery, `source:"inventory"`) and the [pump-launch vanity upsell](docs/pump-launcher.md) — delivered exactly once, ciphertext destroyed on delivery. An hourly cron watches stock and fires a fresh grind batch (Cloud Run Job) when low. Free `vanity_premium` MCP browse tool. Runbook: [docs/ops/gcp-credits.md](docs/ops/gcp-credits.md) |

Also: [skills/](examples/skills) (six installable skill bundles), [three-concierge/](examples/three-concierge) (Trinity, reference agent for manifest spec agent-manifest/0.2), [monicas-apartment/](examples/monicas-apartment) (walkable first-person Three.js scene demo, added 2026-07-17), [agenc-task-roundtrip/](examples/agenc-task-roundtrip) (end-to-end AgenC task flow on Solana devnet), [metamask-agent-wallet/](examples/metamask-agent-wallet) (single-page demo giving an agent a server-side MetaMask wallet), [pump-fun-agent/](examples/pump-fun-agent) (example manifest composing the four production pump.fun skills), [paid-mcp-server/](examples/paid-mcp-server) (an MCP server whose tools charge per call in USDC on Solana over x402: one free orientation tool, one paid glTF/GLB inspector, 15 tests, added 2026-07-30) |
| Examples satellite repo (public developer front door) | Exporter [scripts/export-satellites.mjs](scripts/export-satellites.mjs) (`npm run export:satellites`), runbook [docs/ops/examples-repo-export.md](docs/ops/examples-repo-export.md), curated source [examples/](examples) + the published `@three-ws/*` SDKs | Built and gated in-repo; publishing is owner-gated (the `three-ws` GitHub org does not exist yet) | Assembles the public `three-ws/examples` repo from this monorepo as a strictly ONE-WAY export: the monorepo is the only source of truth, the satellite history is disposable, and it is never pulled, fetched, or merged from (same rule as the retired 3D-Agent mirror). No CI is involved (GitHub Actions are unavailable here), so the export runs locally from the push routine. Every relative reference is resolved against its real location and routed by where it lands rather than regex-swept: to the shipped file, to another exported unit (so an agent manifest's skill `uri` still resolves), to the published package on jsDelivr, to the live docs page, or to a monorepo permalink. A four-stage smoke gate then aborts the export on any failure, because a broken public example is anti-marketing: structure (no reference escapes or dangles, no `file:` dep survives), registry (every pinned `@three-ws/*` version exists on npm), install (`npm install` plus each example's own check command), and links (every `https://three.ws` URL reachable). On success it stamps a single-parent git history into the staging tree and prints the org-creation and push commands; it never touches a remote. Examples referencing a crypto project other than $THREE are held out of the manifest under the CLAUDE.md commit gate (`pump-fun-agent`, `three-concierge`, `agenc-task-roundtrip`). |
| Agent-native 3D + embeddable distribution | MCP tool [api/_mcp/tools/agents.js](api/_mcp/tools/agents.js) (`attach_avatar_to_agent`); shared oEmbed provider [api/agent-oembed.js](api/agent-oembed.js) `→ /api/oembed` + builder [api/_lib/embed.js](api/_lib/embed.js) (`forgeEmbedTarget`, shared by `get_embed_code` in [api/_mcp/tools/embed.js](api/_mcp/tools/embed.js)); Forge share page oEmbed discovery [api/forge-share.js](api/forge-share.js) `→ /forge/share/:id`; distribution snippet builders [src/forge-embed-snippets.js](src/forge-embed-snippets.js) + panel [src/forge-embed-panel.js](src/forge-embed-panel.js); autonomous demo [examples/agent-native-3d/](examples/agent-native-3d) | In-repo | Closes the loop: create → own → discover/remix → **use everywhere**. `attach_avatar_to_agent` gives a registered agent identity a persistent body (chains into `register_agent` for on-chain identity + `anchor_provenance` for a signed authenticity credential). Any Forge creation now unfurls via real oEmbed (paste `/forge/share/:id` into Notion/Discord/Slack) with the same `type:rich` iframe payload `get_embed_code` returns via MCP. The Forge "Embed this model" panel hands out five real distribution flavours from one GLB — iframe, `<model-viewer>`, `<agent-3d>`, a `@three-ws/page-agent` talking guide, and a `@three-ws/walk` companion — all built from one shared, pure snippet module so every surface stays byte-identical. `examples/agent-native-3d/run.mjs` drives the full agent-native chain live against `/api/mcp-studio` (mesh_forge → rig_mesh → create_agent_persona → persona_say → get_agent_persona → every distribution snippet) with zero mocks and a documented, logged fallback if the shared free-tier generation lane is rate-limited at run time. Spec: [specs/EMBED_SPEC.md](specs/EMBED_SPEC.md#share-routes-on-chain-agents). Docs: [docs/mcp.md#attach_avatar_to_agent](docs/mcp.md#attach_avatar_to_agent). The L4 quota is **per region** and both us-central1 and us-east4 are granted 3, so a warm pin in one region only starves lanes in that same region (increase to 16 filed 2026-07-16, still reconciling); the Hunyuan3D-2.1 PBR realism lane also ships an RTX PRO 6000 build (`model-hunyuan3d-21-rtx`, quota granted 1000) as the warm primary. `model-text2motion` (MDM, MIT) is the text→motion lane behind `GCP_TEXT2MOTION_URL`, the `text_to_animation` MCP tool, and `POST /api/forge-motion`: prompt in, a retargetable three.js `AnimationClip` out, applied with the same engine (`src/animation-retarget.js`) as the curated animation library. | Reconstruct completion backstop (abandoned selfie jobs) | [api/cron/reconstruct-sweep.js](api/cron/reconstruct-sweep.js), every 5 min via `vercel.json` crons → Cloud Scheduler | Live 2026-07-25 | A reconstruct job used to be advanced **only by the user's browser**: `/create/selfie` polls `regenerate-status`, and each poll runs the finalize stages. Close the tab and the worker still finished the GLB and stored it durably, but nothing ever collected it — 26 jobs were stranded that way, oldest from 2026-05-31, invisible to `db-retention` (which prunes only terminal rows). This sweep is the server-side driver: it picks up reconstruct jobs quiet >3 min and runs **the same shared stages** in [reconstruct-finalize.js](api/_lib/reconstruct-finalize.js), so an abandoned job still lands in the user's library. 30-day rescue window (the worker's GCS blobs + Firestore job state are durable, so a weeks-old job is still deliverable); past that, or when a job's provider is no longer pollable from platform credentials, rows fail out with an honest reason so the open set can't grow unbounded. BYOK (meshy/tripo) jobs stay browser-driven — a cron has no user context to resolve their keys. Sibling of [auto-rig-sweep.js](api/cron/auto-rig-sweep.js), which plays the same role for the rerig lane. Docs: [docs/avatar-reconstruction.md](docs/avatar-reconstruction.md#who-finishes-the-job) |
| Avatar fidelity evaluation (selfie → avatar, scored) | [workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval) | In-repo · gates every reconstruction change | **Identity Shape Error (ISE)** — does the reconstructed avatar have *this person's* face shape? Texture-blind, pose- and scale-invariant, dimensionless: MediaPipe's 468 landmarks and the avatar's corresponding head vertices are both Umeyama-aligned onto the neutral canonical face over a stable identity subset (no eyes/lips, so a smile is not scored as identity), then compared. A pipeline cannot score better by resizing or tilting the head — only by matching shape, a property pinned in [eval/test_identity_eval.py](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/test_identity_eval.py) alongside monotonicity. [`run_eval.py`](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/run_eval.py) scores a whole reference set against a live worker and diffs two revisions, surfacing per-face regressions a mean would hide; [`tune_morph.py`](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/tune_morph.py) sweeps the morph parameters against ISE offline; [`robustness.py`](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/robustness.py) measures the opposing axis (does a blurred / turned / occluded / dim / low-res photo of the same person still yield the same head?), and the shipped `max_displacement_frac` is the knee of those two curves. The 40-face reference set is **synthesised** by [`make_refs.py`](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/make_refs.py) over a deterministic grid of the platform's diversity matrix — no real biometrics, no licence terms, rebuildable by any agent — gitignored and mirrored to `gs://three-ws-avatar-reconstructions/eval-refs/`. Docs: [eval/README.md](workers/avatar-reconstruction/eval/README.md), program plan [docs/avatar-fidelity-program.md](docs/avatar-fidelity-program.md) |
| Avatar Composer (modular studio generation) | [api/_lib/avatar-composer/](api/_lib/avatar-composer) | In-repo · wired into the studio seed lane | Assembles a rigged, ARKit-expression-ready GLB by mixing skinned parts (hair / top / bottom / footwear / glasses) across the RPM base bodies onto **one shared 67-joint skeleton**, then recolors each part and scales the rig — the Ready-Player-Me / Avaturn "one skeleton, many parts" architecture in pure JS on `@gltf-transform`, no GPU or external service. Replaces the recolor-one-of-five-bodies studio lane so two draws almost never look alike. Borrowed parts keep their own inverse binds but remap onto the identity skeleton; mixing is restricted to a base's rest-pose group so parts always fit. Consumed by [api/cron/avaturn-seed-cron.js](api/cron/avaturn-seed-cron.js) (legacy [studio-avatar.js](api/_lib/studio-avatar.js) recolor is the fallback) and the one-click [api/avatars/surprise.js](api/avatars/surprise.js) endpoint (instant avatar, previewed in [public/gallery/surprise.js](public/gallery/surprise.js)). Docs: [api/_lib/avatar-composer/README.md](api/_lib/avatar-composer/README.md). Tests: [tests/avatar-composer.test.js](tests/avatar-composer.test.js) |

| Motion Swap (avatar body-swap in video) | [pages/motion-swap.html](pages/motion-swap.html) `→ /motion-swap` + [src/motion-swap.js](src/motion-swap.js), API [api/motion-swap.js](api/motion-swap.js), worker [workers/model-video2motion/](workers/model-video2motion) | Live | Upload a video of one person, get it back with a 3D avatar performing their motion. The worker (CPU Cloud Run, MediaPipe pose + hands + multiclass person segmentation, all Apache-2.0) tracks 33 body world landmarks plus 21 per hand each frame, solves them onto the canonical Wolf3D skeleton, body and all 30 finger bones, with an in-house bend-plane IK solver ([pose_solver.py](workers/model-video2motion/pose_solver.py), pure NumPy, unit-tested), and emits a standard `AnimationClip.toJSON()` + person-mask video + per-frame screen anchors. The browser composites in one WebGL scene: footage on a shader quad with the subject pixelated under the mask, avatar retargeted via [src/animation-retarget.js](src/animation-retarget.js) and pinned to the subject's anchor each frame, MediaRecorder export with original audio. Provider mode `video2motion` (`GCP_VIDEO2MOTION_URL`) in [api/_providers/gcp.js](api/_providers/gcp.js). Docs: [docs/motion-swap.md](docs/motion-swap.md). Tests: [test_pose_solver.py](workers/model-video2motion/test_pose_solver.py) (solver) and [test_service.py](workers/model-video2motion/test_service.py) (HTTP contract, ffmpeg normalize/mask encode, SSRF guard, live MediaPipe pass). |

Client [packages/home-bridge/](packages/home-bridge/README.md), MCP server [packages/home-mcp/](packages/home-mcp/README.md), hosted MCP tools [api/_mcp/tools/home.js](api/_mcp/tools/home.js). Migrations `api/_lib/migrations/*_home_*.sql` | Live | The agent past the screen and into the building. **Zero device code, ever:** Home Assistant owns Zigbee, Z-Wave, Matter, Thread, BLE and 1,500 integrations, and three.ws is the layer in between: a connection record with the token encrypted at rest, a pooled per-home WebSocket, a room graph the 3D scene renders live, intent resolved to the household's own scenes, and one gate in front of everything. **The gate is the product decision:** reads are free, moves toward safety (lock, close, arm) run immediately and never prompt, and moves that OPEN the house (unlock, open a door/gate/garage, disarm) never run from a model call at all. They mint a pending confirmation a signed-in person redeems at `POST /api/home/:id/confirm`, which refuses every bearer principal including one holding `home:act`. It exists because Home Assistant's own `intent__HassTurnOff` is documented as performing an **unlock** on a lock, verified live. Entity and area names are untrusted input travelling in `structuredContent`, never interpolated into prose. Three reachability answers, all shipped: a remote https URL, a browser or MCP server inside the network, and the HACS integration that dials out so no Home Assistant credential ever leaves the house. Controls for signing speed, left- vs right-handed signing, and which avatar signs: [src/sign-avatars.js](src/sign-avatars.js) owns the shared rig list, the one preference block every signing surface reads, and the Avatar pill group whose third pill opens the avatar gallery, so any avatar you own (or any public one) signs with the same vocabulary; a rig with no usable skeleton is rejected with a named message and the working rig is remounted. Verified in a real browser by `npm run verify:sign-avatar` ([scripts/verify-sign-avatar.mjs](scripts/verify-sign-avatar.mjs)). Engines: [src/sign-rig.js](src/sign-rig.js) (skeleton measured from the bind pose → forward kinematics, two-bone arm IK, and hand geometry, so a sign is authored as a place in signing space or as CONTACT, "fingertips on the palm", never as joint angles), [src/sign-handshapes.js](src/sign-handshapes.js) (A, Z, 0, 9, and the named shapes), [src/sign-clip.js](src/sign-clip.js) (resting signer, phase poser, non-manual face markers, eased timeline → clip JSON), [src/sign-dictionary.js](src/sign-dictionary.js) (the lexical vocabulary, one- and two-handed), [src/fingerspelling.js](src/fingerspelling.js) (the manual alphabet), [src/sign-speech.js](src/sign-speech.js) (text→one signing clip: hands rise once and lower once, signs where they exist and spelling for the rest), [src/sign-input.js](src/sign-input.js) (browser Holistic landmarks → text). Face markers ride a morph-target lane through [src/animation-retarget.js](src/animation-retarget.js), re-pointed at whichever meshes the target avatar owns. Tools: `npm run sign:sheet` (contact sheet of every sign, [scripts/sign-contact-sheet.mjs](scripts/sign-contact-sheet.mjs)), `npm run build:sign-goldens` (positional snapshot). Docs: [docs/sign-language.md](docs/sign-language.md). Tests: `tests/{sign-rig,sign-dictionary,sign-linguistics,sign-goldens,fingerspelling,sign-speech,sign-input}.test.js`. |

A single letter holds its final frame (`playOnce({ settleTo: null })`) so it can be orbited; a word settles like a signer finishing. Typing any letter key signs it. Spelling a word lights each key in step with the hand, driven by the `marks` schedule the clip builder emits rather than a parallel timer. A practice drill spells a random letter or word and checks what you type, keeping a streak in localStorage. Shares speed / dominant-hand / avatar preferences with [/sign-language](pages/sign-language.html) under one key via [src/sign-avatars.js](src/sign-avatars.js), including a custom avatar picked from the gallery on either page. Deep links `?letter=W`, `?spell=HELLO`. Docs: [docs/sign-language.md](docs/sign-language.md). |

| Smart home: the connect flow and household management | Page [pages/smart-home.html](pages/smart-home.html) `-> /smart-home` + controller [src/home/connect.js](src/home/connect.js), manage view [src/home/manage.js](src/home/manage.js), styles [src/home/home.css](src/home/home.css). Consumes [api/home/index.js](api/home/index.js) (`GET`/`POST /api/home`), [api/home/[id].js](api/home/[id].js), `/api/home/:id/grants` and `/api/home/:id/log`. Reachability rules shared with the server through the [@three-ws/home-bridge](packages/home-bridge) `./url` subpath export | Live | The front door of the Home lane: how a stranger gets from "I have a Home Assistant somewhere" to "my agent is connected", and every way that goes wrong. Twelve designed states rather than a happy path plus an error toast: signed out, empty, inline validation, the private-host refusal, verifying, connected, a rejected token, unreachable, degraded, disconnected, many homes, and the plan ceiling. Two rules hold the surface together. **The token goes to the server once and never comes back**: the field is `type=password` with a reveal toggle, it is never written to storage, never put in a URL, never logged and never echoed by the API, and it is gone from the DOM the moment a connect succeeds. **Reachability is decided in the browser, before the network**: a `192.168.x.x` or `.local` address is refused with zero network calls using the same `normalizeBaseUrl`/`isPrivateHost` the server validates with, so the two can never disagree and nobody waits out a timeout to be told something we knew at the first keystroke. Every string that came from a house (a label, an entity id, an area name) is rendered as `textContent`, because those are attacker-influenceable and there is a physical actuator on the other end. Docs: [docs/smart-home.md](docs/smart-home.md). Tests: [tests/e2e/home-connect.spec.js](tests/e2e/home-connect.spec.js) |

Resolver [api/_lib/home/entitlements.js](api/_lib/home/entitlements.js), counters [api/_lib/home/usage.js](api/_lib/home/usage.js), endpoint [api/home/plan.js](api/home/plan.js). Enforced at [api/home/index.js](api/home/index.js) (acquisition), [api/home/[id]/stream.js](api/home/[id]/stream.js) (streams), [api/home/[id]/members.js](api/home/[id]/members.js) (seats), [api/home/[id]/call.js](api/home/[id]/call.js) (paused homes), [api/_lib/home/privacy.js](api/_lib/home/privacy.js) (retention ceiling). Cost measurement [scripts/measure-home-entitlement-cost.mjs](scripts/measure-home-entitlement-cost.mjs). Migration `api/_lib/migrations/20260903160000_home_plan_overrides.sql` | Live | What a plan covers for connected houses, and the two things no plan may ever refuse. **A limit never blocks a safety action:** over quota, downgraded or paused, locking a door, closing a garage or valve and arming an alarm always execute, checked before any counter is read and classified from the domain and service alone so the exemption never depends on a healthy socket. **Nothing about the gate is sold:** confirmations, the action log's integrity and every role safety property are on every tier and there is deliberately no dimension for any of them. Seven metered dimensions (homes, seats, streams, voice minutes, agent turns, log retention, relay connections), each carrying the measured cost that justifies it. **No fourth entitlement system:** limits resolve from `account-tier.js` badges merged by MAX (so holding $THREE never costs a Pro their Pro limits) and are scaled by the `three-tier.js` holder multiplier, never by thresholds of our own. **One counter:** an agent turn is the same priced `usage_events` chat row an invoice reads, tagged with `home_id`, not a second tally that could disagree. **A downgrade pauses, it never disconnects:** excess homes get `deactivated_at` with a written reason, the credential and the action log survive, `revoked_at` stays null, and the user swaps which houses are live. Enterprise limits are a `home_plan_overrides` row with the reason on it, not a tier. Every number is env-overridable (`HOME_LIMIT_<TIER>_<DIMENSION>`), so applying an owner-approved price is a config change. Docs: [docs/home-plans.md](docs/home-plans.md). Tests: [tests/home-entitlements.test.js](tests/home-entitlements.test.js) |
| Smart home: the live 3D home (`/smart-home/:id`) | Page [pages/home-scene.html](pages/home-scene.html) `-> /smart-home/:id` and `-> /home/:id`, styles [public/home-scene.css](public/home-scene.css). Pure layout [src/home/scene-model.js](src/home/scene-model.js), WebGL renderer [src/home/scene-render.js](src/home/scene-render.js), the 2D house [src/home/scene-fallback.js](src/home/scene-fallback.js), page controller [src/home/scene.js](src/home/scene.js), browser half of the API contract [src/home/api.js](src/home/api.js). Consumes `GET /api/home/:id`, the SSE stream [api/home/[id]/stream.js](api/home/%5Bid%5D/stream.js), [api/home/[id]/call.js](api/home/%5Bid%5D/call.js) and `/api/home/:id/grants` | Live | A live 3D model of the user's house, driven entirely by their own Home Assistant, with the agent's body standing in it. **Home Assistant stores no geometry**, so everything spatial is derived from its four flat registries by a PURE module with no Three.js in it: floors stack by level, rooms pack per floor in name order, and a saved floorplan overrides the packing through one `layout` option. That split is why the layout of a real recorded house is asserted in a unit test without a GPU. **A room is lit the way the room is lit**: colour and intensity from the lights that are actually on, genuinely dark when they are all off, and the outline around its footprint goes amber the moment anything in it is unlocked or open. **A room with sixty entities is not sixty objects**: objects are ranked (lights, locks, covers, climate, alarms, media, fans, cameras, the rest), capped per surface, and the remainder summarized; numeric sensors become readouts, never objects. A device Home Assistant cannot reach is drawn as a ghost with a marker, never omitted. **The house never empties**: a dropped connection greys the scene, keeps every room, and says how old it is. Stale (the platform is retrying) and disconnected (nobody is) are different pills, and only the second offers a button. **The 2D view is not a consolation prize**: same model, real rows and real buttons, reads and operates the whole house, and it is where a browser with no WebGL or a device measured below 18 fps is routed automatically, with the page saying so. A guarded action answers 409 and the question is rendered on the object it would move, with an optional standing allowance for that one entity. Measured: 86 ms median from a real Home Assistant service call to the SSE frame, 10 to 23 ms from that frame to the painted one, 0.1 to 0.3 ms of per-frame work, 108 draw calls, and a flat heap across ten minutes of live updates. Docs: [docs/home-scene.md](docs/home-scene.md). Tests: [tests/home-scene-model.test.js](tests/home-scene-model.test.js), [tests/e2e/home-scene.spec.js](tests/e2e/home-scene.spec.js) |
| three.ws Home privacy, retention, export and deletion | Inventory + purge + export + deletion [api/_lib/home/privacy.js](api/_lib/home/privacy.js), disclosure copy [api/_lib/home/disclosure.js](api/_lib/home/disclosure.js), endpoint [api/home/privacy.js](api/home/privacy.js) `-> /api/home/privacy`, schema [api/_lib/migrations/20260903180000_home_privacy_retention.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260903180000_home_privacy_retention.sql), purge section E of [api/cron/db-retention.js](api/cron/db-retention.js) | Live | The single place that knows everything the Home lane stores. `INVENTORY` is not documentation of the code, it IS the code: the endpoint renders it, the export walks it, and [tests/home-privacy.test.js](tests/home-privacy.test.js) re-derives the set of `home_*` tables from the migration files and fails the build if one of them has no row, which is what stops an undocumented data class from ever landing. The load-bearing promise is that entity names and states are never persisted (the room graph lives in the bridge runtime's memory and dies with the instance); a second test fails any migration that creates a table shaped like a state history. The action log's window is per home and the owner's, 90 days by default, adjustable down to a day and up to ten years with a written reason the schema also enforces, applied immediately on change and swept by the platform's existing retention cron rather than a cron of its own. Deletion goes further than the cascades: memberships and grants on other people's homes, the departed user's id inside another household's action log, invitations addressed to their email, and the lane's rows in the 365-day `audit_log`. Docs: [docs/home-privacy.md](docs/home-privacy.md). Tests: [tests/home-privacy.test.js](tests/home-privacy.test.js) |
| Avatar thumbnail coverage | [api/_lib/avatar-thumbs.js](api/_lib/avatar-thumbs.js) + crons [api/cron/avatar-thumbnail-backfill.js](api/cron/avatar-thumbnail-backfill.js) (absent thumbnails, `*/5`) and [api/cron/avatar-thumbnail-render.js](api/cron/avatar-thumbnail-render.js) (stale marketplace thumbnails, `*/10`) | Live | The single owner of `avatars.thumbnail_key`. Enforces the invariant that a thumbnail key is persisted **only after** its R2 object is confirmed to exist — a key pointing at a missing object makes R2 return 404 `text/plain`, which Chrome blocks as `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ORB` inside an `<img>`. Fills coverage cheapest-first: zero-copy adoption of `forge_creations.preview_key`, then a headless-chromium GLB→PNG render to `thumb/<avatarId>.png` ([api/_lib/render-glb.js](api/_lib/render-glb.js)). Bounded retries via the `avatar_thumbnail_backfill` claim ledger ([migration](api/_lib/migrations/20260709120000_avatar_thumbnail_backfill.sql)), with `isBrowserInfrastructureError()` separating an OOM-killed browser from a genuinely broken model so a crash never retires healthy avatars. Bulk runner: [scripts/backfill-avatar-thumbnails.mjs](scripts/backfill-avatar-thumbnails.mjs) (`--status`, `--adopt-only`, `--loop`, `--reset-infra`). Docs: [docs/avatar-thumbnails.md](docs/avatar-thumbnails.md). Tests: [tests/avatar-thumbs.test.js](tests/avatar-thumbs.test.js), [tests/home-thumbnail-orb.test.js](tests/home-thumbnail-orb.test.js) |
| Forge model categorization | Classifier [api/_lib/forge-classify.js](api/_lib/forge-classify.js), wired at generation in [api/_lib/forge-store.js](api/_lib/forge-store.js) (`createCreation`), backfill [scripts/backfill-forge-categories.mjs](scripts/backfill-forge-categories.mjs), coloured badges in [src/forge-showcase.js](src/forge-showcase.js) + `/forge-studio` twin | Live | Assigns every forge model a `model_category` (avatar/accessory/item/scene/creature/vehicle/other) from its prompt with a deterministic, dependency-free keyword classifier — most-specific-first so "a knight riding a horse" is an avatar, not a creature. Nothing set the category before, so ~99.8% of the corpus was `other` and the category badges, filters, and Forge-Off category dimension were dead; the classifier now runs inline on every generation (all lanes) and reclassifies ~85% of the 9.8k-row back catalogue (dry-run verified). Coloured category pills render across the showcase/gallery/community feed. Tests: [tests/forge-classify.test.js](tests/forge-classify.test.js). |
| Forge thumbnail coverage | [api/_lib/forge-thumbs.js](api/_lib/forge-thumbs.js) + cron [api/cron/forge-thumbnail-backfill.js](api/cron/forge-thumbnail-backfill.js) (`*/5`) | Live | Gives every finished forge model a real picture. The dominant free NVIDIA/TRELLIS lane is geometry-first and paints no reference image, so ~83% of finished `forge_creations` land with `preview_image_url = NULL` and render as gradient placeholders on every browse surface (showcase, gallery, `/community`, `/search`, creator portfolios). This cron claims the newest previewless models, renders each GLB to a 768² PNG via the shared headless-chromium renderer ([api/_lib/render-glb.js](api/_lib/render-glb.js)), uploads it, and fills the preview slot — object confirmed in R2 **before** the key is committed (same ORB-safety invariant as avatars), and **fill-only** so a maker's captured poster always wins. Self-provisioning claim ledger (`forge_thumbnail_backfill`, created at runtime), bounded attempts, lease-based reclaim, browser-death batch abort with attempt refund. `?dry_run=1` reports coverage. Tests: [tests/forge-thumbs.test.js](tests/forge-thumbs.test.js). |
| Catalog quality gate (what earns a slot in the public catalog) | [api/_lib/seed-quality.js](api/_lib/seed-quality.js), consumed by [api/cron/forge-seed-cron.js](api/cron/forge-seed-cron.js) | Live · mesh stage always on, vision stage behind `SEED_CRON_VISION` | The bar every platform-seeded model clears before it reaches [/characters](public/characters.html) or [/objects](pages/objects.html). Nobody asked for seeded content, so it has to earn its slot: a user who generates a coarse mesh chose to, a visitor browsing the catalog did not. Two stages, cheapest first. **Mesh sanity** is free and deterministic (reuses [api/_lib/glb-quality.js](api/_lib/glb-quality.js), the interactive forge's own scorer, plus catalog bounds: vertex floor/ceiling, byte floor/ceiling, must-carry-a-texture, non-collapsed bbox, and a mesh-count rule that applies to `avatar` only because a prop may legitimately be a pile of parts): a degenerate blob is rejected here for the price of a GLB fetch and never costs a model call. **Vision judge** renders one view and asks two questions: realism via `judgeOnce` imported (never forked) from [api/_lib/quality-bench.js](api/_lib/quality-bench.js) so the gate and the regression bench can never disagree about what a score means, plus a catalog-specific rig-readiness check (one complete humanoid with separated limbs, or a bust / blob / crowd?). **Fails closed on mesh, fails soft on vision:** an unreachable judge publishes on the mesh verdict marked `vision_unavailable`, because recording a Vertex outage as a quality reject would corrupt the accept-rate maths. Rejects are copied (never moved) to `forge/rejected/` with a full-verdict JSON sidecar, which is the tuning dataset. `SEED_GATE_VERSION` stamps every verdict since an accept rate is only comparable within one version. Two transports (in-process for Cloud Run, remote via `/api/render/avatar-clip` + `/api/vision` for a credential-less workstation) share one decision path. Docs: [docs/seed-quality.md](docs/seed-quality.md). Tests: [tests/seed-quality.test.js](tests/seed-quality.test.js) |

| Materialize (generation to a physical object) | Page [pages/materialize.html](pages/materialize.html) `→ /materialize` + [src/materialize.js](src/materialize.js) + [src/materialize-lib.js](src/materialize-lib.js), order tracking [pages/materialize-order.html](pages/materialize-order.html) + [src/materialize-order.js](src/materialize-order.js) `→ /materialize/orders/:id`, operator console [pages/materialize-ops.html](pages/materialize-ops.html) + [src/materialize-ops.js](src/materialize-ops.js) `→ /materialize/ops`, certificate page [pages/certificate.html](pages/certificate.html) `→ /cert/:id` + [src/certificate-page.js](src/certificate-page.js), insert card [pages/print-insert.html](pages/print-insert.html) `→ /materialize/insert/:id`. Engine [api/_lib/print/](api/_lib/print) (mesh-io, topology, bvh, voxel-sdf, analyze, repair, export-stl, export-3mf, quote, gate, rules, certificate, editions, adapters, fulfillment, ops-notify). State machine [api/_lib/print-store.js](api/_lib/print-store.js). HTTP [api/print/](api/print) (catalog, quote, prepare, upload, orders, orders/[id], orders/[id]/confirm, certs/[id], editions, ops/[action], webhook/[provider]) plus the agent lane [api/x402/print-order.js](api/x402/print-order.js). Catalog [data/print-catalog.json](data/print-catalog.json). Sweep [api/cron/print-orders-sync.js](api/cron/print-orders-sync.js) | Live | The physical lane of the forge: any generation becomes a real print, shipped. One `POST /api/print/quote` both analyzes the mesh (manifold check, shells, open edges, thinnest wall, exact volume via the Manifold WASM kernel, a 0-100 score with buyer-readable deductions) and prices it from one tunable catalog file, returning an HMAC-signed 24h quote token that carries every priced parameter so checkout cannot be replayed or edited. Prepare reconstructs the solid, fills holes, scales, optionally hollows with drain holes, and writes STL / 3MF (with per-vertex colour sampled from the source texture, which is what makes a full-colour print possible) / repaired GLB. Checkout is USDC on Solana for humans and x402 for agents, on the same pipeline: `POST /api/x402/print-order` validates the token and address BEFORE issuing the 402, so a malformed order is refused for free. Fulfillment is an adapter layer (manual operator lane plus a partner API) behind a whitelisted state machine; provider webhooks are HMAC-verified against the raw bytes and idempotent by delivery id. Every shipped print gets a Solana-attested certificate hashing the exact manufactured file. **The fabrication gate runs twice and is not advisory:** deterministically at quote time (before money moves, returning 451 with the category and the policy link) and again on the paid order through the LLM chain, with `submitOrder()` refusing anything without a recorded `allow` verdict. Docs: [docs/materialize.md](docs/materialize.md), operator runbook [docs/ops/materialize-fulfillment.md](docs/ops/materialize-fulfillment.md). Spec: [specs/PRINT_PIPELINE.md](specs/PRINT_PIPELINE.md). Runs are committed append-only JSON under `data/quality-bench/runs/`; the dashboard renders score trends by lane/tier/subject-class, a worst-5 critique gallery, and the full results table. `node scripts/quality-bench.mjs --compare=latest,previous` exits nonzero on a >1.0 mean drop — run it before/after any forge change that could affect quality. | Likeness bench (Phase 1 verification metric) | Scoring core [api/_lib/likeness-score.js](api/_lib/likeness-score.js), face-embedding runtime [api/_lib/face-embed.js](api/_lib/face-embed.js), store + distribution queries [api/_lib/likeness-store.js](api/_lib/likeness-store.js) ([schema](api/_lib/migrations/20260814000000_avatar_likeness_scores.sql)), weekly sweep [api/cron/likeness-eval.js](api/cron/likeness-eval.js) (`47 3 * * 1`), by-hand runner [scripts/likeness-eval.mjs](scripts/likeness-eval.mjs), internal board [api/likeness-bench.js](api/likeness-bench.js) + [pages/likeness-bench.html](pages/likeness-bench.html) `→ /likeness-bench` (admin or `x-ops-secret`, not in `data/pages.json`) | Live · applied 2026-08-14 (this file alone, via `apply-migrations.mjs --apply --file`, because `db:migrate` would have applied seven other agents' pending migrations with it). The header comment still reads "Draft, NOT applied" and must stay that way: an applied migration is an immutable record and editing its bytes trips the drift guard | The number the roadmap gates Phase 1 on, measured instead of asserted. The realism bench scores whether a generation looks real and never asks whose face it is; the reconstruction worker's ISE eval scores face *shape* offline against a synthetic set and stores nothing. This closes the gap: it renders a finished reconstruction head-framed at three yaws (head-on 0°, three-quarter 35°, profile 65°) with the platform's own `headshot` preset, embeds every render and every input capture with OSS face recognition (YuNet detection, MIT + SFace 128-d identity, Apache-2.0, both hash-pinned from OpenCV Zoo and run on `onnxruntime-web`'s WASM backend so there is no native install step), and files a 1-5 score against the generation record in `forge_creations`. The 1-5 mapping is anchored on SFace's own published decision boundary (cosine 0.363 = 3.0, so the roadmap's 4/5 gate is cosine 0.68, roughly twice the same-identity threshold) rather than on taste. **Refuses rather than fabricates:** `captures_unusable`, `render_unusable` and a genuinely low score are three separate statuses, and a budget-truncated sweep is withheld from the distribution. Reports `turn_falloff` (identity lost between head-on and the worst view), which is the only place a frontally-flattering texture transfer is visible. **Stores no biometrics:** no capture URLs, no crops, no embeddings, only the measurement. Docs: [docs/likeness-eval.md](docs/likeness-eval.md). Tests: [tests/likeness-score.test.js](tests/likeness-score.test.js) |
| Payment-outcome board (x402 failure visibility) | Endpoint [api/ops/payment-outcomes.js](api/ops/payment-outcomes.js), page [pages/payment-outcomes.html](pages/payment-outcomes.html) `-> /payment-outcomes` (admin session or `x-ops-secret`, `noindex`, not in `data/pages.json`), sensors [api/_lib/ops/x402-settle-health.js](api/_lib/ops/x402-settle-health.js) + [api/_lib/x402/wallet-balance-monitor.js](api/_lib/x402/wallet-balance-monitor.js) + [api/_lib/x402/sponsor-runway.js](api/_lib/x402/sponsor-runway.js) + [api/_lib/custodial-key-health.js](api/_lib/custodial-key-health.js) | Live | Every way an x402 payment can fail, in one read, before it halts the economy. The 2026-07-11 audit found the griefing classes (verify-reject floods, settle failures, sponsor-fee starvation) were only visible after the economy stopped: `recordPaymentMetric` was already emitting the events and nothing surfaced them. Four panels: inbound rates over 1h/3h/24h from the durable `x402_audit_log` (settle success, verify reject, replay reject with its own `inbound_attempts` denominator, top failure reasons by stage, replay stage split), the outbound ring settle sensor with its triage hint, every configured ring wallet against **its own** floor plus the measured sponsor runway, and stranded custody. The wallet panel is per-wallet on purpose: settle-floor starvation and dry wallets look identical from outside and have opposite fixes, and a board showing only the sponsor renders a starved payer as healthy. Panels are read independently and a failed one names itself in `degraded` (207 Multi-Status) rather than blanking the board; on the page a rate with no attempts renders `n/a` and an unmeasured custody total renders `not measured`, because a green zero on a reading that never happened is worse than no board. Docs: [docs/ops/payment-outcomes.md](docs/ops/payment-outcomes.md). |

Brand safety is two layers: a SQL denylist plus NemoGuard re-moderation; blocked rows park as `blocked` in the `sketchfab_uploads` ledger (states `pending`/`uploaded`/`live`/`failed`/`blocked`). `?dry_run=1` exercises selection before the token is wired; `SKETCHFAB_UPLOADS_PER_RUN` clamps 1-5 (default 2). | Third-party display-name safety | [api/_lib/display-name-safety.js](api/_lib/display-name-safety.js), applied at ERC-8004 metadata hydration in [api/cron/[name].js](api/cron/%5Bname%5D.js) | Live | The ERC-8004 crawler indexes 126k+ on-chain agents and renders each one's `name`/`description`/`image` from attacker-controlled metadata. Visibility used to be decided solely by the agent's own `meta.active`, so a Base-registered agent whose name was a racial slur shipped to `/marketplace`. `matchedSlurStem()` now withholds such rows (`active = false`), which removes them from every feed at once since explore/marketplace/agents/search all filter `active = true`. Deliberately narrow — **whole-word** hate slurs only, never general profanity, and hex-address aware: leet folding turns `0xdb6b887289c380…` into letter soup, so long alphanumeric runs are excluded from the spaced-out-evasion pass. A false positive silently delists a legitimate agent, so precision outranks recall. Backfill for already-indexed rows: [scripts/deactivate-slur-agents.mjs](scripts/deactivate-slur-agents.mjs) (`--dry-run`). Tests: [tests/display-name-safety.test.js](tests/display-name-safety.test.js) — pins the real agent names an earlier substring matcher wrongly flagged. |

| Open inference network: node operator client | [packages/node-operator/](packages/node-operator) (README, Dockerfile + Dockerfile.gpu, src/, tests/) | In-repo | Phase 4 roadmap: anyone runs an inference node. Self-contained client whose Solana ed25519 keypair IS its identity: it registers with the coordinator (signature over `threews-node-register:{pk}:{ts}`, no shared secret), claims jobs from `/api/nodes/jobs`, executes real local inference (`Xenova/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` on ONNX Runtime; q8 on CPU, fp32 on CUDA via `Dockerfile.gpu`), and returns an ed25519 receipt over `sha256(jobId).sha256(model).sha256(prompt).sha256(output).start.end` that the coordinator recomputes before accepting. `npm run e2e` proves register -> job -> verified signed result against the REAL `api/nodes/*` handlers booted from `server/index.mjs`. | Open Inference Protocol (OIN v0.1) | spec [specs/OPEN_INFERENCE_PROTOCOL.md](specs/OPEN_INFERENCE_PROTOCOL.md), verifier [api/_lib/oin-verify.js](api/_lib/oin-verify.js), conformance runner [scripts/oin-conformance.mjs](scripts/oin-conformance.mjs), reference node [workers/stylize/oin.py](workers/stylize/oin.py) | In-repo | Phase 4 roadmap: the vendor-neutral wire protocol that decouples inference from any single provider. A job envelope, a signed capability advertisement at `GET /.well-known/oin`, and an Ed25519-signed response bound to the job by a SHA-256 digest over its RFC 8785 canonical form, so any third party can verify a node's result without trusting the transport or the platform. The stylize worker is the reference node behind `OIN_ENABLED=true` (byte-for-byte its pre-OIN self with the flag off) and self-hosted operators with no GCS bucket sign filesystem artifacts via `OIN_RESULT_DIR`. `node scripts/oin-conformance.mjs --node <url>` drives advertisement, submit, poll, response verification, and artifact hashing end to end. Settlement rides on [specs/inference-receipts.md](specs/inference-receipts.md). Tests: [tests/oin-verify.test.js](tests/oin-verify.test.js), [workers/stylize/test_stylize.py](workers/stylize/test_stylize.py) |

World state (SQLite + uploaded assets) lives in the GCS bucket `world-three-ws-data` mounted at `/app/world`, so the container is stateless. **The world is gated:** without `ADMIN_CODE` Hyperfy's `effectiveRank` falls back to `Ranks.ADMIN` and every anonymous visitor gets server-side build rights — the fail-open default that emptied the world on 2026-06-12. | Metaplex Agent Registry deploy (Genesis-333 mint for everyone) | [pages/deploy-onchain.html](pages/deploy-onchain.html) + [src/deploy-onchain.js](src/deploy-onchain.js) `→ /deploy-onchain`, shared builders [packages/metaplex-agent-mcp/src/lib/](packages/metaplex-agent-mcp/src/lib) (published as `@three-ws/metaplex-agent-mcp`, also in the official MCP registry as `io.github.nirholas/metaplex-agent`) | Live | Mint any agent into the Metaplex Agent Registry in the exact Genesis 333 shape: data: URI metadata, verified creator, 5% royalties, immutable metadata, EIP-8004 identity with `model.uri` + `x402Support`. The page signs with the user's own wallet (Phantom/Solflare/Backpack, `signAllTransactions`) through the same-origin RPC proxy; the MCP server gives agents the identical mint with their own key (preview-first, `confirm:true` gated) plus a prepare/send lane for external wallets. Both consume one isomorphic builder library, and [tests](packages/metaplex-agent-mcp/test/genesis-shape.test.mjs) pin the output byte-for-byte to the live Genesis #332 URIs. Devnet is a free rehearsal lane end-to-end. A mainnet deploy also pays a flat 0.02 SOL fee to the $THREE buyback wallet, halved at 50k $THREE in the paying wallet and waived at 250k, disclosed in every preview and carried inside the create transaction ([packages/metaplex-agent-mcp/src/lib/three.js](packages/metaplex-agent-mcp/src/lib/three.js)); the `three_status` tool prices it against any wallet. |
| Onchain agent wallets (spending allowances, not private keys) | [packages/onchain-agent-wallets/](packages/onchain-agent-wallets) (published as `@three-ws/onchain-agent-wallets`, MCP registry `io.github.nirholas/onchain-agent-wallets`) | Live | Give an agent a Solana wallet without giving it the money. Funds sit in a vault token account the OWNER owns, derived deterministically from the owner address plus the agent id (`createWithSeed(owner, "oaw1:<agent>")`); the agent holds only a capped SPL Token delegation over it, so the token program itself refuses any spend past the ceiling and decrements it on every transfer. Guardrails on top (per-tx, rolling daily, recipient and host allowlists, expiry, pause, confirm-above threshold) are enforced before signing, and every decision including refusals lands in a local ledger. `pay_x402` probes the price unpaid, checks it against the guardrails, tops the agent up for exactly that amount, pays, and leaves it empty. Owner actions need no key on the machine: they come back as unsigned transactions for Phantom/Solflare/Ledger. [Delegation tests](packages/onchain-agent-wallets/test/token-delegation.test.mjs) run the real SPL Token program in process and assert that 61 against a remaining allowance of 60 fails on-chain. |

| Recurring payments (subscriptions + DCA) | Page [public/recurring.html](public/recurring.html) `→ /recurring`, module [src/recurring.js](src/recurring.js), lifecycle rules [api/_lib/recurring.js](api/_lib/recurring.js), APIs [api/agent-subscriptions.js](api/agent-subscriptions.js) `→ /api/agent-subscriptions` and [api/dca-strategies.js](api/dca-strategies.js) `→ /api/dca-strategies`, hourly crons `run-subscriptions` + `run-dca` in [api/cron/[name].js](api/cron/%5Bname%5D.js), schema [api/_lib/migrations/20260813191500_recurring_payment_lifecycle.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260813191500_recurring_payment_lifecycle.sql) | Live | One signed ERC-7710 permission, then money moves on a schedule: a subscription transfers a fixed USDC amount to an agent's wallet each period, a DCA strategy swaps the same amount into a token through Uniswap V3. `/recurring` is the whole lifecycle in one place (create, pause, resume, cancel, per-charge history) plus an Incoming tab scoped by agent ownership rather than by payer, so a creator sees what is being paid in. Every attempt is written to `subscription_charges` / `dca_executions`, and one shared classifier decides what a failure means: a revoked or expired permission pauses the schedule with the reason attached, a network blip releases the period claim and retries for three ticks, a timeout is never retried (the only failure that could double-charge), a diverged DCA quote skips the period for free, and a platform-side relayer outage never counts against the owner at all. Settlement needs `AGENT_RELAYER_KEY` + `RPC_URL_<chainId>` on the deployment; DCA creation needs `DCA_ALLOWED_TOKEN_OUT` + `DCA_CHAIN_ID`. Docs: [docs/recurring-payments.md](docs/recurring-payments.md). Tests: [tests/recurring-charge-outcomes.test.js](tests/recurring-charge-outcomes.test.js), [tests/api/recurring-subscription-cron.test.js](tests/api/recurring-subscription-cron.test.js) |
| Solana program invariant tests | [contracts/program-tests/](contracts/program-tests) | In-repo | LiteSVM suite running the real compiled SBF bytecode of `skill_license`, `agent_invocation`, and `knock_escrow` against invariants `SL-*`, `AI-*`, and `KE-*`, with the real SPL Token and ATA programs loaded. 42 tests green |
| Reputation Staking Market | spec [specs/REPUTATION_STAKING_MARKET.md](specs/REPUTATION_STAKING_MARKET.md), engine [src/shared/reputation-staking.js](src/shared/reputation-staking.js), server [api/_lib/reputation-market.js](api/_lib/reputation-market.js) + [api/reputation/market*.js](api/reputation), surface [public/reputation/market/](public/reputation/market) `→ /reputation/market` | In-repo · devnet open, mainnet owner-gated | Phase 3 roadmap: stake conviction on an agent and earn from its attested action history. A stake is a staker-signed Solana transfer to the market escrow carrying an `rsm.v1` SPL-Memo, so the chain (not the database) defines a position; the server only verifies it by reading the transaction back. Yield comes from a fixed per-epoch pool split by `principal × time × agentWeight`, where the weight is derived purely from the agent's signed `threews.*` attestations (work concave in `log2`, faults charged double), so conviction never pays itself. Principal is never slashed and settlement clamps earnings to the escrow surplus. Extends the SPL-Memo attestation layer in [docs/solana-reputation.md](docs/solana-reputation.md). Storage: [reputation_staking_market migration](api/_lib/migrations/20260811140000_reputation_staking_market.sql). Walkthrough: [docs/reputation-staking-market.md](docs/reputation-staking-market.md). Devnet proof: `node scripts/reputation-market-proof.mjs`. Tests: [tests/reputation-staking.test.js](tests/reputation-staking.test.js) |
