| Generative 3D drops (supply-capped collections) | engine [api/_lib/drops.js](api/_lib/drops.js), store [api/_lib/drop-store.js](api/_lib/drop-store.js), routes [api/drops/[action].js](api/drops/%5Baction%5D.js) `→ /api/drops/{list,get,items,verify,create,publish,reveal}`, index [pages/drops.html](pages/drops.html) + [src/drops.js](src/drops.js) `→ /drops`, collection [pages/drop-collection.html](pages/drop-collection.html) + [src/drop-collection.js](src/drop-collection.js) `→ /drops/:slug`, styles [src/drops.css](src/drops.css) | In-repo | A drop stores its SPEC, not its art: every item's traits derive from (seed, index, layers) alone, so the whole supply is a pure function and a holder can recompute it. Creator gives a base style plus weighted trait layers; the roll is instant and free at any supply up to 10,000 because no art is generated at create time. Rarity is scored from the ACTUAL rolled distribution (sum of supply/occurrences per trait), ranked, and bucketed into the platform's common/rare/epic/legendary tiers. A published sha256 over the canonical spec is the commitment; the seed stays withheld until publish, which freezes the spec one-way. Art is forged per item on reveal through [api/forge.js](api/forge.js) (claim-then-poll, conditional UPDATE so a double click cannot start two jobs, stale claims self-release after 15 min), which is what keeps a 10k collection economically possible. Reveals inherit the universal animation retarget and the GLB/VRM/USDZ export path. Distinct from `/drop/:id` (the unrelated sealed wallet gift claim page); the routes are ordered so they cannot shadow each other. Schema: [drops + drop_items](api/_lib/migrations/20260901120000_generative_drops.sql). Docs: [docs/drops.md](docs/drops.md). Tests: [tests/drops-engine.test.js](tests/drops-engine.test.js) |

Anti-abuse: short-lived signed access tokens (auto re-verify on expiry) + per-IP/per-wallet rate limits. Docs: [docs/token-gated-3d-embeds.md](docs/token-gated-3d-embeds.md). Tests: [tests/embed-gate.test.js](tests/embed-gate.test.js) |
| Agent token plans (the coin an agent will become) | Rules + storage [api/_lib/agent-token-plan.js](api/_lib/agent-token-plan.js) (migration [api/_lib/migrations/20260811130000_agent_token_plans.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260811130000_agent_token_plans.sql)), API actions `plan` + `plan-dry-run` in [api/agents/tokens/[action].js](api/agents/tokens/%5Baction%5D.js) `→ /api/agents/tokens/{plan,plan-dry-run}`, profile panel [src/agent-token-plan.js](src/agent-token-plan.js) on `/agents/:id` | In-repo · Live | The launch configuration bound to the agent record: one saved, editable plan per (agent, network) carrying the coin's name, ticker, story, artwork, pairing, buyback share and dev buy, with a derived readiness verdict and SOL cost estimate. Saving costs nothing and mints nothing. `plan-dry-run` is the free proof lane: it builds the SAME pump.fun create instructions the real launch builds from the SAME plan, compiles them against a real blockhash and simulates them on the cluster (devnet by default), never signing and never broadcasting, and separates `funding_required` from a genuinely broken plan. Both launch paths (`/api/agents/tokens/launch-confirm` from the owner's wallet, `/api/pump/launch-agent` from the agent's custodial wallet) call `markPlanLaunched` and write a `pump_agent_mints` row, which is also what finally puts user-wallet launches into `/launches` and the profile's launch history. Docs: [docs/agent-tokens.md](docs/agent-tokens.md). | Agent token market data (what the coin looks like on the profile) | Cluster read [api/_lib/pump-curve-view.js](api/_lib/pump-curve-view.js) `→ GET /api/pump/curve` + `GET /api/v1/pump/curve`, view model [src/widgets/bonding-curve.js](src/widgets/bonding-curve.js) (`computeView`), normalization + widget [src/pump/coin-status-card.js](src/pump/coin-status-card.js) (`mapCoin` / `mapCurve` / `mountCoinStatus`), profile wiring [src/agent-detail.js](src/agent-detail.js) (token chip + launch history) | In-repo · Live | The live market half of an agent token. Two sources, one normalized shape: pump.fun's indexer (`/api/pump/coin`, mainnet, carries logo + 24h volume) and the bonding curve read straight off the cluster (`/api/pump/curve`), which is the ONLY source on devnet and the fallback whenever the indexer has not indexed a fresh mint or cannot be reached. A devnet coin renders in SOL and is badged `DEVNET` (devnet SOL has no dollar value) and links to the explorer instead of a pump.fun page that does not exist for that cluster, so the free rehearsal path renders exactly like a real launch. The curve gate accepts a pump.fun-ground `pump` suffix, a three.ws `3ws` mark, any plausible devnet address, and otherwise asks `pump_agent_mints` whether the mint is one of ours: a coin launched from a user's own wallet carries no mark and used to be refused with a cached `not_a_pump_mint` 404. Market cap is derived as price × supply because the SDK's `price.marketCap` goes negative on live curves. Free proof: [scripts/agent-token-market-proof.mjs](scripts/agent-token-market-proof.mjs) (reads only, signs nothing). Docs: [docs/agent-tokens.md](docs/agent-tokens.md). Tests: [tests/pump-curve-mint-gate.test.js](tests/pump-curve-mint-gate.test.js), [tests/agent-detail-launch-history.test.js](tests/agent-detail-launch-history.test.js), [src/pump/coin-status-card.curve.dom.test.js](src/pump/coin-status-card.curve.dom.test.js) |

| Native launchpad (three.ws's own bonding curve) | Curve economics [api/_lib/native-launch/config.js](api/_lib/native-launch/config.js), curve wrapper [api/_lib/native-launch/dbc.js](api/_lib/native-launch/dbc.js), dispatcher [api/native-launch/[action].js](api/native-launch/%5Baction%5D.js) `→ /api/native-launch/{config,quote,pool,launch-prep,launch-confirm,launches}`, lane toggle in [public/studio/launch-panel.js](public/studio/launch-panel.js) | In-repo (devnet-verified; mainnet config not yet deployed) | The second launch lane beside pump.fun: a coin launch on a bonding curve three.ws configures and owns, so the platform earns a share of every trade instead of only creator fees. Runs on Meteora's audited Dynamic Bonding Curve program with three.ws as the on-chain partner — 1B supply, SOL-quoted, immutable metadata, 1% trading fee split 50/50 creator/platform, graduating at ~85 SOL raised into a DAMM v2 pool with **100% of LP permanently locked** (un-ruggable, both sides keep earning). Same custody model as the pump lane (server builds an unsigned tx, the launcher's wallet + mint keypair sign client-side, confirm verifies on-chain that the curve program actually ran before recording). The lane hides itself entirely until a partner config is pinned for that network (`NATIVE_LAUNCH_CONFIG_KEY[_DEVNET]`), so launches fall back to pump.fun rather than half-working. Not to be confused with `/api/launchpad/*` (Launchpad Studio, the page builder). Storage: [native_launches](api/_lib/migrations/20260726100000_native_launchpad.sql). Setup: [scripts/native-launchpad-create-config.mjs](scripts/native-launchpad-create-config.mjs). E2E: [scripts/native-launchpad-e2e-devnet.mjs](scripts/native-launchpad-e2e-devnet.mjs). | pump.fun first-claims Telegram channel (@pumpfunclaims) | Delivery lane [api/_lib/pump-claims-push.js](api/_lib/pump-claims-push.js), cron [api/cron/pump-claims-push.js](api/cron/pump-claims-push.js) (every 5 min), shared scanner [api/_lib/pump-claims.js](api/_lib/pump-claims.js) (also backs `GET /api/pump/first-claims` and the `pumpfun_first_claims` MCP tool) | In-repo · lane wired, channel dark pending `PUMPFUN_BOT_URL` + `TELEGRAM_PUMP_CLAIMS_CHAT_ID` | A live feed of first-time pump.fun creator fee claims, one message per claim: the first time a creator ever pulls accrued rewards out of the fee vault is an irreversible on-chain signal that they are engaged with their coin. Mirrors the commit-feed lane's safety properties (DB lock, seed-without-posting on first run, per-message state writes, oldest-first drain, 3h replay cutoff) but dedupes by transaction signature rather than timestamp, because claims routinely share a second and a timestamp cursor drops the second one. No fallback to the holder changelog channel on purpose. The RPC fallback inside the scanner cannot serve this feed (the pump program clears a 200-signature page in under one second, so a scan covers no useful window); an indexer is required. Docs: [docs/pump-claims-channel.md](docs/pump-claims-channel.md). Tests: [tests/pump-claims-push.test.js](tests/pump-claims-push.test.js) |
| Ship log (release notes joined to the commits that shipped them) | Package [packages/shipfeed/](packages/shipfeed/README.md), endpoint [api/ship/feed.js](api/ship/feed.js) `→ GET /api/ship/feed`, page [public/ship/index.html](public/ship/index.html) `→ /ship`, provenance for the announcement lane [api/_lib/ship-provenance.js](api/_lib/ship-provenance.js) | Live | The join between the two records of the same work this repo keeps: the changelog (readable, incomplete) and the commit log (complete, unreadable). `@three-ws/shipfeed` parses conventional commits, scores each one by audience (holder / developer / internal) and signal, and links release notes to the commits that produced them with no metadata in the workflow: a `Changelog:` trailer wins where one exists, otherwise a time window plus IDF-weighted term overlap plus tag agreement plus proximity, and every link publishes its confidence and its reasons (`?explain=<sha>` returns one commit's full reasoning). Unclaimed commits are grouped into bursts and shown as "unannounced", which is the half no changelog has ever surfaced. The same package now drives both Telegram lanes: release announcements carry a "shipped in N commits" compare link, and the raw commit feed reads its headline from the parser (`feat(resilience):` reads as "Feature · resilience") and skips merges, `chore(deps):` bumps and lockfile-only changes. Zero dependencies, works on any repository, CLI included (`npx @three-ws/shipfeed`). Docs: [docs/shipfeed.md](docs/shipfeed.md). Tests: [packages/shipfeed/tests/](packages/shipfeed/tests), [tests/ship-feed-endpoint.test.js](tests/ship-feed-endpoint.test.js), [tests/ship-provenance.test.js](tests/ship-provenance.test.js) |

| Escrowed knocks (pay-per-reply) | [contracts/knock-escrow/](contracts/knock-escrow) | In-repo | Anchor program making a knock's payment conditional instead of final: `knock` parks the price in a vault owned by the knock's own PDA, and only three things can happen to it. `answer` pays the owner minus a fee capped at 10% in the program itself, `refuse` refunds the sender in full and takes no fee, and `reclaim` refunds the sender once the reply window closes and can be called by anyone. Each `KnockRecord` snapshots the fee that was in force when it was made, so an authority cannot reprice money already in escrow, and it stores only the SHA-256 of the message and the reply, never their text. The trust-me version of this settles straight to the owner at [api/knock/](api/knock) (row above); this is the version that works between strangers. Both sides are reachable from a browser: senders escrow and crank their own refund from the door page, owners open their on-chain door, answer for payment or refuse for a refund from [/knock](pages/knock.html), all signed by their own wallet ([src/knock/escrow-checkout.js](src/knock/escrow-checkout.js)). Docs: [contracts/knock-escrow/README.md](contracts/knock-escrow/README.md), [docs/knock.md](docs/knock.md). Tests: [tests/knock-escrow-program.test.js](tests/knock-escrow-program.test.js), [tests/knock-escrow-pda.test.js](tests/knock-escrow-pda.test.js), [tests/api-knock-escrowed.test.js](tests/api-knock-escrowed.test.js), [tests/api-knock-escrow-sync.test.js](tests/api-knock-escrow-sync.test.js) |

| Money flow map | [pages/flow.html](pages/flow.html) `→ /flow`, pure graph math + force layout [src/flow-map-core.js](src/flow-map-core.js), canvas/DOM layer [src/flow-map.js](src/flow-map.js), styles [src/flow-map.css](src/flow-map.css), backend view [api/pulse.js](api/pulse.js) `→ GET /api/pulse?view=graph` | Live | The TOPOLOGY of the agent economy, which every other economy surface here misses because a leaderboard cannot show shape. A force-directed canvas map of who paid whom, for which skill, and how much, built from two already-public on-chain sources: agent-to-agent x402 skill payments (`meta.to` payee wallet + `meta.service`) and tips (`meta.from` payer wallet). Every edge is a settled transfer with an explorer-verifiable signature; no inferred or estimated edges exist. Nodes are classified `source` / `hub` / `relay` / `sink` / `quiet` from flow asymmetry, which makes capital dispersion (value entering agent wallets and never circulating out) visible at a glance and puts a number on it as "stuck capital". Counterparty privacy matches the live feed: a wallet is NAMED only when it resolves to a public, non-opted-out agent, otherwise it stays an address-only dashed node. Keyboard and screen-reader parity is structural, not a bolt-on: the table below the canvas renders from the same filtered data and selecting a row selects the node. Docs: [docs/money-flow-map.md](docs/money-flow-map.md). Tests: [tests/flow-map-core.test.js](tests/flow-map-core.test.js) |

| Earned autonomy (sniper fleet) | tier engine [api/_lib/sniper-autonomy.js](api/_lib/sniper-autonomy.js), tier-scaled optimizer [api/_lib/sniper-optimizer.js](api/_lib/sniper-optimizer.js) + cron [api/cron/sniper-optimize.js](api/cron/sniper-optimize.js), tier-weighted portfolio layer [scripts/sniper-evolve.mjs](scripts/sniper-evolve.mjs) + cron [api/cron/sniper-evolve.js](api/cron/sniper-evolve.js), judge knowledge pack [workers/agent-sniper/judge-knowledge.js](workers/agent-sniper/judge-knowledge.js) → [workers/agent-sniper/llm-judge.js](workers/agent-sniper/llm-judge.js), board [api/sniper/experiments.js](api/sniper/experiments.js) + [src/sniper-experiments.js](src/sniper-experiments.js) `→ /sniper/experiments` | Live | A trading arm's own realized record decides how much rope it gets, recomputed every optimizer run: `probation → standard → trusted → autonomous`. The tier scales how far and how fast the optimizer may move a knob, which fields it may touch at all (trusted unlocks the entry universe, the LLM confidence bar, and the take-initials ladder), how much of the fixed fleet budget the evolution loop concentrates on the arm, and how much real evidence the LLM judge is handed before it rules (base rate, learned signal weights, the arm's own record, the realized win-rate table, the model's own calibration). Decided on realized net P&L and average edge, never win rate, so a 36%-hit-rate arm that actually makes money earns room while a 60%-hit-rate arm that loses money does not. Symmetric: an arm that stops paying loses its tier on the next pass. No tier can reach a safety rail (firewall, Mayhem exclusion, price impact, concurrency, loss cap, fleet ceiling) and a bounded stop-loss survives every tier. Tests: [tests/sniper-autonomy.test.js](tests/sniper-autonomy.test.js), [tests/sniper-optimizer.test.js](tests/sniper-optimizer.test.js), [tests/sniper-judge-knowledge.test.js](tests/sniper-judge-knowledge.test.js). Image and page render from ONE pure model, so the picture that unfurls and the page it lands on can never disagree about a number. Honest by construction: a loss renders exactly like a win, a simulate-mode fill is stamped PAPER and never counted as live, and a moon-bag exit says the position was not fully sold. | Never sell 100% of a winner (moon-bag policy) | rule [workers/agent-sniper/exit-logic.js](workers/agent-sniper/exit-logic.js) (`moonbagExitFraction`, `moonbagAlways`), retained-bag booking [workers/agent-sniper/executor.js](workers/agent-sniper/executor.js), position fields [api/_lib/migrations/20260725100000_sniper_moonbag_always.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260725100000_sniper_moonbag_always.sql), board [api/sniper/experiments.js](api/sniper/experiments.js) + [src/sniper-experiments.js](src/sniper-experiments.js) `→ /sniper/experiments` | Live | Fleet-wide owner rule: a terminal exit that is IN PROFIT sells down to the moon-bag floor (default 15%) instead of to zero, and the retained tokens ride indefinitely. Once the cost basis is back the remainder is free, so a bag that goes to zero costs nothing and a bag that runs is the whole upside; selling the last slice for a rounding error gives that up. Applies with or without the proactive 2x ladder. A loss exit with the stake still at risk is still a FULL exit and the hard stop-loss is never overridden. The position still books `closed`, so realized P&L and the `max_concurrent_positions` slot are unaffected and a held bag can never stop an arm trading; only the tokens stay behind (`moonbag_base_amount`). Per-strategy opt-out `moonbag_always = false`. Tests: [tests/sniper-laddered-exit.test.js](tests/sniper-laddered-exit.test.js), [tests/trading-experiment-e2e.test.js](tests/trading-experiment-e2e.test.js). | Oracle Lab (the conviction model, published) | [pages/oracle-lab.html](pages/oracle-lab.html) + [src/oracle-lab.js](src/oracle-lab.js) `→ /oracle-lab`, model API [api/oracle/model.js](api/oracle/model.js) `→ /api/oracle/model`, SDK [packages/oracle-model/](packages/oracle-model) `→ @three-ws/oracle-model` | Live | The glass box over the Oracle. Publishes the scoring model itself, not a summary of it: every bucket weight with the number of labeled launches behind it and what happened to them, the held-out reliability curve for each tier claim, the version history of every refit INCLUDING the candidates the promotion gate refused and why, and a scorer that runs the real weights in the visitor's browser. The model is a lookup table (~32KB), so `@three-ws/oracle-model` downloads it once and reproduces any score offline with no key, no rate limit and no telemetry; `verify()` re-measures our published AUC/Brier/reliability against a caller's own labeled outcomes, and `diff()` shows every weight that moved between two versions. `?view=registry` and `?view=diff&from=&to=` serve the same data server-side. Docs: [docs/oracle-model.md](docs/oracle-model.md) `→ /docs/oracle-model`. Tests: [packages/oracle-model/test/](packages/oracle-model/test) (23), [tests/oracle/fit.test.js](tests/oracle/fit.test.js) (43) |
| Oracle conviction model (three heads, learned on a clock) | Fitter [api/_lib/oracle/fit.js](api/_lib/oracle/fit.js), scorer [api/_lib/oracle/conviction.js](api/_lib/oracle/conviction.js), DB-backed weights [api/_lib/oracle/model-store.js](api/_lib/oracle/model-store.js), learning loop [api/cron/oracle-refit.js](api/cron/oracle-refit.js) (every 6h), CLI [scripts/oracle-fit.mjs](scripts/oracle-fit.mjs) | Live, v3 | v2 ranked "spikes 3x or graduates", so a coin that ran 3x and went to zero counted as a hit: Prime-tier coins hit that target 58.2% of the time and rugged 53.0% of the time. v3 fits three heads over one design matrix (`win` = ran and the holder kept it, `rug` = holder down more than half, `moon` = ran at all) and anchors the public score on `win`, publishing `giveBackRisk = 1 - P(win)/P(moon)` beside it: given it runs, how often a coin like this hands the run straight back. Weights moved out of the container image into `oracle_model_versions`, so the refit cron can promote a better model between deploys; it was frozen at its 2026-08-09 fit for nineteen days because nothing but a human could refit it. Every candidate faces a promotion gate (complete fit, absolute AUC floor, every tier band still earning its claim, no feature-set collapse, a material gain over the incumbent, no regression on the other heads) and is stored either way with the reason. Thin buckets are shrunk toward zero by `n/(n+200)` so 34 samples cannot reverse a verdict. Docs: [docs/oracle-model.md](docs/oracle-model.md) |
| Price-independent outcome labels | [workers/agent-sniper/intel/learn.js](workers/agent-sniper/intel/learn.js) `deriveOutcome`, migrations [20260828170000](api/_lib/migrations/20260828170000_oracle_price_independent_labels.sql) + [20260828190000](api/_lib/migrations/20260828190000_oracle_recompute_rug_flag.sql) | Live | The rug flag was a readout of the SOL price. It was decided by "market cap under $3,000", and an empty pump.fun bonding curve is worth a fixed `30 * 1e9 / 1073000191 = 27.958993 SOL`, so the test amounted to asking whether SOL was above about $107.30 that day: of 206,428 coins labeled rugged, 206,419 were under $3,000; of 25,180 labeled survivors, the cheapest was exactly $3,000. Same dead curves, sorted by a price feed. Labels now use `retained = last_mc/ath_mc` and `hold_multiple = ath_multiple * retained`, both taken from the same reading so the price cancels; the real rug rate is 11.4%, not 91%, and it holds within two points from 60 minutes to three days. `label_version` marks which rule judged a row and the fitter trains on version 2 only. The training-set upsert also stopped being `do nothing`, which had made a written label permanently uncorrectable. |
| Coin Intelligence (the read the sniper trades on) | [pages/coin-intel.html](pages/coin-intel.html) `→ /coin-intel` (self-contained page: markup, styles and module script in one file), read API [api/pump/intel.js](api/pump/intel.js) `→ /api/pump/intel`, serverless firehose [api/cron/coin-intel-observe.js](api/cron/coin-intel-observe.js), shared finalize path [workers/agent-sniper/intel/finalize.js](workers/agent-sniper/intel/finalize.js) | Live | The public dashboard over the Coin Intelligence engine: every pump.fun launch observed for its first ~90 seconds, classified, scored 0..100, and labeled by outcome after the fact. Four views off one endpoint (`view=feed` live radar with category/verdict/quality/search filters, `view=leaderboard` best of 24h plus confirmed graduated/pumped winners, `view=traders` cross-coin wallet reputation with real win-rates, `view=learning` the per-signal weights the model learned). A coin's card opens a drawer with its launch signals, trade footprint, risk flags, a funder-cluster bubble map, the classified order book, and Oracle conviction fetched in parallel from [api/oracle/coin.js](api/oracle/coin.js). Reads `pump_coin_intel` / `pump_coin_wallets` / `pump_coin_outcomes` / `pump_intel_weights`, all written by the engine; the endpoint never writes and degrades to a designed "engine warming up" state (200 with `degraded:true`) when those tables are absent. `traders` and `learning` are whole-history aggregates and are edge-cached for minutes; the radar stays on a 5s cache and auto-refreshes every 15s. Watchlist stars share the `ld_watchlist` key with `/oracle`, `/launches` and `/watchlist`. |
| Agent voice cloning (your voice, on your agent) | Capture + BYOK panel [src/voice/voice-setup.js](src/voice/voice-setup.js) (`VoiceSetup`), mounted as step 5 of the create wizard ([src/create-agent.js](src/create-agent.js) `→ /create-agent`), in the agent editor, and in the talk-mode shell [src/voice/voice-clone-modal.js](src/voice/voice-clone-modal.js); server [api/agents/_id/voice.js](api/agents/_id/voice.js) `→ GET/PUT/DELETE /api/agents/:id/voice` + `POST /api/agents/:id/voice/clone`; credential resolver [api/_lib/agent-voice-key.js](api/_lib/agent-voice-key.js); playback config mapper `agentVoiceConfig` in [src/runtime/speech.js](src/runtime/speech.js), consumed by the hosted embed [pages/agent-embed.html](pages/agent-embed.html) `→ /agent/:id/embed` and the SDK runtime [src/runtime/index.js](src/runtime/index.js) | In-repo · BYOK live, platform lane awaits a key | Record or upload 30+ seconds and the agent speaks in that voice everywhere on three.ws. Three credential lanes, and they are not interchangeable: a clone only exists inside the ElevenLabs account that made it, so the winning lane is recorded on `agent_identities.voice_key_source` and replayed on every synthesis. **BYOK is the activation path today** (no platform `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` is set on the service), and the panel renders inline key entry rather than a dead "not configured" message; setting that one variable lights up the platform lane, metered to $THREE credits. `/api/tts/eleven` only spends an owner's key when the request names the agent, which is what lets a bound voice speak to signed-out visitors inside a cross-origin embed. Docs: [docs/tutorials/voice-and-lipsync.md](docs/tutorials/voice-and-lipsync.md), [src/voice/README.md](src/voice/README.md). | Alpha Co-pilot (the agent narrates its own read) | page [pages/alpha-copilot.html](pages/alpha-copilot.html) `→ /alpha-copilot`, client [src/alpha-copilot.js](src/alpha-copilot.js), styles [src/alpha-copilot.css](src/alpha-copilot.css), profile cross-link [src/shared/alpha-copilot-link.js](src/shared/alpha-copilot-link.js), server [api/agents/alpha.js](api/agents/alpha.js) `→ GET /api/agents/:id/alpha/candidates` + `POST /api/agents/:id/alpha/read`, grounding + scrub [api/\_lib/alpha-read.js](api/_lib/alpha-read.js) | Live | The intelligence and voice layer on the same trade rails the Trading Copilot uses: pick a public agent from a real gallery, pick a real live pump.fun mint off the candidate feed, and the agent reads it in character while its `<agent-3d>` avatar speaks the verdict aloud (agent TTS provider, then the free server lane, then browser speech). Every figure it says is validated against the `signals` bundle the server actually fetched (bonding-curve state, a real non-binding reference buy quote for true price impact, graduation progress, smart-money wallets, and for the owner the wallet balance and spend limits); a mismatch replaces the line and raises `hallucination_guard.line_replaced`, which the page surfaces rather than hides. The narrator never signs: acting on a read is an owner-only drawer that re-quotes through `previewAgentTrade` and executes through `executeAgentTrade`, so the spend policy, firewall, and custody audit all still apply. Verify (off-chain, free, replay-guarded via Redis `SET NX PX`) always runs before settle; without `B402_*` merchant credentials, settle fails closed with `mpp_not_configured` (503) rather than fabricating a receipt. | iOS App Store app | [ios/](ios): Capacitor shell + config [ios/capacitor.config.ts](ios/capacitor.config.ts), Xcode project [ios/native/App/](ios/native/App), web-side native bridge [ios/src/native-bridge.js](ios/src/native-bridge.js), offline shell [ios/shell/](ios/shell), listing copy [ios/publish/](ios/publish), app association handler in [api/wk.js](api/wk.js) | In-repo · project builds and the web half ships on every deploy · awaiting an Apple Developer organization account | three.ws as a native iOS app (`ws.three.app`). A Capacitor 8 shell whose WKWebView runs the live `https://three.ws`, so the app is never a version behind the site; what makes it an app is the native layer around it. `ios/src/native-bridge.js` ships with the SITE, not the .ipa (Capacitor injects its bridge into the remote page, so `window.Capacitor` exists on three.ws only inside the app): it supplies the `navigator.share` WKWebView does not have, routes off-site links to an in-app Safari sheet instead of stranding the app's only WebView, handles universal links and `threews://` wallet returns, holds the launch screen until the first real frame, and marks `html.ios-app`. The `three-ws-ios-native-bridge` plugin in [vite.config.js](vite.config.js) copies it into `dist/` content-hashed and injects it into every built page except the third-party embed entries (671 pages, `/viewer` and `/ar` included, which a `transformIndexHtml` could not reach because they are copied verbatim from `public/`). `GET /.well-known/apple-app-site-association` is served from the API rather than a static file because Apple demands an exact path, a JSON content type and no redirect; it answers `503 not_configured` until `APPLE_TEAM_ID` is set rather than publishing an association that would fail silently on device. The app also carries the **CarPlay** scene for three.ws Drive: `CarPlaySceneDelegate` renders the car screen's templates while `DriveLink` bridges them to `/drive` in the WebView, gated behind the `com.apple.developer.carplay-voice-based-conversation` entitlement, which is inert until Apple grants it. Docs: [ios/README.md](ios/README.md), [docs/ios-app.md](docs/ios-app.md), App Review posture [ios/docs/REVIEW-RISK.md](ios/docs/REVIEW-RISK.md), submission [ios/docs/SUBMISSION.md](ios/docs/SUBMISSION.md), CarPlay [ios/docs/CARPLAY.md](ios/docs/CARPLAY.md). The project also carries the **Agent glance** WidgetKit extension (`ws.three.app.glance`, iOS 17+), whose sources live in [apple/](apple/README.md) and are shared byte for byte with the Mac app. |
| three.ws Glance for Mac | [apple/](apple/README.md): shared sources [apple/GlanceKit/](apple/GlanceKit), WidgetKit extension [apple/GlanceWidget/](apple/GlanceWidget), menu bar host app [apple/macos/](apple/macos) (XcodeGen spec [apple/macos/project.yml](apple/macos/project.yml)), icon [apple/scripts/make-macos-icon.mjs](apple/scripts/make-macos-icon.mjs) | In-repo · builds with XcodeGen and a Development Team · Developer ID distribution, so it needs no App Store review | The agent in a Mac's Notification Centre, and the same extension on iPhone. A menu bar app whose whole job is to hold a revocable widget token and prove it works: the Mac never sees a password, because signing in happens on three.ws and the page hands back the token through `threews://glance/link` or as a code to paste. The widget fetches `GET /api/glance/mine?format=png` on the system timeline, in both themes so it follows the reader's appearance, caches the last card in the shared App Group container so an offline Mac shows yesterday's card with the time on it, and opens the agent on click. One set of sources builds for both platforms; only the project differs, because an App Group identifier on a Developer ID Mac must carry the team prefix that the same group on iPhone must not. Guard: [scripts/check-apple-widget.mjs](scripts/check-apple-widget.mjs) (`npm run check:apple-widget`, in `npm run gate`) proves both Xcode projects and the client-to-server contract on a machine with no Xcode. Docs: [apple/README.md](apple/README.md), [docs/native-widgets.md](docs/native-widgets.md). |
| Walk Avatar browser extension | [extensions/walk-avatar/](extensions/walk-avatar) — MV3 `manifest.json`, `background.js` | In-repo · Chrome Web Store release checklist staged ([RELEASE.md](extensions/walk-avatar/RELEASE.md)) | Walks your three.ws avatar across any site and reads pages aloud — a browsing companion built on the same rig as the [walk-sdk](walk-sdk). Ships its own store-required [PRIVACY.md](extensions/walk-avatar/PRIVACY.md), [PERMISSIONS.md](extensions/walk-avatar/PERMISSIONS.md), and [TERMS.md](extensions/walk-avatar/TERMS.md). |
| Checkout companion browser extension | [extensions/checkout-companion/](extensions/checkout-companion) (MV3: [extract.js](extensions/checkout-companion/extract.js), [content.js](extensions/checkout-companion/content.js), [panel.js](extensions/checkout-companion/panel.js), [background.js](extensions/checkout-companion/background.js)), analysis [api/_lib/companion/checkout.js](api/_lib/companion/checkout.js), endpoint [api/companion/checkout.js](api/companion/checkout.js) | In-repo | The companion at the moment money moves: on a checkout page it reports the total against the price you were shown, money the page did not itemise, fees named and summed, whether the charge repeats, and the cancellation terms. **Arithmetic is never the model's job** (integer minor units, computed server-side) and **no finding is ever a legal verdict** (`sanitizeModelFindings()` drops any that claims an amount or calls something illegal). With no model reachable the arithmetic findings still stand. The extension **never reads an input**: `collectText`/`collectAmounts` refuse form fields, iframes and editable regions structurally, and redaction runs in the browser before anything is sent; only the hostname travels and nothing about the page is stored. Docs: [docs/checkout-companion.md](docs/checkout-companion.md). Tests: [tests/companion-checkout.test.js](tests/companion-checkout.test.js), [tests/checkout-extract.test.js](tests/checkout-extract.test.js) |

Subscribes to `/api/companion/stream` with the same bridge token the CLI stores, and drives the published [walk embed](pages/walk-embed.html) over its postMessage contract, so it carries no 3D code of its own. Not an npm workspace: it installs and builds on its own (see [apps/desktop/README.md](apps/desktop/README.md)). |

| Base58 difficulty model (the honest math) | [src/solana/vanity/base58-distribution.js](src/solana/vanity/base58-distribution.js), dispatch in [src/solana/vanity/validation.js](src/solana/vanity/validation.js); ported copies in [solana-agent-sdk/src/vanity/verify.ts](solana-agent-sdk/src/vanity/verify.ts) and [mcp-server/src/lib/base58-distribution.js](mcp-server/src/lib/base58-distribution.js), held in agreement by [tests/vanity-model-parity.test.js](tests/vanity-model-parity.test.js) | In-repo | The exact probability that a random Solana address matches a vanity pattern, and the single source of truth behind every quote, price, rarity tier, bounty payout and signed receipt. Base58 is a positional encoding of a 256-bit integer, so the *leading* character is not uniform: the 40 symbols `K`-`z` are 17.2x harder to lead with than `5`-`H`, a 58x spread the old flat 58ⁿ model erased. Models are versioned (`base58-exact/v2`, superseding `58^effectiveLength`) and receipts name theirs, so attestations issued before the correction still verify. Spec: [docs/PROTOCOL-vanity.md](docs/PROTOCOL-vanity.md#41-difficulty-model-versioning) |

Compiled to WebAssembly so grinding runs on the visitor's machine, never the server. Surfaced as the `vanity_grinder` MCP tool and the premium pre-ground inventory (`vanity_premium`). |

| Meta-Allocator (the ETF of degens) | [pages/meta-allocator.html](pages/meta-allocator.html) + [src/meta-allocator.js](src/meta-allocator.js) `→ /meta-allocator`, brain [api/_lib/meta-allocator.js](api/_lib/meta-allocator.js), endpoint [api/meta-allocator.js](api/meta-allocator.js) | Live (non-custodial planning) | Spreads a budget across the top verified pump.fun leader agents, ranked by risk-adjusted on-chain track record (win rate, realized ROI, peak-to-trough max drawdown from `agent_sniper_positions`) and diversified across derived correlation groups, then returns a rebalance rule. Free-first `llmComplete` shaping with a deterministic same-shape fallback so it never fails. Returns a plan (weights + suggested sizes), never a transaction; users act one leader at a time via [/vaults](pages/vaults.html). Doc: [docs/meta-allocator.md](docs/meta-allocator.md). |
| Alpha-drip (a leader prices their signal latency) | Ladder rules [api/\_lib/alpha-drip.js](api/_lib/alpha-drip.js), fanout half [api/\_lib/copy-drip-runtime.js](api/_lib/copy-drip-runtime.js), measured edge half-life [api/\_lib/alpha-drip-stats.js](api/_lib/alpha-drip-stats.js), endpoint [api/copy/alpha-drip.js](api/copy/alpha-drip.js) `-> /api/copy/alpha-drip`, leader editor [src/alpha-drip-panel.js](src/alpha-drip-panel.js) mounted on [/dashboard/copy](src/dashboard-next/pages/copy.js), copier seat on the trader page [src/copy-panel.js](src/copy-panel.js), schema [api/\_lib/migrations/20260903235500\_copy\_alpha\_drip.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260903235500_copy_alpha_drip.sql) | Live (off unless a leader turns it on) | The $THREE sink on the copy rail: a leader sets how long each holder tier waits before a copy intent is revealed to them, so the thing being sold is latency rather than only a share of the profit. Two rules are structural and enforced server-side: a higher tier can never wait longer than a lower one, and **a drip delays the reveal, never the record** (the intent row is written in full at fanout time and the public track record is untouched, so there are no hidden trades). The copier is shown the seat their balance buys BEFORE they subscribe, not after; a locked intent renders as a countdown that unlocks in place, and `POST /api/copy/executions` refuses an unreleased one in the same statement that changes its status, so the guard cannot be raced. Tier size caps are applied after the copy engine has sized and gated the order, never before, so the copier's own caps still bind first. The edge half-life the fairness warning judges a ladder against is measured from the leader's own profitable closes, and is null rather than guessed below five of them. Release runs inside [api/cron/copy-fanout.js](api/cron/copy-fanout.js) rather than a second cron, so the release cadence is the fanout cadence. Doc: [docs/alpha-drip.md](docs/alpha-drip.md). Tests: [tests/alpha-drip.test.js](tests/alpha-drip.test.js) |
| Ghost-copy (paper-copy a leader) | [pages/ghost-copy.html](pages/ghost-copy.html) + [src/ghost-copy.js](src/ghost-copy.js) `→ /ghost-copy`, simulator [api/_lib/ghost-copy.js](api/_lib/ghost-copy.js), endpoint [api/pump/ghost-copy.js](api/pump/ghost-copy.js), tests [tests/ghost-copy.test.js](tests/ghost-copy.test.js) | Live (paper only, zero custody) | The bridge between the leaderboard and actually copying someone. Pick a verified trader agent, a budget and a window, and it replays that agent's real closed on-chain round-trips (`agent_sniper_positions`) against a wallet that does not exist, sizing every order through `planCopyOrder`, the SAME function the live [copy-fanout cron](api/cron/copy-fanout.js) uses, so the result is what the copy engine would actually have generated rather than a marketing figure. Deliberately unflattering: P&L per trade is derived from exact chain lamports (never the stored pct column), losing round-trips all count, the ghost wallet has a real cash constraint so capital locked in an open copy blocks the next one, every skip carries a machine reason plus a human sentence, and the leader's still-open positions are marked at THEIR last on-chain quote and reported as unrealized outside the realized headline. Needs no account, no wallet, no signature and no funded signer, so it ships ahead of any custody decision; state lives in the query string so a result is deep-linkable and shareable. Converts into [/vaults](pages/vaults.html) for real money. Doc: [docs/ghost-copy.md](docs/ghost-copy.md). |
| Trader Wrapped (season recap) | [pages/wrapped.html](pages/wrapped.html) + [src/wrapped.js](src/wrapped.js) `→ /wrapped`, deck builder [api/_lib/wrapped.js](api/_lib/wrapped.js), endpoint [api/pump/wrapped.js](api/pump/wrapped.js), social card [api/wrapped-og.js](api/wrapped-og.js), share page [api/wrapped-share.js](api/wrapped-share.js) `→ /wrapped/<agent_id>/share`, tests [tests/wrapped.test.js](tests/wrapped.test.js) | Live (read-only, zero custody) | The artifact the trading stack was missing: one image and one link that say what a season was. Eight slides cut server-side from a trader agent's closed on-chain round-trips (scoreboard, best trade, worst trade, the coin that carried it, rhythm, rank against the field with the nearest rival, receipt), swipeable on touch and keyboard-driven on desktop, with the current slide in the URL hash so a share link lands on the slide the sharer was reading. Arithmetic is delegated wholesale to `computeTraderMetrics` in [api/_lib/trader-stats.js](api/_lib/trader-stats.js), the same truth layer the leaderboard and trader profile use, so a recap can never disagree with the profile it links to. Deliberately unflattering: the worst-trade slide is not optional, the scoreboard carries an explicit verdict and colors a red season red, per-coin ranking includes coins that lost money, self-launched coins stay excluded from every superlative, the rank always prints the size of the real field beside it, and under 3 settled round-trips the page says there is no season rather than drawing a chart out of noise. Needs no account, wallet, signature or funded signer. Converts through [Fork](src/fork-trade.js) on the best-trade slide and links onward to the verified profile, [Ghost-copy](pages/ghost-copy.html) and the rival's own wrapped. Doc: [docs/trader-wrapped.md](docs/trader-wrapped.md). |
| Clip Director (content engine) | [pages/clip-director.html](pages/clip-director.html) + [src/clip-director-page.js](src/clip-director-page.js) `→ /clip-director`, brain [api/_lib/clip-director.js](api/_lib/clip-director.js), endpoint [api/clip-director.js](api/clip-director.js) | Live | Turns one real closed trade (`agent_sniper_positions`) into the optimal shareable card per surface (X / Telegram / Feed): honest hook, the real number, an avatar gesture mapped to a real animation clip, CTA, and alt text, plus an on-chain proof link. Losses get an honest card too. Free-first `llmComplete` with a deterministic same-shape fallback. Doc: [docs/clip-director.md](docs/clip-director.md). |
| Atlas (site-wide Cmd+K search + route map) | Palette [public/atlas.js](public/atlas.js) `-> /atlas.js`, ranking [public/atlas/score.js](public/atlas/score.js), map page [pages/atlas.html](pages/atlas.html) + [src/atlas-page.js](src/atlas-page.js) `-> /atlas`, index [scripts/build-atlas-index.mjs](scripts/build-atlas-index.mjs) `-> public/atlas-index.json`, task catalog [data/atlas-intents.json](data/atlas-intents.json), coverage [scripts/inject-atlas.mjs](scripts/inject-atlas.mjs) | Live | **Discovery layer for 650+ routes.** Cmd+K (or `/`) opens a search over every page in `data/pages.json` plus a curated set of TASKS that answer with ordered steps rather than one link, because "which page starts the get-paid flow" is a harder question than "where is /x402/studio". Zero-dependency single file so it runs identically on 250+ standalone HTML entry points; the index is fetched on first open, not page load. Every task step target is resolved against the real route table at build time, so an onboarding path cannot dead-end on a 404. Injected by a Vite `three-ws-atlas` plugin plus a post-build `dist/` walk (same belt-and-suspenders as [scripts/inject-tour-boot.mjs](scripts/inject-tour-boot.mjs), for the same plugin-ordering reason). Embeds and the nav/footer fragments are excluded. Doc: [docs/atlas.md](docs/atlas.md). Tests: [tests/atlas.test.js](tests/atlas.test.js). |
| Internationalization (i18n) | Runtime [src/i18n.js](src/i18n.js) `→ /i18n.js`; catalogs [public/locales/](public/locales) (`en` + 84 locales, `manifest.json`, `localized-pages.json`); pipeline [scripts/i18n-annotate.mjs](scripts/i18n-annotate.mjs) · [i18n-extract.mjs](scripts/i18n-extract.mjs) · [i18n-translate.mjs](scripts/i18n-translate.mjs) (Vertex Gemini, glossary-locked) · [i18n-backfill.mjs](scripts/i18n-backfill.mjs) (re-translates copy that was baked to English during a backend outage); hreflang in [api/sitemap/[type].js](api/sitemap/%5Btype%5D.js) | Live (85 languages) | LobeHub-model client-side copy swap. `npm run i18n:annotate` auto-injects `data-i18n*` into static HTML (byte-surgical), `i18n:extract` builds `en.json`, `i18n:translate` fans it out to every locale via Vertex with brand/protocol terms masked. `i18n:backfill` finds keys whose value is still byte-identical to English (the silent failure mode: a baked fallback is present, so every later run skips it) and re-translates them. Runtime auto-detects locale, mounts a floating `<lang-switcher>`, and injects hreflang. Doc: [docs/i18n.md](docs/i18n.md). Tests: [tests/i18n.test.js](tests/i18n.test.js), [tests/i18n-annotate.test.js](tests/i18n-annotate.test.js), [tests/i18n-backfill.test.js](tests/i18n-backfill.test.js). |
| Shared utilities (import instead of hand-rolling) | Frontend [src/shared/markdown.js](src/shared/markdown.js) · [src/shared/toast.js](src/shared/toast.js) · [src/shared/fuzzy.js](src/shared/fuzzy.js) · `timeAgo`/`toEpochMs` in [src/shared/coin-format.js](src/shared/coin-format.js); backend [api/_lib/resilience.js](api/_lib/resilience.js) · [api/_lib/mem-cache.js](api/_lib/mem-cache.js) · [api/_lib/pool.js](api/_lib/pool.js) · [api/_lib/csv.js](api/_lib/csv.js); ops [scripts/check-cron-drift.mjs](scripts/check-cron-drift.mjs) | Live · doc [docs/shared-utilities.md](docs/shared-utilities.md) | Thin layers over vetted packages, each replacing a family of hand-rolled copies that had drifted. `markdown.js` = `marked` + `DOMPurify`, the single sanitized path for model/user text reaching `innerHTML` (the concierge SDK carries its own copy at [concierge-sdk/src/markdown.js](concierge-sdk/src/markdown.js) because it ships as an independent bundle). `toast.js` = one aria-live region with a real queue. `fuzzy.js` = `@leeoniya/ufuzzy` ranking + typo tolerance + match ranges for both command palettes. `resilience.js` = `cockatiel` circuit breakers **and** jittered retry (`withRetry`, `parseRetryAfter`, `isRetryableError`). `mem-cache.js` = `lru-cache` with true recency eviction and single-flight `cached()`. `pool.js` = `p-limit` bounded concurrency. `csv.js` = RFC 4180 quoting **plus** spreadsheet formula-injection neutralization. `check-cron-drift.mjs` = `cron-parser` validation of every cron declared in vercel.json against live Cloud Scheduler, sharing its job-id derivation with [scripts/create-gcp-scheduler.mjs](scripts/create-gcp-scheduler.mjs) so the two can never disagree. Tests: [tests/shared-markdown.test.js](tests/shared-markdown.test.js), [tests/fuzzy.test.js](tests/fuzzy.test.js), [tests/csv.test.js](tests/csv.test.js), [tests/resilience-shared.test.js](tests/resilience-shared.test.js) |
| X post archive + engagement analysis (internal) | Snapshots [data/x-archive/](data/x-archive), schema [api/_lib/migrations/20260814120000_x_account_archive.sql](api/_lib/migrations/20260814120000_x_account_archive.sql), normalization + analysis [scripts/x-archive-lib.mjs](scripts/x-archive-lib.mjs), commands [scripts/x-archive-import.mjs](scripts/x-archive-import.mjs) · [scripts/x-archive-refresh.mjs](scripts/x-archive-refresh.mjs) · [scripts/x-archive-analyze.mjs](scripts/x-archive-analyze.mjs), report [docs/x-archive/](docs/x-archive) | Internal · doc [docs/x-archive.md](docs/x-archive.md) | Every post our own X accounts published, kept as raw snapshot files and indexed into `x_account_posts` / `x_account_post_snapshots` so engagement is a measured time series rather than a memory. `npm run x:archive:analyze` regenerates a report ranking posts by engagement and by engagement *rate*, and scores every measurable signal (media, mentions, length, subject, hour) as lift against the corpus median. Two scraper defects are handled rather than inherited: a profile scrape returns reposts of other accounts with `isRetweet: false` (authorship is read from the permalink instead), and X renders like counts lazily so real posts arrive at "0 likes" (those are archived, named, and excluded from the medians until `npm run x:archive:refresh` replaces them with exact X API counters). Distinct from `x_posts`, which is what platform users publish through [api/x/post.js](api/x/post.js). Tests: [tests/x-archive.test.js](tests/x-archive.test.js) |
| Interactive 3D history timeline | [pages/timeline.html](pages/timeline.html) `→ /timeline`, [src/timeline.js](src/timeline.js), data [data/timeline.json](data/timeline.json) | Live | three.ws's public history (launches, partnerships, listings, press, product ships) as an explorable 3D scene: 60+ milestones as glowing markers along a curved path, 360° free-orbit or a marker-to-marker Walk mode with a guide beacon (reuses the shared `AnimationManager` + default rig from [src/walk.js](src/walk.js)'s pattern), a synced bottom scrubber, category filters, and a detail panel linking to the original source where one exists (IBM Community, NVIDIA Developer Forums, Yahoo Finance, Phemex, CoinGecko, HackerNoon). Real accessible HTML-list fallback for no-WebGL or fetch-failure. |
| Upstream resilience toolkit | [scripts/resilience/](scripts/resilience) + [scripts/audit-upstreams.mjs](scripts/audit-upstreams.mjs) `→ npm run audit:upstreams`, baseline [data/upstream-baseline.json](data/upstream-baseline.json), generated map [docs/resilience.md](docs/resilience.md) | Live | Answers the question a fallback alone cannot: when a third-party service is down, what is each endpoint ALLOWED to say? An AST pass grades every outbound call site on five protections (deadline, retry, failover, last-good, breaker) and ratchets against a committed baseline, so a new unbounded call fails the build while the existing tail is paid down at its own pace. On top of it, endpoints declare a degradation contract (`may-serve-stale`, `must-refuse`, `must-not-serve-stale`) that [tests/upstream-degradation.test.js](tests/upstream-degradation.test.js) proves by replacing fetch with a dead upstream and judging the real response. Built after four fallbacks that each passed review turned out to serve remembered data on paths where staleness costs money or grants access. Complements [scripts/check-fetch-timeouts.mjs](scripts/check-fetch-timeouts.mjs), which enforces the deadline floor. Docs: [scripts/resilience/README.md](scripts/resilience/README.md) |

## Workers and services not mapped above

One-liners for every `workers/<name>/` and `services/<name>/` directory that no
surface-map row already covers:

| Worker / service | What it does |
|---|---|
| [workers/oracle/](workers/oracle) | Score / agent / settle loops powering `/oracle`. Runs in production inside the API container via the every-2-minutes [api/cron/oracle-score.js](api/cron/oracle-score.js); the same modules also run as a long-lived process (`npm run worker:oracle`) |
| [workers/agent-anchor/](workers/agent-anchor) | Newsroom Anchor worker: turns market intel into anchor-read segments |
| [workers/agent-forge/](workers/agent-forge) | Headless Live Avatar Forge caster: forges on a free lane and broadcasts the GLB onto an agent's live screen. Operator-run CLI, not a deployed service |
| [workers/agent-mm/](workers/agent-mm) | Launch Copilot market-maker engine (rules-based) |
| [workers/agent-orders/](workers/agent-orders) | Programmable Orders Engine: limit / stop / trailing / DCA / TWAP / conditional orders swept against live quotes and fired through the audited agent-trade path. Built and tested, not deployed as a Cloud Run service yet, so orders stay `active` until it runs |
| [workers/agent-screen-worker/](workers/agent-screen-worker) | Stagehand-driven real browser worker behind the agent screen cast |
| [workers/avatar-pipeline-controller/](workers/avatar-pipeline-controller) | FastAPI CPU orchestrator behind Scan-yourself-to-3D (multi-backend lane; not deployed today, `GCP_RECONSTRUCTION_URL` points at `avatar-reconstruction`) |
| [workers/deploy/](workers/deploy) | Cloud Run deploy runbook for the selfie-to-3D pipeline |
| [workers/longcat/](workers/longcat) | LongCat-Video-Avatar-1.5 talking-avatar worker. Built and tested, not deployed: the resident weight set is ~45 GB, so it needs one 80 GB GPU (A100-80GB/H100-80GB on Compute Engine) rather than a Cloud Run L4. Until `LONGCAT_WORKER_URL` is set, `/api/avatar/video-generate` answers its designed `503 worker_unconfigured` |
| [workers/model-video2scene/](workers/model-video2scene) | Streaming video to 3D point-cloud reconstruction on Cloud Run GPU (LingBot-Map). Built and weights staged, not deployed as its own service yet, so `/capture` runs its designed unconfigured state until `GCP_VIDEO2SCENE_URL` is set |
| [workers/texture/](workers/texture) | Text-guided texturing + magic-brush region retexture GPU server |
| [workers/pump-fun-mcp/](workers/pump-fun-mcp) | Cloudflare Workers mirror of the canonical pump.fun MCP server |
| [services/agent-screen-caster/](services/agent-screen-caster) | Self-hosted Playwright caster: an agent owner runs it on their own host with their agent id + key, and it streams the browser it drives into the agent-screen feed. No managed instance exists (the platform's own casting runs in `workers/agent-screen-pool` and `workers/agent-screen-worker`). Tests: [tests/agent-screen-caster.test.js](tests/agent-screen-caster.test.js) |
| [services/fleet-console/](services/fleet-console) | Health console for an open-source fleet: enumerates a GitHub owner's repositories, probes every deployment URL and npm package they advertise, and scores what each one actually delivers. Ships an HTTP dashboard, a CLI (`scan --attention` exits 1 when anything is broken, so cron can gate on it) and an MCP server over the same snapshot. Not deployed and not meant to be: it runs from a terminal, a cron job, or beside an agent. Tests: [tests/fleet-console.test.js](tests/fleet-console.test.js), [tests/fleet-console-server.test.js](tests/fleet-console-server.test.js) |
| [services/pump-graduations/](services/pump-graduations) | Standalone watcher for pump.fun bonding-curve graduations |
| [services/home-relay/](services/home-relay) | Terminates the outbound WebSocket a LAN-only Home Assistant dials, and multiplexes allowlisted platform sessions onto it. Holds no database and no credential at rest: a house proves ownership with an HMAC install token, and the Home Assistant authentication happens inside the house and never crosses this process. Not a proxy, on purpose: it carries a fixed list of Home Assistant message types and refuses everything else, including every service domain that would administer the machine rather than a device. Deployed unthrottled with min-instances 1 because it holds sockets between requests. Docs: [docs/home-relay.md](docs/home-relay.md), [docs/home-relay-threat-model.md](docs/home-relay-threat-model.md). Tests: [tests/home-relay-protocol.test.js](tests/home-relay-protocol.test.js), [tests/home-relay-transport.test.js](tests/home-relay-transport.test.js) |
| [home-assistant-integration/](home-assistant-integration) | The Python that runs inside a user's house: a Home Assistant custom integration, distributed through HACS as its own public repository, that dials out to `services/home-relay`. A custom integration rather than a Home Assistant add-on because add-ons only work on OS and Supervised installs while an integration works on all four, and because HACS distributes integrations rather than add-ons. Mints its own system refresh token locally, so no Home Assistant credential ever reaches three.ws, and enforces the relay's allowlist a second time from a generated `allowlist.json` shipped byte-identical from `services/home-relay`. Publishing the public repository is owner-gated. |


`packages/*` is the home for clean, publishable spec/schema/preset packages
with no runtime dependency on the main app.

Cross-chain SDKs that ship on their own (`sdk/` → `@three-ws/sdk`,
`solana-agent-sdk/` → `@three-ws/solana-agent`,
`agent-protocol-sdk/` → `@three-ws/agent-protocol-sdk`) live at the top level
but are **not** npm workspaces.

`packages/` is reserved for spec/schema/protocol packages with no runtime
dependencies on the main app — things that need to be installable by external
consumers (e.g. another team building an avatar viewer that wants to validate
our manifest format).

Runtime SDKs and apps live at the top level for historical compatibility with
the deploy pipeline and existing import paths.

## SDK packages (published)

These wrap already-live platform capabilities (real `api/` endpoints + MCP
tools) into single-import `@three-ws/*` SDKs. Each is a **zero-dependency,
pure-ESM** client: it ships `src/` directly (no build step), hand-written
`.d.ts` types, and a `node --test` suite — all green (216 tests across the
suite). They share one byte-identical HTTP core (`src/http.js`: base-URL
resolution + typed `ThreeWsError`/`PaymentRequiredError`, with 402 carrying the
x402 challenge). Verify any of them with `cd packages/<name> && node --test
test/*.test.js`.

Launch state: most are **published to npm** as `@three-ws/<name>`. Install with
`npm i @three-ws/<name>`. A few rows are packages finished in tree and queued for
their first publish; `node scripts/publish-packages.mjs --dry-run` prints which is
which, live, rather than a claim here going stale. The merchant SDK
(`@three-ws/x402-server`) advertises the two main x402 assets, **USDC and
$THREE**, in one 402 challenge. Publishing is idempotent and the package list is
DISCOVERED from the filesystem (every non-private `packages/*` without a
`server.json`), so a new package ships by existing, not by being added to an
array. Runbook: [docs/contributing.md](docs/contributing.md#publishing-packages--standalone-mirrors).

Live Home Assistant entity state and service calls over the first-party WebSocket API, the user's own curated tools over the `mcp_server` MCP endpoint, a floor/area/entity room graph with per-room lighting, climate and security rollups for the 3D scene, household macros resolved to the scenes the user already built, and a physical-action gate that never lets an unlock through without an explicit yes. Zero device code: Home Assistant owns Zigbee, Z-Wave, Matter, Thread and the other 1,500 integrations. Docs: [docs/smart-home.md](docs/smart-home.md) |
| `@three-ws/irl` | [packages/irl/](packages/irl) | `api/irl/`, `_lib/geohash.js` | Geofenced real-world presence + nearby discovery |
| `@three-ws/sign-language` | [packages/sign-language/](packages/sign-language) | `/sign-language`, `/asl-alphabet`, signed chat replies | The ASL engine as a published bundle: text → one signed clip, zero runtime dependencies |
| `@three-ws/pose` | [packages/pose/](packages/pose) | `get_pose_seed`, `api/mcp-3d.js` | Pose-seed generation for rigged avatars |
| `@three-ws/motion` | [packages/motion/](packages/motion) | [api/motion.js](api/motion.js), [api/_lib/motion-author.js](api/_lib/motion-author.js), [pages/motion.html](pages/motion.html) `→ /motion` | Text and structure to a full-body three.js `AnimationClip` with no mocap data, no model weights and no renderer: a Motion Score (a compact, human-editable description of what the body does) is solved into keyframes by forward and inverse kinematics with balance, ground contact and secondary motion. Zero dependencies, so the same solver runs in the browser page, the API handler and the CLI. Tests: [packages/motion/test/](packages/motion/test) |
| `@three-ws/render` | [packages/render/](packages/render) | [api/_lib/render-cpu.js](api/_lib/render-cpu.js) | The renderer behind every server-side avatar picture on three.ws: a pure-JavaScript software rasterizer that turns a rigged, animated GLB into PNG, animated PNG or truecolor terminal output with no GPU, no WebGL and no headless browser. It replaced a headless-chromium pipeline that cost a 300 MB browser launch and 3 to 15 seconds per cold render, which is what made social-card and batch rendering fit inside a serverless budget at all. Sibling of [packages/tty-3d/](packages/tty-3d), which owns the terminal-only path. Tests: [packages/render/tests/](packages/render/tests) |
| `@three-ws/avatar-stream` | [packages/avatar-stream/](packages/avatar-stream) | [api/avatar-stream.js](api/avatar-stream.js), [src/stream-demo.js](src/stream-demo.js), [pages/stream.html](pages/stream.html) `→ /stream` | Progressive 3D over plain HTTP. Packs a GLB into a layered A3S stream whose first 50 KB is already a complete, skinned, spec-valid avatar, then refines it in place with byte-range requests, so a viewer on a slow connection gets something posable immediately instead of a spinner until the last byte. No custom server, no WebSocket, no new codec: range requests are the whole transport. Tests: [packages/avatar-stream/test/](packages/avatar-stream/test) |
| `@three-ws/brownout` | [packages/brownout/](packages/brownout) | [docs/brownout.md](docs/brownout.md) | Data-provenance headers plus request-scoped fault injection: a response says which upstream its data came from and how fresh it is, and a caller can break those upstreams on purpose to prove the integration degrades the way its contract claims. The companion to the resilience audit above, which grades call sites statically; this proves the behaviour at runtime. Tests: [packages/brownout/test/](packages/brownout/test) |

## Where things actually live

- **3D viewer & creator** — [avatar-sdk/](avatar-sdk), [character-studio/](character-studio)
- **Avatar / accessory assets** — [public/avatars/](public/avatars), [public/accessories/](public/accessories), CC0 parametric-body source data [avatar-sources/anny/](avatar-sources/anny)
- **Animations** — [public/animations/](public/animations) (Mixamo FBX → built GLB clips), [scripts/build-animations.mjs](scripts/build-animations.mjs)
- **Animation source clips**: [animation-sources/](animation-sources) (61 source Mixamo `.fbx` clips built to GLB by [scripts/build-animations.mjs](scripts/build-animations.mjs))
- **On-chain identity & contracts** — [contracts/](contracts), [packages/avatar-schema/](packages/avatar-schema)
- **API endpoints** — [api/](api) (serverless-style handlers, served in production by the [server/](server) Cloud Run container), [workers/](workers) (long-running workers)
- **Examples & demos** — [examples/](examples), [multiplayer/](multiplayer)
- **Announcement pipeline**: [docs/announcements/](docs/announcements/README.md) (one pack per feature: the claim and its evidence, the postable text, the media by shot id, the Telegram variant), [data/announcements.json](data/announcements.json) (the ledger: every shipped surface, whether it was ever announced, its score and its stage), [data/announce-media.json](data/announce-media.json) (media recipes captured from the live product into `public/announce/`), [docs/announce-voice.md](docs/announce-voice.md) (the voice contract, derived from the engagement archive). `npm run announce:rank` ranks the backlog, `npm run announce:media` captures the frames, `npm run check:announce` gates a pack. Nothing here posts: publishing stays owner-gated.
- **Marketing assets**: [marketing/](marketing/README.md) (produced promo videos + re-render scripts, e.g. [marketing/imessage-video/](marketing/imessage-video); source-of-truth assets, not shipped to production). The narrated product demo lives in [marketing/product-demo/](marketing/product-demo/README.md): [scripts/make-product-demo.mjs](scripts/make-product-demo.mjs) (`npm run demo:video`) films a presenter walking every feature page on the live site, chaptered, with narration from the platform's own TTS lane. Its route is the Feature Tour curriculum, so a new page is in the next recording without editing a script. Docs: [docs/product-demo.md](docs/product-demo.md)
- **Platform agents (server-side)**: [agents/](agents/README.md) (endpoint-shopper, fact-checker, tutor, unstoppable, x402-buildout: library modules imported by `api/` handlers, each with its own README)
- **Site data files**: [data/](data/README.md) (pages.json, changelog.json, skills seed, RSS items: what each file drives and which script regenerates it)
- **Repository guards**: [data/guards.json](data/guards.json) is the registry of every mechanical check and the stage it runs in (prebuild, gate, deploy build, pre-push, on demand). [scripts/audit-guards.mjs](scripts/audit-guards.mjs) proves each entry is real and each stage claim is true, [scripts/build-guards.mjs](scripts/build-guards.mjs) publishes it to `public/guards.json`, [pages/guards.html](pages/guards.html) renders it at `/guards`, and [docs/guards.md](docs/guards.md) plus [docs/tutorials/write-a-guard.md](docs/tutorials/write-a-guard.md) document it. There is no CI here, so this is the enforcement layer.
- **Blog**: [blog/](blog/README.md) (hand-authored post HTML plus dataset and syndication companions; SEO injection and routing documented there)
- **Agent work orders**: [prompts/](prompts/README.md); everything still open sits in one folder, [prompts/finish/](prompts/finish/); machine-written x402 sweep reports: [tasks/](tasks/README.md)
- **Rust crates (WASM)**: [crates/](crates/README.md) (vanity-grinder, built via `npm run build:wasm`)
- **Browser extensions**: [extensions/](extensions/README.md) (walk-avatar Chrome extension)
- **Standalone deploy targets**: [deploy/](deploy/README.md) (always-on sniper worker; the world.three.ws Hyperfy server)
- **AWS infra**: [infra/](infra) (AWS CDK stack)
- **Page snapshots**: [snapshots/](snapshots) (daily visual record of every page)
- **Maintenance holding page**: [maintenance/](maintenance) (zero-build static holding page)
- **Robinhood Chain (4663) developer tooling** — [robinhood/](robinhood), standalone packages published to their own `nirholas/*` GitHub repos (each with its own README): `erc8056` (ERC-8056 Scaled UI Amount reference impl), `hood-connect` (wallet onboarding kit), `hood-pay` (USDG checkout + payment links), `hood-alerts` (Telegram/Discord/X launch + whale alerts), `hood-status` (chain status page), `hood-tokenlist` (canonical token list), `hoodkit`/`hood402`/`robinhood-chain-sdk` (shared chain + x402 primitives other packages depend on), `hood-cli` (CLI + the `hood init` trading-stack setup wizard), `hood-launcher`, `hood-mcp`, `hood-api`, `hood-js`, `hood-traders` (autonomous trading fleet, four strategies incl. a bring-your-own-key LLM strategist), `robinhood-chain-examples`, `learn-robinhood-chain`. Distinct from the in-repo `/play` and `/markets/robinhood` product surfaces above, which live in this repo's own `api/`/`src/`.
- **Cross-chain SDKs** — [sdk/](sdk), [solana-agent-sdk/](solana-agent-sdk), [agent-payments-sdk/](agent-payments-sdk), [agent-protocol-sdk/](agent-protocol-sdk)
- **MCP integration** — [mcp-server/](mcp-server), [mcp-bridge/](mcp-bridge)
- **Specs & protocol docs** — [specs/](specs)
- **Vendored third-party reference trees**: [third_party/](third_party/README.md) (complete upstream MIT source kept to read, not to build: the Design+Code agent-skill corpus and the ThreeUI Community component library). Read-only, excluded from ESLint, i18n extraction, and the Cloud Build upload. Distinct from the `vendor/` directories inside a surface ([src/scene-studio/vendor/](src/scene-studio/vendor), `public/vendor/`, `pages/ibm/vendor/`), which are third-party code we actually ship.
- **Frontend pages** — [src/](src), [pages/](pages), [public/](public)
- **Tests** — [tests/](tests)

## Promotion path

A surface graduates to its own repo only when there is a concrete external
need that the monorepo cannot serve:

1. **A third party wants to consume it without pulling our whole tree.**
   Mitigation: publish to npm under `@three-ws/*` first. If the npm package
   is enough, no repo split is needed.
2. **Independent release cadence required.** When the surface needs its own
   semver line decoupled from the app.
3. **External contributors blocked.** When the repo size or unrelated CI cost
   is a real friction for outside PRs.

Promotion procedure (when triggered):

```bash
# Cleanly split a directory's full history into a new repo
git subtree split --prefix=packages/avatar-schema -b avatar-schema-split
cd ../  && mkdir avatar-schema-repo && cd avatar-schema-repo
git init && git pull ../three.ws avatar-schema-split
git remote add origin https://github.com/nirholas/avatar-schema.git
git push -u origin main
# Then in three.ws: replace the workspace dir with a normal npm dep
```

Until the trigger fires, splitting is premature: each new repo adds release
overhead, CI cost, and cross-repo PR coordination tax for zero functional
gain.
